# Build State

(Last updated: 2026-05-11 — Brief 111 completed (JotForm viewer — per-form column packs + EST timestamps + Status column dropped + Location dropdown defensive fallback). apps/web-only polish on top of Brief 109's flat-list viewer + Brief 110's filter/grouping layer for `/admin/jotform/[form_id]`. Two new modules: `apps/web/app/admin/jotform/[form_id]/_lib/form-columns.tsx` is the per-form column registry — each `form_id` maps to an ordered `FormColumn[]` whose `render` accessor returns ReactNode; a `DEFAULT_COLUMNS` fallback (`Submitted | Site`) keeps unregistered forms working. Adding a 5th / 6th JotForm form via SQL INSERT + backfill needs zero apps/web code unless non-generic columns are wanted. `apps/web/app/admin/jotform/_lib/format-est.ts` formats `jotform_created_at` via `Intl.DateTimeFormat` + IANA zone `America/New_York` — auto-handles EDT (summer) / EST (winter) per the IANA DB. `submittedColumn()` in the registry renders `<span title={absolute}>{relative}</span>` matching the rest of apps/web's relative-with-absolute-on-hover pattern; header label is "Submitted (EST)" so operators don't have to mentally convert. `apps/web/app/admin/jotform/[form_id]/page.tsx` table rewrite: hard-coded `<thead>` + `<tbody>` columns replaced with `columnsFor(form_id).map(col => ...)` dynamic loop in both; trailing `View →` link column appended unconditionally per form. Status column dropped (every onboarded form emits `"ACTIVE"` only — deleted submissions never ingest, so the column carried no signal). Brief 110's location → date grouping retained; group-header location label gained the same address-shape detection the FilterBar uses below (prefer `location_pretty` unless it has a comma OR starts with a digit, in which case use `location_code`). `apps/web/app/admin/jotform/[form_id]/_components/FilterBar.tsx` `Location` dropdown options gained `locationDisplayLabel(loc)` helper — same heuristic as the group header. The roster worker (`apps/jotform-worker/src/handlers/roster.js`) falls back to `locations.location` (postal address) when `pricing_simple.location_pretty` is null; Phase 5.3 explicitly forbade a worker change, so the client-side override is the entire fix. Per-form numeric-answer LABELS in the registry are placeholder pending Supabase sample-data inspection (executor was headless; the brief's "Read first" step specified live-data SELECTs against the operator's Supabase which Claude Code can't run): rewash's "Rewash Reason" key sits in a `REWASH_REASON_KEY = "TBD"` constant; salt-log keys 5/6/8 use generic "Answer (key 5)" etc.; retention keys 28/29/30/31 same shape; time-card-edit is intentionally generic (operator didn't specify per-form columns). Operator / next-executor greps `REWASH_REASON_KEY` + `"Answer (key "` and replaces with the JotForm builder's question text from `answers[KEY].text` of a representative sample row — no code-shape changes needed, only label-string edits. The `prettyFormat → answer → ""` accessor chain keeps each column rendering meaningful regardless of label. Timezone story: option (a) by design — display-only conversion. The worker's `parseJotformDate` (Brief 107) stamps incoming JotForm timestamps with `Z` (UTC); this brief converts UTC → EST at display time. Operator post-deploy verification in brief: submit a test entry, compare JotForm dashboard timestamp to Supabase `jotform_created_at` to the apps/web EST display; if a 5-hour offset shows, follow-up brief to fix the ingest path (display layer would still be correct under either interpretation once we know which applies). (Previously: Brief 110 completed (JotForm viewer — RD / RM / Location filter dropdowns + grouped location → date rendering). Additive UX on top of Brief 109's flat-list viewer for `/admin/jotform/[form_id]`, addressing the 2026-05-11 operator review note that RD / RM operators read submissions by roster, not by clock. Worker side: new `apps/jotform-worker/src/filters.js` is the single resolver for the three filter params (`am_email` / `rm_email` / `location_code`) — each resolves to a `Set<string>` of `site_number` strings in BOTH zero-padded 3-digit AND unpadded forms (per the Brief 107 convention — JotForm's `typeA` widget returns "090" for Milford and "127" for Elmira Heights — both forms must match the downstream PostgREST `in.(...)` filter), and the result is the intersection of all resolved filter sets AND the caller's `accessibleSiteNumbersForSession`. Empty intersection short-circuits to a 200 with `rows: []` (the same pattern an out-of-scope `site_number` already used). `handleListSubmissions` + `handleCsvExport` in `src/handlers/admin.js` now call `resolveLocationFilters(env, session, url.searchParams)` instead of reading scope directly — the existing `siteNumbersFilter` Set plumbing into `db.js`'s `listSubmissions` / `listSubmissionsForCsv` is unchanged. New `src/handlers/roster.js` serves `GET /admin/jotform/api/roster` (any-session gate) returning `{ regional_directors, regional_managers, locations, scope }` derived from one `locations` read + one `pricing_simple` lookup for `location_pretty`, scoped to the caller's accessible site_numbers when not admin-tier. Email + location grouping uses the same most-common-name tiebreak as `listContactRoster` in `@splash/db-supabase`. apps/web side: `apps/web/app/admin/jotform/_lib/worker-fetch.ts` extended with `getRoster()` + four new types (`JotformRoster` / `RosterAm` / `RosterRm` / `RosterLocation`) + three new opts (`amEmail` / `rmEmail` / `locationCode`) on both `listSubmissions` and `csvExportUrl`. Each `<select>` change pushes the URL and clears `?offset=` (narrow filters mean the prior offset might be past the new total). Empty-roster slots (e.g., GM with one site sees no RDs) are hidden entirely. `apps/web/app/admin/jotform/[form_id]/page.tsx` now renders the FilterBar above the DateRangePicker and groups the current 50-row page by location → date with friendly headers ("Binghamton (127) — Regional Manager: Bob Smith" / "May 11, 2026 (3 submissions)"). Single-location pages render flat (no outer group chrome — the date sub-headers carry the structure). Pagination preserves filters in the URL passthrough. No worker schema / R2 / wrangler.toml / secret changes. CLAUDE.md `JotForm submissions` glossary entry gains a Brief 110 one-liner. AM-narrows-RM-and-Location client-side cascade implemented as the UX nicety; defense-in-depth is the worker-side intersect. (Previously: Brief 108 completed (Fleet update webhook — coerce top-level `id` to integer; use `arr[0].id`). Single-line fix in `apps/fleet-inquiry-worker/src/admin.js` `handleUpdateSubmission` (~L443-449): the `fireFleetSubmissionUpdateWebhook` call's top-level `id` field switched from the URL-path string (`id`, parsed by router as `"21"`) to the PostgREST response row's integer (`arr[0].id`). After the operator migrated `fleet_submissions.id` from UUID to bigint identity, PA's HTTP-trigger schema (correctly typed `integer`) started returning 400 at the trigger level (schema validation, before any actions run) on every dashboard PATCH webhook fire. CF Workers Logs showed `[fleet-submission-update] POST failed for 21: status 400`. SharePoint was NOT receiving status/notes updates; dashboard PATCH itself succeeded and Supabase recorded the edit. `row.id` inside the payload was already an integer (PostgREST returns the column as a JSON number); only the top-level `id` was a string. Brief 105 was drafted when the column was UUID — the shape was correct at the time; PA was already updated post-column-switch (operator-verified schema + `triggerBody()?['id']` Filter Query without quotes). The worker was the lone holdout sending a string. Fix sources the integer id from the same row that's about to ship inside `row`, so top-level `id` and `row.id` are guaranteed consistent post-fix. No D1 / R2 / Supabase / wrangler.toml / secret changes. CLAUDE.md "Fleet inquiries admin" glossary entry extended with a Brief 108 paragraph noting the update-webhook `id` field is sourced from the PostgREST response row (`arr[0].id`), not the URL param, plus a latent reminder for future workers: when a Supabase column changes type, audit any worker code that passes URL-path params as that column's value in downstream payloads. Validation: root `pnpm typecheck` 18/18 green; `pnpm --filter @splash/fleet-inquiry-worker exec wrangler deploy --dry-run` 786.26 KiB raw / 149.74 KiB gzipped (≈ unchanged vs Brief 105 baseline). Operator post-deploy smoke (deferred): edit status or notes from `/admin/fleet/[id]`, confirm CF Workers Logs no longer show `[fleet-submission-update] POST failed for X: status 400`, and confirm the PA flow run history is green end-to-end with SharePoint reflecting the change.

(Previously: Brief 107 completed (JotForm submissions — new worker, Supabase storage, backfill, webhook receiver, admin read API + CSV). Ninth worker on the monorepo (`splash-jotform`, `apps/jotform-worker/`, JS not TS — mirrors fleet-inquiry-worker shape per Brief 81 / 105 conventions; the operator confirmed during planning "moving away from JotForm" so investment in a richer TS scaffold isn't warranted). Ingests submissions from four onboarded JotForm Enterprise forms (rewash `250165655616055`, salt-log `243523811897060`, retention `250855287972067`, time-card-edit `250193775451056`) into two new Supabase tables — `jotform_forms` (registry; slug + display_name + enabled) and `jotform_submissions` (one row per submission; common filterable fields `site_number` / `site` / `site_email` / `jotform_created_at` / `jotform_status` promoted to columns, rest in JSONB `answers`). ~50K rows lifetime, ~30K dominated by rewash. Architecture decision: Supabase-as-source-of-truth (not direct JotForm API per query) because JotForm's filter API only supports top-level fields (`created_at`, `id`, `workflowStatus`, `status`) — filtering on the dominant use case (answer fields like site / employee / reason) would require pulling every row and filtering client-side. Sync mechanism: webhook + one-time backfill. `POST /jotform/webhook/{token}/{form_id}` validates a URL-path token (`JOTFORM_WEBHOOK_TOKEN`, constant-time compare; JotForm Enterprise webhook UI does NOT expose a signing secret per operator screenshot — URL secrecy is the entire auth posture), re-fetches the rich submission payload via JotForm API (`fetchSubmissionById`; 15s `AbortSignal.timeout`), normalizes via `normalizeSubmission` (strips `control_head` / `control_pagebreak` / `control_button` / `control_text` form-definition noise — retention's ~20 KB raw payload becomes ~3-5 KB stripped — and extracts the three common fields via JotForm-`name`-keyed walk: `typeA` → site_number, `site` → site, `siteEmail` / `siteEmail56` → site_email with email-typed preferred when both present), and upserts via PostgREST `?on_conflict=id` + `Prefer: resolution=merge-duplicates`. 2xx for accepted / 5xx for retry so JotForm retries network failures; unknown form_id → 200 to halt retries. `POST /admin/jotform/api/{form_id}/backfill?after_id=` is super_admin-only, paginates one 1000-row JotForm API page per call (operator drives the loop externally with the returned `last_id`); `id`-ascending order so the cursor advances monotonically. Admin read API at `/admin/jotform/api/*`: `GET /forms` (admin-tier — super_admin / admin / dcRole-admin / dcRole-super_admin — returns enabled forms + COUNT-via-`Content-Range` submission counts), `GET /{form_id}/submissions` + `GET /{form_id}/submissions/{id}` + `GET /{form_id}/submissions.csv` (any authenticated session passes; permission scope via `accessibleSiteNumbersForSession` — super_admin / admin sees all, RM / RD / GM scopes by email-on-locations match against `locations.am_email` / `rm_email` / `site_email` via `getLocationsByContactEmail`, converting each integer site to BOTH zero-padded 3-digit AND unpadded string forms because the JotForm `typeA` widget returns site numbers as strings sometimes padded ("090" for Milford) and sometimes not ("127" for Elmira Heights) — operator-verified in sample payloads). Detail endpoint anti-leak: out-of-scope row returns 404, not 403 (don't expose existence). CSV uses the Brief 96 schema-union pattern — header is the union of every `answers` key across the date range as `answers__{key}__answer` columns (filled with `prettyFormat` value, falling back to `answer`); 10000-row safety cap, 416 on overflow. New `apps/web/wrangler.toml` `[[services]] binding = "JOTFORM_WORKER" service = "splash-jotform"` + `apps/web/cloudflare-env.d.ts` `JOTFORM_WORKER: Fetcher` declaration. New deploy doc `PRE_DEPLOY_JOTFORM.md` (Phase 1 SQL, secret bind commands, per-form backfill loop sample in PowerShell, webhook URL pattern, 7-step first-run smoke). CLAUDE.md glossary gains `jotform-worker` entry + a `JotForm submissions` paragraph (forms list, strip-noise rules, common fields extraction, permission gate notes). Workers.dev only at v1 per the operator-driven-cutover posture (CLAUDE.md constraint #9 / Brief 81 mirror); production routes operator-driven. New worker module shape: `src/{index.js, jotform.js, normalize.js, auth-gate.js, db.js, handlers/{webhook.js, admin.js}}`. Three secret bindings the operator must add before the worker is useful: `SUPABASE_SERVICE_KEY`, `SUPABASE_ANON_KEY`, `JOTFORM_API_KEY`, `JOTFORM_WEBHOOK_TOKEN`. Validation: root `pnpm typecheck` 18/18 green (jotform-worker added to the pipeline); `pnpm --filter @splash/jotform-worker exec wrangler deploy --dry-run` 741.06 KiB raw / 140.34 KiB gzipped (well under CF's 3 MiB compressed limit). Operator follow-up: (1) run Phase 1 SQL; (2) bind the four secrets; (3) backfill each form via the admin endpoint (smallest first — salt-log); (4) configure JotForm webhook per form in the Integrations panel; (5) submit a test entry on one form to confirm webhook end-to-end. Blocks Brief 108 (apps/web JotForm viewer pages).

(Previously: Brief 104 — Damage — fix internal-new-claim webhook photo URLs that were 404ing on click). Single-file bug fix in apps/damage-worker/src/index.ts fireInternalNewClaimNotification (~L3402-3413): the photo URL built by Brief 102 was `${baseOrigin}/claims-api/photo/${p.r2_key}` verbatim, but `r2_key` already includes the `claims/` prefix that `serveClaimPhoto` (packages/storage-r2) re-prepends before the R2 `.get()` — double-prefix → "Photo not found" (lookup against `claims/claims/MIL-…/damage_1.jpg`). Fixed by replicating the existing `damagePhotoUrl` helper pattern (apps/web/app/admin/damage/_lib/worker-fetch.ts L140-144): strip leading `claims/` defensively, split on `/`, URL-encode each segment, rejoin. Preserved the existing field-name shape (`p.content_type` → `mime`, `p.filename` → `original_filename`) — the brief's verbatim example used `p.mime` / `p.original_filename` which don't exist on the row type returned by `listPhotosForClaim`. No data / contract / wrangler.toml change; PA Flow B is unaffected (the photo URL field name `triggerBody()?['photos']?[*]?['url']` is unchanged). Customer-claim webhook (Brief 32) unaffected — it doesn't include photo URLs in its payload. CLAUDE.md INTERNAL_NEW_CLAIM_WEBHOOK_URL glossary entry extended with the Brief 104 strip-and-encode paragraph. Validation: root pnpm typecheck 17/17 green; pnpm --filter @splash/damage-worker exec wrangler deploy --dry-run 1728.81 KiB raw / 391.10 KiB gzipped (≈ unchanged vs Brief 102 baseline).

## Status snapshot

The 5-worker port + 7 shared packages are feature-complete on `pnpm typecheck` (13/13 green) and deployed only to `*.workers.dev`; apps/web has 8 real pages + 0 placeholders + 3 Step-4 stubs, with Brief 1 (login + middleware), Brief 2 (legacy URL redirects + global Header + logout flow), Brief 4 (admin landing dashboard), Brief 5a (damage claim list), Brief 5b (damage claim detail, read-only), Brief 5c (damage write actions: transitions, notes, check-request preview), Brief 5d (damage documents: upload, edit, delete + photo lightbox), Brief 6 (performance tracker list + new-submission form + LocationPicker typeahead), and Brief 7 (sysadmin UI: five-card super_admin gate consuming all 5 sysadmin-worker mutation endpoints) landed — the damage manager UI, performance tracker UI, and sysadmin UI are at functional parity with legacy. Production routes for the new workers remain commented in every `wrangler.toml`, and the legacy `info-signup-worker` still owns customer signup traffic on `splashcarwashes.info`. Cutover is gated on apps/web reaching parity for admin / signup / claim surfaces and on a dedicated cutover-strategy plan being run for the only production-critical worker (signup).

| 31 | Brief 31 | Server-action ID stability across deploys + graceful stale-tab UX — fixes the white-page client-exception that fires when apps/web is redeployed between page-load and form-submit (operator hit it 2026-05-05 while granting the claims tool to batavaiawash@splashcarwashes.com). **Layer 1 (root cause):** documented `NEXT_SERVER_ACTIONS_ENCRYPTION_KEY` build-time env var in `apps/web/.env.example` (rationale, generation snippets for PowerShell + bash, CF Workers Builds vs. `wrangler secret put` distinction) and added a comment block at the top of `apps/web/next.config.mjs` flagging the env var as build-time-only. No code change needed — Next.js 15.5.15 reads the env var natively during `next build`. Operator action item: generate a 32-byte base64 key once and set as a build-time env var on splash-web's CF Workers Builds config before the next deploy. **Layer 2 (defense in depth):** new `apps/web/app/admin/error.tsx` segment-level error boundary covering all `/admin/*` routes (`/admin/sysadmin`, `/admin/damage/[id]`, `/admin/pricing/*`, future action-driven pages) — detects `UnrecognizedActionError` via message-substring check (`"Server Action"` + `"was not found on the server"`) and renders an "App was updated / Reload" UI with `window.location.reload()` button; non-action errors fall through to a generic "Something went wrong / Try again" UI calling `reset()`. Includes a `console.warn` of the offending stale action ID for forward debugging. | 37 | Brief 37 | Mobile quote-upload fix — bypass Next 15 server actions for the multipart upload path. Legacy `info-signup-worker` upload (`legacy/damagemanager.js:2446` `handleDocumentUpload`) "just worked" on iPhone Safari because it's a vanilla CF-Worker multipart POST + 303 redirect; the new monorepo port routed the same multipart through `apps/web/app/admin/damage/[id]/actions.ts uploadDocumentAction` (a Next 15 server action invoked through OpenNext-on-Cloudflare-Workers), which threw the digest 924441341@e394 white-page on iPhone (Brief 36 Part B). Brief 37 retires the server-action path entirely: `apps/web/app/admin/damage/_components/UploadDocumentCard.tsx` was rewritten as a plain `<form action="/manage/api/claim/{id}/document" method="POST" enctype="multipart/form-data">` — relative URL resolves through CF same-zone routing in prod/staging and through `next.config.mjs`'s `/manage/api/:path*` rewrite in dev. `apps/damage-worker/src/index.ts` `handleDocumentUpload` was changed to 303-redirect on every branch (success → `${appsWebOrigin}/admin/damage/{claimId}`; validation/storage failure → same path with `?upload_error=<msg>`; new helper `buildUploadRedirect(request, claimId, errorMessage?)`); the apps-web origin is read from the request's `Origin` header (set by the browser on every form POST), falling back to `new URL(request.url).origin` for the same-zone production case. `apps/web/app/admin/damage/[id]/actions.ts uploadDocumentAction` deleted (along with its `damagePostMultipart` import and Brief 36 Part B's defensive try/catch — both now dead code). `damagePostMultipart` deleted from `apps/web/app/admin/damage/_lib/worker-fetch.ts` (sole consumer was the deleted action). `apps/web/app/admin/damage/[id]/page.tsx` reads `?upload_error=...` searchParam and renders a new `UploadErrorBanner` above `UploadDocumentCard` when present (re-introduces the param-based error pattern Brief 19 retired, scoped strictly to this one form because it bypasses `<ActionForm>`). The "Add document" anchor button (`<a href="#upload-document">`) was added next to the existing "Add note" button in `RecentNotesBox` — same shape, same smooth-scroll target (the global `html { scroll-behavior: smooth; }` from Brief 22 still applies). HEIC handling unchanged: `packages/storage-r2/src/index.ts uploadClaimPhoto` already mirrors the legacy `uploadToR2` (HEIC → JPEG via `env.IMAGES.input(file.stream()).output({ format: "image/jpeg" })`, fallback to passthrough on conversion failure). **Files modified:** `apps/damage-worker/src/index.ts` (handleDocumentUpload + new buildUploadRedirect helper), `apps/web/app/admin/damage/_components/UploadDocumentCard.tsx` (full rewrite — drop ActionForm, plain form posting to `/manage/api/...`), `apps/web/app/admin/damage/[id]/actions.ts` (delete uploadDocumentAction + drop damagePostMultipart import; re-title docblock), `apps/web/app/admin/damage/_lib/worker-fetch.ts` (delete damagePostMultipart), `apps/web/app/admin/damage/[id]/page.tsx` (read ?upload_error + render UploadErrorBanner; add "Add document" anchor button in RecentNotesBox), `BRIEFS/INDEX.md` (Brief 37 row marked Completed), `BRIEFS/QUEUE.md` (Brief 37 line moved to completed-tombstone block), `BUILD_STATE.md`, `BRIEFS/brief-037-mobile-upload-legacy-port-plus-add-doc-anchor.md` (Outcome filled in, Status set to Completed). **Files created:** none. **Files deleted:** none (logical deletions of `uploadDocumentAction` and `damagePostMultipart` are line-level edits inside surviving files). **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (1) **Worker returns 303 unconditionally** — every error branch redirects with `?upload_error=<msg>` rather than returning JSON. The brief explicitly chose Option A ("Worker responds with a 302 to /admin/damage/{claim_id} — browser follows the redirect"); JSON return values would only matter for non-browser API consumers, and the only consumer was the deleted `uploadDocumentAction`. (2) **Form action is a relative URL** (`/manage/api/claim/{id}/document`) rather than an absolute env-resolved URL — works in prod via CF same-zone routing, works in dev via the existing `next.config.mjs` rewrite. Avoids passing a server-resolved URL prop into the client component, which would have re-introduced the apps/web↔worker indirection this brief is designed to eliminate. (3) **Apps-web origin for the redirect Location is read from the `Origin` header**, falling back to `new URL(request.url).origin`. Browser sets `Origin` on every POST submission; same-zone production has matching origin/url-origin so both paths land at the same place; cross-zone dev (without Next rewrites) gets the apps/web localhost origin via the header. (4) **`?upload_error=<msg>` re-introduces the param-based banner pattern Brief 19 retired**, scoped to this one form. Brief 19's pattern flip was specifically for Next 15 server actions whose `redirect()` calls were unreliable on OpenNext-on-CF-Workers; raw HTTP form POSTs from a plain `<form>` to a Cloudflare Worker have no such issue (browser handles the redirect natively). The banner reads `?upload_error` once at SSR; on a fresh load (no form submit) the param is absent and nothing renders. (5) **`damagePostMultipart` deleted entirely** rather than left in place for future use — sole consumer was `uploadDocumentAction`, both retired together. CLAUDE.md guidance: "If you are certain that something is unused, you can delete it completely." (6) **No new pattern abstraction** — brief explicitly out-of-scoped a generalized "raw worker form" helper. The single use case is the document upload; if a second one shows up later, lift the pattern then. (7) **HEIC handling left as-is** — `packages/storage-r2/src/index.ts uploadClaimPhoto` already mirrors the legacy `uploadToR2`. Brief Phase 2.4 said "If the legacy code transcodes HEIC to JPEG before the R2 write, replicate that. … otherwise the legacy worker probably just passes HEIC through" — verified the new helper already transcodes via `env.IMAGES` when bound, falls back to passthrough when not, which is identical to legacy. No code change needed. (8) **`UploadErrorBanner` is rendered above `UploadDocumentCard`** (between the photo gallery and the upload form) rather than at the top of the page next to the confirm-delete banner. The error is contextually about the upload action; rendering it where the user's eye goes after the failed submit is the smaller cognitive jump. (9) **`docType`/`payToType` client state preserved** in the rewritten card so the conditional `required` UX (Brief 20 Bugs 5/6) still gates Quote-row amount/pay_to/vendor/vendor_address — worker re-validates as defense in depth, but pre-submit native HTML `required` attrs are still the better UX. (10) **The "Add document" anchor button's HTML structure mirrors "Add note" exactly** — same container, same Tailwind classes, same `<a href>` shape; the only diff is the href target (`#upload-document` vs `#add-note`) and label text. Pattern continuity matters more than micro-optimizing the markup. **Latent issues / forward flags:** (a) **Cross-origin dev without Next rewrites is unsupported.** If the operator runs `next dev` without `NEXT_PUBLIC_DAMAGE_WORKER_URL` set, the upload form's relative-URL POST 404s on apps/web (no rewrite to fall through to). Pre-existing limitation per `apps/web/app/admin/damage/_lib/worker-fetch.ts`'s docblock; this brief inherits the same posture. (b) **The submit-then-redirect pattern is page-reload-based, not optimistic.** Operators see a brief blank-then-loaded transition between submit and the post-redirect SSR. Compared to the prior `<ActionForm>` flow's `useActionState` spinner + inline result banner, this is slightly less polished; brief explicitly chose this trade-off (matches legacy iPhone path that took 2 seconds end-to-end). (c) **`?upload_error=<msg>` persists in URL after the user dismisses by clicking elsewhere.** A hard-reload re-shows the banner. Future polish could clear the param via `router.replace()` from a small client island wrapping the banner, but this isn't worth a separate brief unless operators complain. (d) **No mobile smoke test executed in headless** — the brief's DoD includes "Smoke test: upload an HEIC photo from iPhone Safari to staging — should succeed in <5 seconds." Per CLAUDE.md headless constraint, this session does not deploy; operator runs the smoke test on next deploy. (e) **Bundle delta on damage-worker:** 1661.70 KiB → 1662.94 KiB uncompressed (+1.24 KiB) / 376.97 KiB → 377.28 KiB gzip (+0.31 KiB). Slightly above the brief's "+0.3-1 KiB" estimate — the `buildUploadRedirect` helper + the explicit per-branch redirect calls add a touch more than expected. Comfortably within CF's 3 MiB compressed limit. (f) **Bundle delta on apps/web:** post-Brief-37 `/admin/damage/[id]` is 3.1 kB / 108 kB First Load JS (Next build output). The apps/web client-bundle delta is dominated by removing `<ActionForm>`'s `useActionState`/`useEffect` plumbing from the upload card; net: down a small number of bytes vs. the pre-Brief-37 baseline. Server-side action count drops from 5 to 4 in the route. (g) **Brief 36 Part B's defense-in-depth try/catch on `uploadDocumentAction` is now retired alongside the action itself** — the underlying root cause (server-action multipart path) is excised, so the wrap is no longer load-bearing. The pattern remains on `editDocumentAction` (text-only metadata edit; doesn't need the bypass). (h) **`uploadDocumentAction` is gone from `apps/web/app/admin/damage/[id]/actions.ts`'s exports** — any future code that imports it gets a TypeScript error pointing at the change. Grep across `apps/web` confirms zero remaining imports post-deletion. **Validation:** `pnpm typecheck` 13/13 successful, 8.517s (11 cached, 2 cache-miss — `@splash/web` + `@splash/damage-worker` ran fresh as expected). `pnpm --filter @splash/web build` succeeded — `next build` compiled in 7.9s, all 12 routes generated; `/admin/damage/[id]` route bundle 3.1 kB / 108 kB First Load JS. `pnpm --filter @splash/damage-worker exec wrangler deploy --dry-run --outdir=./dist` succeeded — bundle 1662.94 KiB / 377.28 KiB gzip (+1.24 KiB / +0.31 KiB vs Brief 35 baseline). **Operator action items:** (i) on next staging deploy, smoke-test HEIC upload from iPhone Safari — should succeed in <5 seconds (matches the legacy 2-second baseline that motivated this brief); (ii) verify the `?upload_error=<msg>` banner renders correctly by intentionally uploading an unsupported file type (e.g., `.txt` or oversized 11 MB JPEG); (iii) verify the "Add document" anchor button at the top of the claim detail page smooth-scrolls to the upload card on mobile + desktop. | **completed** (2026-05-06) | mobile upload bug closed; legacy port complete | items 5d (upload pipeline this brief patches), 22 (anchor-button + smooth-scroll pattern), 36 (try/catch defense-in-depth landed in Part B; this brief addresses underlying cause) |

| 43 | Brief 43 | GM/RM/admin/super_admin equipment-related override modal on the two active-repair approval transitions ("Approved — Pending Quotes" and "Approved — In House — Parts Ordered"). **Files created:** `apps/web/app/admin/damage/_components/EquipmentOverrideModal.tsx` — single named export `EquipmentOverrideSubmit` (client island that renders the transition form's submit button + a modal-gated pre-flight). Modal asks "Was this damage equipment related?", segmented No/Yes radios with No defaulted, reveals an `equipment_piece` `<select required>` on Yes (6 options hard-forked from `EQUIPMENT_CHOICES` at `apps/damage-worker/src/render/claim-form.ts:18` — Top Brush / Side Wraps / Conveyor / Dryer / Wheel Blaster / Other; comment in the new file pins the worker as canonical so future edits coordinate both sides). Click handler intercepts the submit only when `modalEnabled && enabled` (otherwise the form submits normally), opens the modal, and on confirm appends `override_equipment_related` ("yes"/"no") + `override_equipment_piece` (when yes) hidden inputs to the parent `<form>` and calls `form.requestSubmit()` — re-uses the existing `<ActionForm>` + React 19 `useActionState` pipeline unchanged. ESC + click-outside both cancel without submitting. **Files modified:** `apps/damage-worker/src/index.ts` — `handleStatusTransition` now reads `override_equipment_related` + `override_equipment_piece` from the form, validates (yes-only on the two target statuses, only no→yes flips, equipment_piece required + ≤200 chars; "no" or absent passes through unchanged), folds `equipment_related = 1` + `equipment_piece = ?` into the same UPDATE that lands the status change, then post-batch fires a new module-local `tryCreateMaintainXIfMissing` helper. The helper re-reads `claims.maintainx_workorder_id` via `getClaimById` (defense-in-depth dedupe — the loaded claim row's snapshot is pre-update, and a concurrent retry could have landed a WO meanwhile), constructs a transient post-UPDATE `ClaimRow` with `equipment_related=1`, `equipment_piece=overrideEquipmentPiece`, `claim_status=finalTo`, calls Brief 42's `createMaintainXWorkOrder` with the same fail-soft posture (8 s `AbortController` timeout, helper never throws past the handler), writes a `[maintainx]` `note`-typed activity-log entry on success/failure (success: "Work order #N created via approval-time override (mode: M)"; failure: "Override WO creation failed — {error} (status: S, mode: M)"). The status_change activity row's notes field gets a `[Equipment override] {actor} flipped equipment_related to yes during "{finalTo}" approval (equipment_piece: P)` sentinel suffix on the same status_change row — no new activity_type introduced. The transition response now includes optional `maintainx_attempted` + `maintainx_ok` flags (only when the override fired); apps/web `transitionAction` reads them and surfaces "Status updated to X, but the MaintainX work order couldn't be created — see activity log" on the failure path so the operator knows to check the timeline. `apps/web/app/admin/damage/[id]/page.tsx` — `TransitionSection` + `TransitionForm` now accept `equipmentRelated: 0 | 1`; new module-local `EQUIPMENT_OVERRIDE_TARGETS` set holds the two target statuses; the inline `<button type="submit">` was swapped for `<EquipmentOverrideSubmit>` with `modalEnabled={equipmentRelated === 0 && EQUIPMENT_OVERRIDE_TARGETS.has(to)}`. `apps/web/app/admin/damage/[id]/actions.ts` — `transitionAction` reads `result.body` and emits the failure-toast message when `maintainx_attempted === true && maintainx_ok === false`. **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (1) **Equipment-piece options hard-forked into the modal file** rather than hoisted into `@splash/types` — the brief explicitly delegated this to executor judgment. The list is short (6 entries), rarely changes, and the in-file comment pins the worker constant as canonical so future edits coordinate both sides. The cross-package alternative would have required a runtime export from `@splash/types` (which today is types-only) plus a re-export through both `@splash/damage-worker` (server) and `apps/web` (client) — three touch points instead of two. (2) **Modal-show condition is purely client-side**: the worker re-validates as defense-in-depth (rejecting yes-with-other-target and yes-when-already-yes), but the apps/web modal only opens when `claim.equipment_related === 0` AND target is one of the two active-repair statuses AND the button is enabled. Hidden inputs are appended via `appendHiddenInput` which dedupes by name — re-opening the modal after a previous confirm updates in place rather than appending duplicates. (3) **`form.requestSubmit(submitButton)` invoked with the button as the submitter** so the form's submit pipeline picks up the same React `formAction` it would on a native click — the `<ActionForm>` `useActionState` handler reacts to it identically. (4) **Post-batch dedupe re-read** in `tryCreateMaintainXIfMissing` short-circuits when `maintainx_workorder_id != null` already — covers the scenario where Brief 42's form-submit flow created a WO but the GM somehow approved with override anyway (shouldn't happen because the modal's `modalEnabled` check gates on the loaded `equipment_related === 0`, but a stale page state + concurrent submission could in theory race). The first writer wins via `updateMaintainXWorkOrderId`'s "UPDATE … WHERE maintainx_workorder_id IS NULL" semantics; the dedupe re-read avoids the wasted MaintainX POST. (5) **Override side-effect on the same status_change row** rather than a separate activity row — per brief Phase 1.5 ("don't introduce a separate activity_type"). The `[Equipment override]` sentinel + `[maintainx]` follow-up rows on the timeline tell the full story; the existing `note`-typed timeline entries cover the audit need. (6) **WO URL pattern + empirical probe** still inherited from Brief 42 — Brief 43 doesn't introduce new MaintainX call shape; the helper signature was stable before this brief landed. (7) **Modal styling uses inline tailwind classes** rather than ModalShell from `@splash/ui` — `ModalShell` is a 5-modal overlay primitive sized for the signup-worker's success/deny/warn cards, with hard-coded card width (max 420px, padded `40px 30px`, centered text). The override modal's content (left-aligned copy, segmented choice, conditional dropdown, two-column action row) didn't fit cleanly inside ModalShell's prescribed layout. The ad-hoc dialog implementation matches the visual posture of the existing confirm-delete banner pattern on the same page. **Latent issues / forward flags:** (a) **Empirical MaintainX probe still pending** — inherited from Brief 42; flipping `MAINTAINX_MODE` to `"production"` is still gated on the operator running the probe and confirming the WO ID extractor's fallback chain works. (b) **The override modal's "Yes" branch fires the MaintainX hook synchronously inside the request lifecycle** — adds up to 8 seconds (the AbortController timeout) to the transition's response time on the WO-failure path. apps/web's `<ActionForm>` shows the loading state via `data-pending="true"`; operators on a slow MaintainX response will see the button disabled for the duration. Acceptable for v1; future polish could move the hook to `ctx.waitUntil()` (out of scope per CLAUDE.md "no abstractions beyond what the task requires"). (c) **Worker's response-shape extension only emits `maintainx_attempted`/`maintainx_ok` on the override path** — non-override transitions return the same shape they always did, so existing apps/web message handling is unchanged for every other transition. (d) **The "Cancel" button + outside click + ESC all close the modal without firing the transition** — the underlying form is unchanged in DOM, so a subsequent click of the same submit button re-opens the modal in its initial state (No selected, no piece). (e) **No headless smoke test possible** — the brief asked to confirm "smoke-testing both paths in test mode produced exactly one MaintainX WO per claim (no duplicates from the dedupe gate)"; this requires a live MaintainX call which headless cannot exercise. Operator must run the smoke test post-deploy: (i) submit a customer claim with `equipment_related=1` (Brief 42 path), confirm one WO; (ii) on a separate claim, submit with `equipment_related=0`, then GM-approve into one of the two target statuses with the modal saying Yes, confirm one WO; (iii) attempt to submit the override modal twice on the same claim and confirm the dedupe gate prevents a second WO. **Validation:** `pnpm typecheck` 13/13 successful (7.119s, cache hit on 11, fresh build on `@splash/web` + `@splash/damage-worker` as expected — only those two packages were modified). `pnpm --filter @splash/web build` succeeded — `next build` compiled in 9.3s, all 12 routes generated; `/admin/damage/[id]` route bundle **3.1 kB → 4.11 kB / 108 kB → 109 kB First Load JS (+1.01 kB / +1 kB)** — `EquipmentOverrideSubmit` client island accounts for the delta. `pnpm --filter @splash/damage-worker exec wrangler deploy --dry-run` succeeded — bundle **1679.53 KiB → 1685.55 KiB uncompressed (+6.02 KiB) / 380.73 KiB → 381.86 KiB gzip (+1.13 KiB)** vs. Brief 42 baseline; comfortably within CF's 3 MiB compressed limit. The three new `[vars]` entries from Brief 42 still appear in the dry-run binding table. **Operator action items:** (1) Run the smoke test described in latent-issues (e). (2) The PA Parse JSON schema doesn't need updating — the override path doesn't extend the `claimData` payload (the WO ID lives on the claim row in D1, not on the transition's PA hook). (3) If the modal copy needs tweaking ("Was this damage equipment related?" / "...assigned to maintenance.") it lives in `apps/web/app/admin/damage/_components/EquipmentOverrideModal.tsx` as plain JSX. | **completed** (2026-05-06) | Nothing | items 42 (`createMaintainXWorkOrder` helper + `claims.maintainx_workorder_id` dedupe key this brief reuses), 41 (`damage_type` allow-list — read by the WO title via Brief 42's `buildTitle`), 19 (`<ActionForm>` server-action pattern this brief works inside), 5c (transition handler + UI surface) |
| 42 | Brief 42 | MaintainX work order auto-creation on `equipment_related=1`. **Phase 1 — D1 schema:** `ALTER TABLE claims ADD COLUMN maintainx_workorder_id INTEGER` run REMOTE on `splash-damage-claims` D1; PRAGMA confirms cid 56 (INTEGER, nullable). **Phase 2 — wrangler.toml + Env:** new top-level `[vars]` block in `apps/damage-worker/wrangler.toml` (`MAINTAINX_MODE = "test"`, `MAINTAINX_BASE_URL = "https://api.getmaintainx.com/v1"`, `APPS_WEB_BASE_URL = "https://splashcarwashes.info"`); `Env` interface in `apps/damage-worker/src/index.ts` extended with `MAINTAINX_API_KEY?: string` (secret, optional — fail-soft skip when unbound), `MAINTAINX_MODE: "production" \| "test"`, `MAINTAINX_BASE_URL: string`, `APPS_WEB_BASE_URL: string`. **Phase 3 — helper module:** new `apps/damage-worker/src/maintainx.ts` exports `createMaintainXWorkOrder(input)` and `MaintainXResult`. Module-level `ASSIGNEES_PRODUCTION = [{id: 409112}, {id: 426577}]` (Brett Sullivan + Scott Butler) and `ASSIGNEES_TEST = [{id: 443948}]` (Josh) const arrays for grep-ability. Title shape `Damage Claim - {locationPretty} - {damageTypeOrFallback}`, with `Other ({damage_other})` suffix when `damage_type === "Other"` and `damage_other` is non-empty. Multi-line plain-text description includes Claim ID / Submitted / Submitted by / Customer block / Vehicle block / Damage type / Damage description / Equipment involved / Pre-existing damage / Determination / Admin link (`{appsWebBaseUrl}/admin/damage/{claim_id}`) — null fields render as em-dash. Body shape: `{ title, description, priority: "HIGH", categories: ["Vehicle Damage"], assignees, locationId? }` (locationId omitted when null — MaintainX accepts WOs without a location). Helper never throws: fetch errors / non-2xx / non-JSON / missing WO id all return `{ ok: false, ... }`. WO ID extracted via fallback chain (`body.id` → `body.workOrder.id` → `body.data.id`) so the empirical-probe-pending response shape doesn't block the brief. **Phase 4 — handleClaimSubmission wiring:** hook fires inside the writeClaimBatch try block, AFTER location_pretty resolution and BEFORE the Power Automate POST so the WO ID can ride along on the PA payload. Constructs a transient `ClaimRow` from `insert` (avoids an extra D1 round trip) and calls the helper with an 8000ms `AbortController` timeout. On success: `updateMaintainXWorkOrderId(env.DB, claim_id, workOrderId)` writes the column AND the activity log gets a `[maintainx] Work order #{id} created (mode: {mode})` `note`-typed row. `claimData.maintainxWorkorderId` is set so the in-memory PA payload includes it (R2 submission JSON saved at step 4 already; doesn't pick up the late-arriving field — acceptable since PA is the authoritative downstream consumer). On failure: activity log gets `[maintainx] Work order creation failed — {error} (status: {status}, mode: {mode})`. Customer-facing 303 → /thanks redirect path is unchanged. **Phase 5 — Supabase helper:** new `getMaintainXLocationId(env, locationCode)` in `packages/db-supabase/src/locations.ts` does a TWO-STEP lookup because `locations` does NOT have a `location_code` column (sysadmin-worker confirms this at `index.ts:682`): step 1 fetches `pricing_simple.site` filtered by `location_code=eq.<sanitized>` (limit 1, `select=site`), step 2 fetches `locations.maintainx_id` filtered by `site=eq.<site>` (limit 1). Fail-soft on bad slug, missing rows, or non-2xx — returns null and the helper omits `locationId` from the WO body. **Phase 6 — manager detail:** new "MaintainX WO" Field row inserted directly after "Equipment involved" in `apps/web/app/admin/damage/[id]/page.tsx`; renders `#{id}` as a link to `https://app.getmaintainx.com/workorders/{id}` (URL pattern unverified — flagged in Outcome) when set, a yellow `bg-yellow-100/text-yellow-800` "MaintainX work order not created — see activity log" pill when `equipment_related === 1` and the column is NULL, em-dash otherwise. **ClaimRow extended** with `maintainx_workorder_id: number \| null`; `updateMaintainXWorkOrderId` D1 helper added (UPDATE only when column IS NULL, so duplicate creation attempts are no-ops). **Validation:** `pnpm typecheck` 13/13 successful (25.681s, 13 cache-miss as expected — types changes invalidate everything downstream); `pnpm --filter @splash/web build` succeeded — `/admin/damage/[id]` route bundle 3.1 kB / 108 kB First Load JS (zero delta — server-rendered Field row, no client JS); `pnpm --filter @splash/damage-worker exec wrangler deploy --dry-run` succeeded — bundle 1679.53 KiB / 380.73 KiB gzip (was 1667.47 KiB / 378.06 KiB gzip post-Brief-41 → +12.06 KiB / +2.67 KiB gzip; helper module + transient ClaimRow construction + activity log writes). **Operator action items:** (1) **Empirical MaintainX probe** — operator must hit `POST /v1/workorders` once via `wrangler dev` or staging with `MAINTAINX_MODE=test` and the bound `MAINTAINX_API_KEY`, capture the response JSON, and confirm the WO ID lives at `body.id` (current default in the extractor's fallback chain). If the field is `body.workOrder.id` or `body.data.id`, the helper already handles it; if it's something else, edit `extractWorkOrderId` in `apps/damage-worker/src/maintainx.ts`. (2) **Verify the WO URL pattern** — `app.getmaintainx.com/workorders/{id}` is plausible but unverified. Click a WO link from `/admin/damage/[id]` after a probe-created test WO and confirm it lands on the correct WO. (3) **Update PA Parse JSON schema** to include `maintainxWorkorderId` (number, nullable) so SharePoint can surface the WO link alongside Brief-41's `damageType`/`damageOther`. (4) **Flip `MAINTAINX_MODE` to `"production"` at cutover** — manual `wrangler.toml` edit + `wrangler deploy`, OR override via the CF Workers dashboard. Until flipped, every customer claim with `equipment_related=1` pages Josh only. **Latent issues / forward flags:** (a) **Brief incorrectly described `locations.location_code` as a queryable column** — that column doesn't exist on the locations table; `getMaintainXLocationId` does a two-step pricing_simple→locations lookup via `site` (text). The two-step adds one extra REST call but only fires when `equipment_related=1`. (b) **Activity log uses `note` activity_type** with `[maintainx]` prefix because the D1 CHECK constraint rejects new activity_type values; the brief asked for new `maintainx_workorder_created`/`maintainx_workorder_failed` types. Following the existing legacy pattern (per `packages/types/src/claims.ts:75-79` — "An audit-log annoyance but not a bug"). (c) **R2 submission JSON written at step 4 doesn't pick up the late-arriving `maintainxWorkorderId`** — the field is set after the unconditional R2 save. PA payload (step 6) DOES carry it. Re-saving the R2 JSON after MaintainX returns is a small follow-up if forensic recovery from R2 needs the WO ID; flagged here for completeness. (d) **`ClaimInsert` was NOT extended with `maintainx_workorder_id`** despite the brief's "Read first" line implying it should be — the column always starts NULL on initial insert (we don't have a WO ID at writeClaimBatch time) and is filled via `updateMaintainXWorkOrderId` post-MaintainX. Brief 43's GM modal will also use the UPDATE path (the row already exists). Adding it to ClaimInsert would be no-op churn. (e) **Test WO created during probing: zero from this session** — headless cannot exercise live MaintainX. Operator probe will create at most one test WO assigned to Josh. | **completed** (2026-05-06) | Brief 43 (GM-side modal for setting `equipment_related=yes` post-submit; reuses `createMaintainXWorkOrder` + `updateMaintainXWorkOrderId` dedupe gate) | items 41 (`damage_type` column read by the WO title), 33 (Supabase pricing_simple→site→locations lookup pattern this brief extends), 23 (`handleClaimSubmission` pipeline this brief hooks into) |

| 93 | Brief 93 | Forms — lookup mechanism (resolver + on-demand endpoint + submit-time re-resolve). Fifth user-visible deliverable for the form-builder feature; with this brief landed every one of the 16 field types is functional end-to-end. `packages/db-supabase/src/lookup.ts` replaces the Brief 89 stub with the real `resolveLookup` — dispatches on `source.table`: pricing_simple → direct SELECT WHERE keyColumn = keyValue (limit 1; pricing_simple has multiple rows per location_code, the denorm columns are identical across them); locations → two-hop, first derive `pricing_simple.site` from pricing_simple WHERE keyColumn = keyValue, then SELECT FROM locations WHERE site_number = <that>. Brief 62 fix (right join column is `locations.site_number`, NOT `locations.site` which is the location name) reapplied here. Two-hop is hidden inside the helper — callers specify `sourceTable: 'locations'` and `keyColumn: 'pricing_simple.location_code'` and the helper does the rest. Returns `string | null`; null + log on Supabase error / unknown sourceTable / missing keyColumn segment. New `apps/forms-worker/src/lookup/resolve.ts` exports `handleLookupResolve` for `POST /forms/api/lookup/{slug}`: reads form by slug, validates the body's `lookup_field_id` is a `lookup` field in the current schema, validates `sourceTable + sourceColumn` are in `LOOKUP_SOURCES` (defense against hand-edited `form_versions.schema` JSONB exfiltrating arbitrary pricing_simple/locations columns), instantiates a service-key client via `createServiceClient` from `@splash/db-supabase`, calls `resolveLookup`, returns `{value, resolved_at}` 200 with `Cache-Control: no-store`. Same-origin via `isOriginAllowed`; structured 4xx errors via `jsonError`. `apps/forms-worker/src/submit/index.ts` extended with the canonical server-side re-resolve loop (planning Decision 5a.ii) — runs after the file/signature R2 HEAD pass, before validation: for each lookup field, find its key field (key field missing in schema → `lookup_failed`); read the submitted key value; if empty, drop the payload entry (display_only) or store `""` (other modes); otherwise resolve fresh; log `[forms.lookup] drift detected at submit` if the client value is non-empty AND differs from the fresh value (tampering or mid-fill drift signal); store the FRESH value (`""` when null + allow_empty); for `display_only` mode delete the payload key entirely; for `nullBehavior === 'block_submit'` + required + null fresh return 422 `{error: 'lookup_failed', fields: {...}}` so the operator sees a precise per-field error. `packages/forms-schema/src/validators/payload.ts` lookup branch un-stubbed: display_only returns null (validator skipped — submit deletes the key); otherwise returns `z.string().min(1)` when block_submit + required, plain `z.string()` otherwise (allow_empty). `apps/forms-worker/src/submit/parse.ts` lookup skip is now intentional (file header docblock + inline comment updated). `apps/forms-worker/src/render/fields/lookup.ts` extended with three mode-aware variants: `prefill_hidden` → `<input type="hidden">` (no UI, value round-trips silently); `prefill_visible` → disabled `<input type="text">` populated visibly when the key field changes; `display_only` → styled `<div class="field-display-value">` callout, no input, no payload entry. All three carry `data-lookup-field-id="${field.id}"` so the client JS can route resolves back to the right element. `apps/forms-worker/static/forms-public.js` extended with `wireLookups(formEl, slug)` — groups dependent lookups by their key field id, listens on `input` + `change` (250ms debounce), POSTs to `/forms/api/lookup/{slug}`, populates the visible UI (text input value, or display callout div via direct text-content swap). Initial resolve fires if the key field has a value at load time (browser-restored back-button state). `apps/forms-worker/src/render/shell.ts` `SHELL_CSS` extended with `.field-display-only .field-display-value` rule — light-gray background + cyan left border + 44px min-height callout matching the brief's spec. `apps/forms-worker/src/index.ts` routes `POST /forms/api/lookup/{slug}` to the new handler; header docblock route inventory + executive summary updated. Caching: none — sub-10ms point reads on indexed columns; cache invalidation complexity isn't worth the marginal speedup at v1. **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (a) `createServiceClient` is imported directly from `@splash/db-supabase` (not re-exported via `db/forms`) — the brief sample's import path was a forward-reference that didn't match the actual package surface; the actual export is at the package root, no re-export needed. The forms-worker has been intentionally avoiding `@supabase/supabase-js` for the forms-table reads (Brief 89/90/91/92 used direct PostgREST `fetch()`), but the SDK is already a transitive dep via `@splash/auth` (post-Brief-91 5.7× bundle bump) so importing `createServiceClient` adds no new bundle weight. (b) `data` cast through `unknown` first in resolveLookup — `SupabaseClient.from().select(column)` returns `GenericStringError` union; direct cast to `Record<string, unknown>` errored; `as unknown as Record<string, unknown>` is the canonical workaround. (c) input `id` attribute stays `${field.id}` (matches `fieldLabel`'s `for` attr in `util.ts`). The brief sample wrote `id="lookup-${field.id}"` but that breaks the label association; reverted. (d) `cssAttrEscape` rather than `CSS.escape` polyfill — UUID-shaped ids only contain hex + hyphen so the escape universe is small; `\\` and `"` are the only characters worth escaping for `[id="..."]` selectors. (e) Initial resolve fires on page load when the key field already has a value — handles browser back-button form restoration; matches the planning intent that the dependent field reflects the key field's value at all times. (f) Drift logging compares `clientValue !== ""` (truthy) AND `!== fresh` — empty client value isn't drift, just an unfilled lookup; saves log noise. (g) Empty key value branch on the submit re-resolve drops the payload key for display_only and stores `""` for other modes (rather than deleting the key for prefill_visible / prefill_hidden) so the validator sees the field and validates `z.string()` consistently — required + allow_empty fields can submit empty without erroring. (h) `block_submit` triggers `lookup_failed` only when `fresh == null` AND `field.required` — required-field semantics are operator-controlled at form-build time. (i) Render fields use `escapeHtml` on every interpolated value (id, key, attrs) per the existing convention; this brief introduces no new escape paths. **Latent issues found:** (1) The drift-logging branch only fires for `prefill_visible` mode — `prefill_hidden` writes the value silently after fetch, so by submit time the client value matches the fresh value (no drift to detect). Genuine prefill_hidden tampering from DevTools (rewriting the hidden input's value pre-submit) IS detectable because the client value at submit time will differ from the fresh re-resolve. Verified by reading the submit handler's drift-comparison shape. (2) `resolveLookup` accepts `keyValue` as-is for the .eq query — Supabase's PostgREST handles SQL escaping; no injection risk. (3) The `LOOKUP_SOURCES` registry hardcodes only string-typed columns plus one boolean (`mla_location`); booleans coerce to string via `String(value)` per the existing return shape — operator-facing display of a "true"/"false" string isn't ideal but v2-typed-lookups are out of scope. **Files created:** `apps/forms-worker/src/lookup/resolve.ts`. **Files modified:** `packages/db-supabase/src/lookup.ts` (stub → real resolver), `apps/forms-worker/src/index.ts` (route + import + header docblock), `apps/forms-worker/src/submit/index.ts` (re-resolve loop + LOOKUP_SOURCES + resolveLookup imports), `apps/forms-worker/src/submit/parse.ts` (intentional-skip docblock + inline comment), `apps/forms-worker/src/render/fields/lookup.ts` (three mode-aware render variants + `data-lookup-field-id`), `apps/forms-worker/src/render/shell.ts` (CSS for `.field-display-only`), `apps/forms-worker/static/forms-public.js` (wireLookups + resolveLookupField + cssAttrEscape; header docblock), `packages/forms-schema/src/validators/payload.ts` (lookup case un-stubbed), `PRE_DEPLOY_FORMS.md` (Section 5 gets 7 Brief-93 smoke tests covering visible/hidden lookup population, drift defense via DevTools tampering, block_submit `lookup_failed` 422, and direct curl of the resolve endpoint with cross-origin / unknown-field error paths), `CLAUDE.md` (forms-worker glossary Brief 93 paragraph), `BUILD_STATE.md` (this entry + Last-updated bump), `BRIEFS/INDEX.md` (Brief 93 row), `BRIEFS/brief-093-forms-lookup-mechanism.md` (Status → Completed, Outcome filled). **Validation:** `pnpm --filter @splash/db-supabase typecheck` green (after the `as unknown as` cast fix); `pnpm --filter @splash/forms-schema typecheck` green; `pnpm --filter @splash/forms-worker typecheck` green; `pnpm --filter @splash/forms-worker exec wrangler deploy --dry-run` green (989.26 KiB / 190.44 KiB gzip — +13.93 KiB / +3.33 KiB gzip vs Brief 92's 975.33 / 187.11; from the new lookup module + the resolveLookup helper); root `pnpm typecheck` 17/17 green. No Delete endpoint per planning Decision 7 — destructive ops via SQL only; CASCADE FKs make `DELETE FROM forms WHERE id=...` safe. (4) **Group-header label also got the address-shape fallback** (out of strict scope but consistent with the brief's intent — Phase 4 mentioned the FilterBar specifically but the address-fallback root cause means the same data leaks into group headers via the same roster response). (5) **Timezone story = option (a) by default.** The brief listed verification-via-test-entry as a deferred operator step; assuming option (a) (display conversion only — worker stores UTC, display converts to EST) is the safer default because it doesn't require any storage rewrite. Empty intersection short-circuits to a 200 with `rows: []` (same posture as the existing out-of-scope `site_number` collapse). `handleListSubmissions` + `handleCsvExport` in `src/handlers/admin.js` call `resolveLocationFilters(env, session, url.searchParams)` instead of reading scope directly; the existing `siteNumbersFilter` Set plumbing into `db.js`'s `listSubmissions` / `listSubmissionsForCsv` is unchanged. New `src/handlers/roster.js` (~270 LOC) serves `GET /admin/jotform/api/roster` — any-session gate — returning `{ regional_directors, regional_managers, locations, scope }` derived from one `locations` PostgREST read + one `pricing_simple` lookup for `location_pretty` (the two-table join is locations.site_number → pricing_simple.site as text); scoped to the caller's accessible site_numbers when not admin-tier. Email + location grouping uses the same most-common-name tiebreak as `listContactRoster` in `@splash/db-supabase`. `index.js` route comment block + `handlers/admin.js` docblock updated; route table in `handleAdminApi` gains the `/admin/jotform/api/roster` branch. **apps/web side.** `apps/web/app/admin/jotform/_lib/worker-fetch.ts` extended with `getRoster(): Promise<JotformRoster | null>`, four new types (`JotformRoster` / `RosterAm` / `RosterRm` / `RosterLocation`), and three new opts (`amEmail` / `rmEmail` / `locationCode`) on both `listSubmissions` and `csvExportUrl`. New client component `apps/web/app/admin/jotform/[form_id]/_components/FilterBar.tsx` (~150 LOC) — three URL-driven `<select>`s (RD / RM / Location); RM + Location options narrow client-side when RD is selected (UX hint; worker re-validates on submit either way). Each `<select>` change writes its key to the URL search params and clears `?offset=` (narrow filters may push the prior offset past the new total). Empty-roster slots (e.g., a GM with one site sees no RDs over them) are hidden entirely. `apps/web/app/admin/jotform/[form_id]/page.tsx` rewritten — `getRoster()` runs in parallel with `listSubmissions()` via `Promise.all`; FilterBar renders above DateRangePicker; the row table is replaced by a `<GroupedSubmissions>` component that buckets the current 50-row page by location → date and renders headers ("Binghamton (127) — Regional Manager: Bob Smith" / "May 11, 2026 (3 submissions)"). Single-location pages render flat (no outer group chrome). Pagination's URL builder now passes through filter params + `from` + `to`. **Decisions made on operator's behalf.** (1) Roster-API shape: one endpoint returning three arrays (RD + RM + Location) rather than three separate endpoints (the brief explicitly preferred this — one round-trip backs the FilterBar). (2) AM-narrows-RM-and-Location client-side cascade implemented as a UX nicety — operators get sensible defaults without an extra round-trip. The worker-side `resolveLocationFilters` intersect is the defense-in-depth. (3) Clearing `?offset=` on every filter change: narrow filter sets imply the prior offset may be past the new total; clearing avoids "Showing rows 51-100 of 12" weirdness. (4) Single-location-page flat rendering: the date sub-headers carry the structure when there's only one location group; redundant outer "Binghamton" headers were the operator's specific gripe in the brief. (5) `pricing_simple` join in roster: locations.site_number → pricing_simple.site (text, denormalized by `trg_sync_pricing_simple`) — the same pattern `getMaintainXLocationId` uses (Brief 42 / 62 forward). The pricing_simple-rooted resolution is fine for read-only display because pricing_simple is canonical for `location_pretty` + `location_code` mapping. (6) `getLocationsByContactEmail` is the resolver for the `am_email` / `rm_email` filter params; it's already used by `accessibleSiteNumbersForSession` (Brief 107). The `matched_via` field on `UserAccessibleLocation` (Brief 71) lets us scope each filter to only the contact column it represents (`am_email` filter → keep rows matched via `am_email` only, not site_email). (7) Sanitization: filter params strip via `^[a-zA-Z0-9_-]{1,64}$` for location_code and `^[^@\s]+@[^@\s]+\.[^@\s]+$` for emails; rejected values silently become `null`/no-op (matching the existing `sanitizeSiteNumber` posture). **Latent issues / forward flags.** (a) Cross-page aggregate counts ("this RD's total submissions YTD") deferred to v2 (separate brief candidate — JotForm's `/admin/damage/reporting` equivalent). (b) Per-user-saved filter presets deferred to v2 (URL-driven filters are bookmarkable in v1). (c) Group-by-RM or group-by-date as alternate primary groupings deferred to v2 (current: location-primary, date-secondary). (d) Per-form custom field columns in the table deferred to v2 (the generic Submitted / Site / Status / View columns stay). (e) The Brief 110 brief noted "any locations with NULL am_email / rm_email would silently drop them from the dropdown resolution" — confirmed: a location whose `am_email` is NULL doesn't surface as an option to filter on (correct behavior — there's no AM to filter by), but the location itself still appears in the Location dropdown. | 2026-05-11 | **Brief 108 completed:** Fleet update webhook — coerce top-level `id` to integer (use `arr[0].id`). Single-line fix in `apps/fleet-inquiry-worker/src/admin.js` `handleUpdateSubmission` (~L443-449): the `fireFleetSubmissionUpdateWebhook` call's top-level `id` field switched from the URL-path string (parsed by the router as `"21"`) to the PostgREST response row's integer `arr[0].id`. **Bug context.** After the operator migrated `fleet_submissions.id` from UUID to bigint identity, PA's HTTP-trigger schema (correctly typed `integer`) started returning 400 at the trigger level (schema validation, before any actions run) on every dashboard PATCH webhook fire. CF Workers Logs showed `[fleet-submission-update] POST failed for 21: status 400`. SharePoint was NOT receiving status/notes updates; the dashboard PATCH itself succeeded and Supabase recorded the edit. `row.id` inside the payload was already an integer (PostgREST returns the column as a JSON number); only the top-level `id` was a string. Brief 105 was drafted when the column was UUID — the shape was correct at the time. PA was already updated post-column-switch (operator-verified schema + Filter Query reading `triggerBody()?['id']` without quotes). The worker was the lone holdout sending a string. **Fix.** Sourcing the integer id from the same row that's about to ship inside `row` guarantees top-level `id` and `row.id` are consistent post-fix; PA's schema now accepts the wire shape on every fire. **Decisions made on operator's behalf.** None — the brief specified the exact call-site fix. The out-of-scope alternative (adding `Number(payload.id)` coercion inside `fireFleetSubmissionUpdateWebhook`) was explicitly rejected by the brief on grounds the helper should stay a thin POST wrapper. **Latent issues / forward flags.** Pattern reminder for future briefs: when a Supabase column changes type (UUID→bigint, text→jsonb, etc.), audit any worker code that passes URL-path params as that column's value in downstream payloads — the URL-path parser returns strings, the database returns whatever shape PostgREST encodes, and PA-side schema validators won't catch the mismatch until production traffic hits them. The apps/web `FleetSubmissionRow.id` type (`apps/web/app/admin/fleet/_lib/worker-fetch.ts`) was flagged in Brief 106's context as Option B cleanup; it's explicitly out of scope here and remains a v2 candidate. No other workers (damage, workorders, signup, jotform, forms, sysadmin) need the same fix — their webhook payloads already ship ids from the data row, not URL params (audited via brief context). **Validation.** Root `pnpm typecheck` 18/18 green. `pnpm --filter @splash/fleet-inquiry-worker exec wrangler deploy --dry-run` 786.26 KiB raw / 149.74 KiB gzipped — bundle clean and unchanged vs Brief 105 baseline; `.tmp-build/` cleaned up post-build. Three render-site fixes for a visible bug the operator reported on 2026-05-11: SUBMITTED column on the list page was empty for every row because the JSX read `r.created_at`, but `fleet_submissions.created_at` is unpopulated — the fleet-inquiry-worker writes `submitted_at` explicitly on insert (per Brief 86's note: "fleet_submissions has only `submitted_at`, not the conventional `created_at`"). Same bug in two places on the detail page: the "Created at" key/value entry was always blank, and the under-title "Submitted N days ago" caption read `created_at` too. Brief 86 fixed the worker-side filter / sort + CSV ordering; this brief closes the apps/web render-time gap that slipped through that fix. **Fixes.** `apps/web/app/admin/fleet/page.tsx` L172-173 — list-row Submitted cell now reads `r.submitted_at` (title attr + visible text both swapped). `apps/web/app/admin/fleet/[id]/page.tsx` — dropped the always-blank "Created at" key/value entry from the detail grid (L60 in pre-change file); under-title caption now reads `row.submitted_at` (L123 pre-change). **Decision made on operator's behalf.** Option A on the type per the brief's recommendation: `created_at: string` stays on `FleetSubmissionRow` in `apps/web/app/admin/fleet/_lib/worker-fetch.ts` — harmless leftover, the worker still `SELECT *`s and PostgREST returns whatever columns Supabase has, no downstream consumers after the fix. Removing the field would touch more code paths (every consumer of the type) for no functional gain; if the column ever gets added to Supabase later, the leftover field continues to work. **Latent issues / forward flags.** None — the bug surface is closed. Worker-side rendering is unchanged. Adding a real `created_at` column to `fleet_submissions` with a Supabase row-default of `now()` was explicitly out of scope (the legacy worker has shipped with `submitted_at` for the row's entire history; splitting the timestamp surface in two for no product gain). Backfilling historical row data is not applicable (only rendering changes). **Files modified.** `apps/web/app/admin/fleet/page.tsx`, `apps/web/app/admin/fleet/[id]/page.tsx`, `CLAUDE.md` (Fleet inquiries admin glossary entry gains a Brief 106 one-liner noting apps/web reads `submitted_at`), `BUILD_STATE.md` (this entry + Last-updated bump), `BRIEFS/INDEX.md` (Brief 106 row), `BRIEFS/brief-106-fleet-admin-use-submitted-at-not-created-at.md` (Status → Completed; Outcome filled). **Files created.** None. **Files deleted.** None. Net diff: 8 lines across 2 TSX files plus doc rows. **Validation.** Root `pnpm typecheck` 17/17 green (cache miss only on @splash/web — both fleet TSX files changed; everything else cache-hit). `pnpm --filter @splash/web build` succeeded — `/admin/fleet` 920 B / 106 kB First-Load JS, `/admin/fleet/[id]` 739 B / 106 kB First-Load JS (both unchanged vs Brief 105 baseline — the three-character text swap doesn't move bundle weight). **Operator post-deploy smoke** (deferred — no headless deploy per CLAUDE.md): load `/admin/fleet`, confirm SUBMITTED column shows a relative-date string for every row (e.g., "2h ago", "3d ago", or absolute "May 11, 2026" via the title attribute on hover). Load any detail page, confirm the under-title caption shows a date and the "Created at" row is gone from the key/value grid. | Claude Code |

Rejects unknown body keys with 400 as defense-in-depth. Rejects all-missing bodies with 400. New `ALLOWED_STATUSES` Set at the top of `admin.js`. Stamps `splash_notes_updated_{at,by}` / `status_updated_{at,by}` per-field on each PATCH (operator runs Phase 1 SQL once: `ALTER TABLE fleet_submissions ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS status_updated_at timestamptz, ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS status_updated_by text, ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS splash_notes_updated_at timestamptz, ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS splash_notes_updated_by text;` in Supabase SQL Editor — no migration framework in this repo). After PostgREST PATCH commits, fires new `fireFleetSubmissionUpdateWebhook(env, payload)` helper (co-located at the bottom of `admin.js` — worker is verbatim-lifted JS per Brief 81; separate `notifications.js` module deferred until a second notification surface appears) with `{id, change_type:'status'|'notes'|'both', changed_fields, actor:{email}, row: <updated row>}`. 15s `AbortSignal.timeout` + try/catch swallows on throw / non-2xx (logged `[fleet-submission-update] POST {failed|error} for {id}: ...`). The existing 30-min PA new-submission ingest flow stays untouched — Brief 105 adds a parallel update channel keyed on dashboard edits only. `apps/fleet-inquiry-worker/wrangler.toml` comment block extended documenting the new secret. **apps/web side.** New `apps/web/app/admin/fleet/_lib/constants.ts` exports `FLEET_STATUS_OPTIONS: readonly ['new','reviewed','contacted','closed']`, `FleetStatus` type, `isFleetStatus(v)` guard, and `FLEET_STATUS_PILL_CLASS` (record keyed by enum value + `default` fallback for legacy NULL/unexpected values). `updateFleetSubmissionNotes(id, notes)` in `_lib/worker-fetch.ts` renamed `updateFleetSubmission(id, {splashNotes?, status?})` (parameter-object pattern); the body sent to the worker is `{splash_notes?, status?}` — both optional, caller (or worker) enforces "at least one". `[id]/actions.ts` server action renamed `updateSplashNotesAction` → `updateSubmissionAction` and now reads both `splash_notes` + `status` from FormData on every save; validates `status` against `isFleetStatus()` as defense-in-depth before calling the worker. `[id]/page.tsx` "Splash Notes" section relabeled "Status & Notes" with a Status dropdown above the textarea (options from `FLEET_STATUS_OPTIONS`; defaults to `row.status` if valid else `"new"`) and a single "Save" button driving both fields in one PATCH. The read-only key/value grid below continues to show the persisted values. `page.tsx` `StatusPill` widened to four discrete colors via `FLEET_STATUS_PILL_CLASS` (blue / sudsy / amber / emerald; neutral fallback for unexpected values). **Decisions made on operator's behalf.** (1) Webhook helper co-located at the bottom of `admin.js` rather than a separate `notifications.js` module — the worker is verbatim-lifted JS (Brief 81), so the modularity gain is smaller than the damage-worker `notifications.ts` case (Brief 101). When a second update notification surface appears on fleet, hoist then. (2) Color tokens for the new pill states: `bg-amber-100 text-amber-900` (already used by `StatusActionPill.tsx` for damage) and `bg-emerald-100 text-emerald-900` (default Tailwind palette; consistent with the "closed" semantic). (3) Single ActionForm submit for both fields rather than two separate forms — operator confirmed Supabase should be authoritative for both, so one combined Save is more ergonomic and the payload's `change_type:'both'` is the trigger for PA to update both SharePoint columns in one shot. (4) `changed_fields` order is `["notes","status"]` when both ride the same PATCH (push order in the worker is `splash_notes` first, then `status` — `change_type` is the authoritative discriminator for PA). (5) Worker re-validates `status` against the four-value enum even though the dropdown's options are constrained; UI gating is a UX hint, not access control. (6) Worker rejects unknown body keys (`Unknown body keys: ...`) so a future typo on apps/web's side fails fast rather than silently dropping the field. (7) Renamed `updateFleetSubmissionNotes` → `updateFleetSubmission` (and the server action) because the new shape genuinely takes either field; keeping the legacy name would be misleading. The brief allowed either rename or extend; rename was the cleaner option since the helper has exactly one call site. **Latent issues / forward flags.** (a) Existing rows get NULL in the new audit columns until their first PATCH (no backfill in scope; operator action item if historical data is needed). (b) Last-write-wins audit only — no append-only history table. v2 candidate if needed. (c) No bidirectional sync (SharePoint → Supabase). Operator confirmed Supabase is authoritative going forward; SharePoint edits are out of policy. (d) No per-actor permission scoping — admin/super_admin auth gate covers all fleet status edits. (e) Phase 1 SQL is operator-side; the worker will write the four audit columns whether they exist or not — if the columns are missing, PostgREST returns an error and the PATCH returns 500 (the worker logs the upstream errText). Operator should run the SQL before deploying. (f) Phase 4 PA flow build is also operator-side; the worker fires the webhook regardless of whether PA is configured — when unbound or unreachable the fire is a no-op. (g) Email used for audit-column stamps + webhook actor is taken from `gate.session.email` (from `auth_unified` via `@splash/auth`'s `authenticate()`); the new defensive 401 branch for blank email shouldn't fire in practice. **Files modified.** `apps/fleet-inquiry-worker/src/admin.js` (file header docblock extended, `ALLOWED_STATUSES` constant added, `handleUpdateSubmission` rewritten, new `fireFleetSubmissionUpdateWebhook` helper appended). `apps/fleet-inquiry-worker/wrangler.toml` (binding comment block extended). `apps/web/app/admin/fleet/_lib/worker-fetch.ts` (renamed `updateFleetSubmissionNotes` → `updateFleetSubmission`, new `UpdateFleetSubmissionInput` interface, docblock rewritten). `apps/web/app/admin/fleet/[id]/actions.ts` (renamed action to `updateSubmissionAction`, validates `status` against `isFleetStatus`). `apps/web/app/admin/fleet/[id]/page.tsx` (import swap from `updateSplashNotesAction` → `updateSubmissionAction`, added Status dropdown above textarea, "Save Notes" → "Save", header relabeled "Status & Notes"). `apps/web/app/admin/fleet/page.tsx` (import + `StatusPill` widened). `CLAUDE.md` (Fleet inquiries admin glossary entry extended; | 2026-05-11 | **Brief 104 completed:** Damage — strip `claims/` prefix in internal-new-claim webhook photo URLs. Single-file bug fix in `apps/damage-worker/src/index.ts` `fireInternalNewClaimNotification` (~L3402-3413). **Root cause.** Brief 102 built photo URLs as `${baseOrigin}/claims-api/photo/${p.r2_key}` verbatim, but `r2_key` already includes the `claims/` prefix that `serveClaimPhoto` (`packages/storage-r2/src/index.ts`) re-prepends before the R2 `.get()` — every photo link in the internal email resolved to "Photo not found" (`/claims-api/photo/claims/MIL-…/damage_1.jpg` → R2 lookup against `claims/claims/MIL-…/damage_1.jpg`). **Fix.** Mirrors the existing `damagePhotoUrl` helper pattern (`apps/web/app/admin/damage/_lib/worker-fetch.ts` L140-144): strip the leading `claims/` (defensive `.startsWith` check), split on `/`, URL-encode each segment, rejoin. The strip is defensive — every row written by `uploadClaimPhoto` carries the prefix today, but the check makes the fix resilient to any historical or future row that doesn't. **Decision made on operator's behalf.** Preserved the existing field-name shape (`p.content_type` → `mime`, `p.filename` → `original_filename`) — the brief's verbatim "currently" and "replacement" example used `p.mime` / `p.original_filename` which don't exist on the row type returned by `listPhotosForClaim`. Brief 102's actual code path used `content_type` / `filename`; this stays consistent and avoids breaking PA Flow B's existing JSON shape. **Out of scope.** Extracting a shared `buildPhotoServeUrl` helper in `packages/storage-r2` (3 lines of duplication; v2 candidate when a third caller appears) and changing `serveClaimPhoto` to accept either prefixed or unprefixed keys (contract change with risk of breaking existing callers — the legacy customer-facing photo route at `/claims-api/photo/<suffix>` keeps its current contract). **Scope of bug + fix.** Photo links inside emails fired by `INTERNAL_NEW_CLAIM_WEBHOOK_URL` (Brief 102). The customer-claim webhook (Brief 32) doesn't include photo URLs in its payload, so customer emails are unaffected. The `summary_pdf_url` field (present in both webhooks' payloads) uses a different route (`/claims-api/summary/{claim_id}`) and is correct — only the photo URLs were broken. PA Flow B doesn't need re-editing — it reads the `triggerBody()?['photos']?[*]?['url']` field as-is; fixing the worker-side build is sufficient. **Files modified.** `apps/damage-worker/src/index.ts` (10-line net diff inside `fireInternalNewClaimNotification`), `CLAUDE.md` (`INTERNAL_NEW_CLAIM_WEBHOOK_URL` glossary entry extended with the Brief 104 strip-and-encode paragraph + cross-reference to `damagePhotoUrl`), `BUILD_STATE.md`, `BRIEFS/INDEX.md`. **Validation.** Root `pnpm typecheck` 17/17 green (1 cache miss on @splash/damage-worker, fresh build); `pnpm --filter @splash/damage-worker exec wrangler deploy --dry-run --outdir=.tmp-build` succeeded at 1728.81 KiB raw / 391.10 KiB gzipped (≈ unchanged vs Brief 102 baseline). `.tmp-build` directory cleaned up after run. Operator post-deploy smoke (deferred): submit a test claim via `/claims/{slug}` on workers.dev or staging, wait for the internal email, click any photo link — should now load the image inline (Content-Type from R2 object metadata) instead of "Photo not found". | Claude Code |
