(...) : null}` gate — sibling of the email + role badge `<span>`s. Previously the Change Password button was always rendered when `isAdminContext` was true regardless of `user` prop population; after the demotion the link only renders when `user` is present. Reasoning: the link is a contextual action attached to the role badge ("you are signed in as X — change password here") and the user-less admin-context render is a corner case (dev cross-origin without auth — middleware would redirect in production anyway). Brief 74 inferred the singular form from the heading; the resulting `/attachment/` URL returned 404 routing errors that masqueraded as a missing endpoint. Empirical 2026-05-08: with the plural path, the endpoint exists and responds 200; the MaintainX UI's request-edit panel renders a "Files" section with multiple attached files, confirming multi-attachment is a first-class feature on work requests. **Changes:** (1) `apps/workorders-worker/src/maintainx.ts` — `uploadMaintainXWorkRequestFile` URL builder now uses a small `REQUEST_FILE_URL_SEGMENT` lookup (`thumbnail`→`thumbnail`, `attachment`→`attachments`) so the discriminator type stays `"thumbnail" \| "attachment"` while the URL emits the plural segment. JSDoc updated to call out the plural path so future readers don't repeat the typo. (2) `apps/workorders-worker/src/index.ts` — `POST /workorders/api/request` handler restores the per-photo loop: photo[0] → thumbnail endpoint, photo[1..4] → attachments endpoint; over-5-cap redirects with `?request_error=too_many_photos`; per-photo failure collected into a count surfaced via `?request_ok={id}&photo_warn={N}-of-{M}-photos-failed`. Filename sanitizer gains a `replace(/_+/g, "_")` step plus a trailing-underscore trim before the extension (`download (2).jpg` → `download_2.jpg`). Phone-required check from Brief 75 preserved. (3) `apps/web/app/workorders/_components/NewRequestForm.tsx` — `<input>` regains `multiple`; helper text now reads "Photo(s) (optional, max 5). First photo becomes the thumbnail; additional photos attach to the request." (4) `apps/web/app/workorders/_components/WorkOrdersTabsClient.tsx` — `RequestResultBanner.kind` extended with `"ok-warn"` so the green success banner stacks over a secondary amber `photo_warn` banner when both params are present; `photo_warn` query param read alongside `request_warn` for back-compat with any in-flight tab on the Brief 75 shape. New `photoWarnFriendly` translates `{N}-of-{M}-photos-failed` into "N of M photos failed to upload." (5) Documentation: CLAUDE.md /workorders glossary block — Brief 75 narrative replaced (per the brief's instruction) with the corrected Brief 76 line; INDEX.md row appended; QUEUE.md already had brief-076 row. **Validation:** typecheck + apps/web build + workorders-worker `wrangler deploy --dry-run` all pass. **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** kept Brief 75's `request_warn` query param read alongside the new `photo_warn` for back-compat (1-2 day grace; in-flight tabs on the Brief 75 shape don't break); kept the helper's discriminator type as `"thumbnail" \| "attachment"` (singular) rather than renaming to "attachments" (plural) since the brief explicitly preferred the lookup approach to keep call sites unchanged. **Latent issues:** none surfaced; the per-photo upload still uses sequential `await` (not `Promise.all`), pacing the requests naturally — defer optimization until operators surface real volume issues per the brief's out-of-scope list. | Claude Code |

Most likely root cause: work REQUESTS are pre-approval objects with a limited file surface — only the thumbnail. Once a request is approved into a work order, additional file endpoints (`/v1/workorders/{id}/...`) likely become available. MaintainX's API docs may document an attachment endpoint on work orders that doesn't exist on work requests. **Phase 1 — `apps/web/app/workorders/_components/NewRequestForm.tsx`:** Requester Phone input gains `required` attribute and `FieldRow required` prop (red asterisk follows the same convention as the other 4 required fields); placeholder `"Optional"` removed. Photo `<input>` loses `multiple` attribute; the inline `useState<number>(0)` photo-count tracker, the `MAX_PHOTOS` constant, the alert-and-clear `onChange` cap check, and the dynamic `${photoCount} selected. First photo becomes the work order's thumbnail.` helper string are all deleted; helper text now reads `"Additional photos can be added in MaintainX after the request is approved."` and the field label collapses to `"Photo (optional)"`. `useState` import dropped. **Phase 2 — `apps/workorders-worker/src/index.ts`:** new validation block after the requester-name length check rejects empty `requesterPhone` with `?request_error=requester_phone_required`; existing 30-char cap stays. Multi-photo upload loop deleted. New single-photo path: read `formData.getAll("photo")` (still uses `getAll` for defense-in-depth — operator could still submit via curl with multi-file body), reject any photo > 15 MB, take first and `console.warn` if more than one arrives, then upload via `uploadMaintainXWorkRequestFile({ endpoint: "thumbnail" })` if a file is present. Failure is non-fatal; success surfaces `request_warn=thumbnail_failed`. `REQUEST_MAX_PHOTOS` constant removed. Description footer flips from `Phone: ${requesterPhone || "—"}` to `Phone: ${requesterPhone}` since empty phone never reaches the footer build step now. Per-upload AbortController timeout stays at 15s; total endpoint upper bound shrinks from ~90s (1 create + 5 uploads) to ~30s (1 create + 1 thumbnail). **Phase 3 — `apps/web/app/workorders/_components/WorkOrdersTabsClient.tsx`:** new module-level `REQUEST_ERROR_MESSAGES` map translates `requester_phone_required` → `"Requester phone is required."`; warn-banner branch special-cases `thumbnail_failed` → `"the photo couldn't be uploaded as the request thumbnail"` (vs the generic dash-replacement fallback); copy of the warn banner reflows to "You can attach the photo directly in MaintainX." (singular). **Helper signature preserved:** `uploadMaintainXWorkRequestFile`'s `"thumbnail" \| "attachment"` discriminator stays in `apps/workorders-worker/src/maintainx.ts` exactly as Brief 74 added it. No call site for `endpoint: "attachment"` exists in the codebase post-Brief 75; a future brief that finds (or builds) a working work-request multi-photo endpoint can re-call it without modifying the helper. **Out of scope:** investigating alternate MaintainX endpoints (`/files/`, `/attachments/` plural, etc.); two-step request → approve → attach flow; phone format validation (operator may want a 10-digit regex later). **Validation:** see Outcome section in BRIEFS/brief-075-…md. | Brief 75 |

Multipart form parse with required text fields (`title`, `description`, `priority`, `requester_name`, `location_id`) and optional `requester_phone`. Photos read via `form.getAll("photo")`, capped at 5, validated for size (≤15 MB each) and emptiness. Description composed with footer block: `Requested by: {name}\nPhone: {phone or "—"}\nSubmitted via: Splash /workorders`. On create success, photos uploaded sequentially: first → thumbnail, rest → attachment; per-upload AbortController with 15s timeout; failures non-fatal (counted in `photoFailures`). List response shape extended with `accessibleLocations: AccessibleLocationOut[]` (each entry: `{ maintainx_id, location_address, location_name }` — `location_name` harvested from MX `expand=location.name` on the WO list, null when no WO has yet referenced this loc) and `currentUser: { email, full_name }` (operator's MaintainX `full_name` from the Brief 71 cache; null when no row matches). **Phase 5 — `packages/db-supabase/src/maintainx-users.ts`:** new `getMaintainXUserByEmail(env, email)` helper using PostgREST `email=ilike.<sanitized>` for case-insensitive match (maintainx_users emails are mixed-case from MX, session.email lowercased by Brief 71 gating helpers); fail-soft to null. **Phase 4 — apps/web:** `apps/web/app/workorders/_lib/worker-fetch.ts` extended with `AccessibleLocation` + `WorkOrdersCurrentUser` interfaces and `accessibleLocations` / `currentUser` on the response type. New `apps/web/app/workorders/_components/NewRequestForm.tsx` — plain `<form action="/workorders/api/request" method="POST" enctype="multipart/form-data">` with seven fields (Location dropdown, Title, Priority radios, Description, Requester Name [defaulted to MX full_name], Requester Phone, Photos file input). Client-side max-photos check on file-input change with alert + reset on overflow. `WorkOrdersTabsClient.tsx` extended to three-tab state (`reactive`/`preventive`/`new`); on mount reads URL search params (`?tab=new&request_ok=N` / `&request_error=...` / `&request_warn=...`), forces `tab="new"` when those are present, and clears the params via `history.replaceState` so refresh doesn't resurrect the banner. Three-color banner above the form (green ok / amber partial-photo-fail / red error) with deep link to `https://app.getmaintainx.com/workrequests/{id}` on success. `BucketEmptyState` and `GroupSection` tightened to `tab: "reactive" \| "preventive"` since they're never rendered with `tab="new"`. `apps/web/app/workorders/page.tsx` threads the two new fields through to `WorkOrdersTabsClient`. **Validation:** `pnpm typecheck` green for all 14 packages; `pnpm --filter @splash/web build` green (`/workorders` 5.29 kB / 107 kB First Load JS — +1.83 kB vs Brief 73's 3.46 kB baseline); `pnpm --filter @splash/workorders-worker exec wrangler deploy --dry-run` green (748.05 KiB / 142.38 KiB gzip — ~+30 KiB vs Brief 72/73 baseline for the new helpers + handler). **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (a) MEDIUM color: brief offered `bg-gray-100 text-gray-800` as a spec but said "match existing visual style" — chose Splash brand `bg-gray-light text-splash-navy/80` for consistency with HIGH/LOW/NONE (all use brand classes already; no need to introduce default Tailwind grays). (b) Filename sanitization: 80-char cap with extension preserved (cuts stem rather than extension when over). (c) Per-photo size cap 15 MB (no operator-stated limit; 15 MB matches the damage-document cap precedent). (d) Optional photo upload failures roll forward — request stays created, operator gets a partial-success banner with explicit "re-add manually in MaintainX" copy. (e) `creatorContactInfo` = session email directly (skipped the `maintainx_users` round-trip; both are equivalent and session email is simpler). (f) `request_warn` query param shape = `{N}-of-{M}-photos-failed`; banner replaces hyphens with spaces for display. **Latent issues / forward flags:** (1) MaintainX `POST /v1/workrequests` response shape unverified empirically — the helper tries three envelope shapes (`body.id` / `body.workRequest.id` / `body.data.id`); operator's first live submit will confirm which is canonical. (2) MaintainX `PUT /v1/workrequests/{id}/{endpoint}/{filename}` body shape (`application/octet-stream`) unverified — if it returns 415 or similar, swap to `application/json` with base64 body. (3) Form submit with no client-side spinner — operator clicks submit, browser shows nothing for up to ~90s while photos upload. v2 candidate: client-side submit handler that disables the button + renders a spinner. (4) Form data lost on validation error — page reload clears all inputs since the worker redirects rather than re-rendering the form with prior values; operator retypes. v2 candidate: ?form_state= cookie-based round-trip. (5) Photos field `accept="image/*"` only; brief flags PDF/video/audio support as out-of-scope. (6) No `assetId` selector — operator describes asset in title/description; MX team links post-submit. **Files created:** apps/web/app/workorders/_components/NewRequestForm.tsx. **Files modified:** apps/web/app/workorders/_components/PriorityPill.tsx, apps/web/app/workorders/_components/WorkOrdersTabsClient.tsx, apps/web/app/workorders/_lib/worker-fetch.ts, apps/web/app/workorders/page.tsx, apps/workorders-worker/src/index.ts, apps/workorders-worker/src/maintainx.ts, packages/db-supabase/src/maintainx-users.ts, CLAUDE.md, BUILD_STATE.md, BRIEFS/INDEX.md, BRIEFS/QUEUE.md, BRIEFS/brief-074-workorders-new-request-tab-and-priority-pill-fixes.md. | Claude Code |
| 2026-05-07 | **Brief 73 completed:** Work Orders UX polish — Preventive-tab due-date pill + age-under-priority on both tabs. **Phase 1 — `apps/web/app/workorders/_components/DueDatePill.tsx`:** new client component (`"use client"`) renders four states based on `Math.floor((nowDay - dueDay) / 86_400_000)` where both sides are UTC-day-floored via `Date.UTC(getUTCFullYear, getUTCMonth, getUTCDate)`: `diffDays > 0` → red pill `Overdue Nd` (`bg-red-100 text-red-800`); `diffDays === 0` → amber pill `Due today` (`bg-amber-100 text-amber-800`); `diffDays < 0` → muted plain text `Due MMM D` (`text-xs text-gray-500`); `null`/`NaN` → muted em-dash. Day-floor comparison is intentional — operators think in calendar days, so a WO due at 16:00 UTC today renders "Due today" all day, not "Overdue 0d" once the clock passes 16:00. UTC chosen over local time as the safer default for an org-wide internal tool. Ships with `now?: number` prop default (`Date.now()`) for testability. **Phase 2 — `WorkOrdersTabsClient.tsx`:** import `DueDatePill`; add module-local `ageLabel(iso)` helper that returns `"<1d"` for same-day rows and `"${days}d"` otherwise (Brief 68/69 floor convention). Threaded the active `tab` down through `GroupSection` props so the conditional Due column renders only on Preventive — `<th>Due</th>` and `<td><DueDatePill .../></td>` are wrapped in `tab === "preventive"` ternaries; `colSpan` for `ExpandedRow` widens from 7 to 8 on the Preventive tab. Priority `<td>` now stacks: `<PriorityPill />` on top, `<div className="text-xs text-gray-500 mt-0.5">{ageLabel(wo.createdAt)}</div>` underneath. Renders on BOTH tabs (a 30-day-old preventive that's due tomorrow tells a different story than a same-day one). **Phase 3 — expanded-row cleanup:** dropped the `Age` `<dt>/<dd>` block from the expanded grid (now duplicated by the collapsed-row label); grid collapses from `sm:grid-cols-3` to `sm:grid-cols-2` (Created + Assignees only). Created date stays — more granular than "Nd". Description + Categories blocks unchanged. The dead `daysSince` helper deleted. **Decisions made on the operator's behalf:** (1) Used the conditional-row approach (`{showDueColumn ? <td>…</td> : null}`) over a "always render `<td>`, leave it empty on Reactive" approach — the brief left both options open; conditional row keeps the Reactive table column count at the original 7 (no useless empty column). (2) Threaded `tab` through `GroupSection` rather than re-deriving `showDueColumn` from a context or prop further down — minimum-prop-distance to the row template. (3) Followed Phase 3.1 strictly (drop the Age block) rather than Phase 3.2's "leave it as-is" judgment-call alternative; the duplication was cleanly avoidable and `getMaintainXLocationId`-style "the same number twice" reads more like a bug than a feature. (4) Updated the file's leading docblock to drop the now-stale "expanded rows surface ... age in days" claim and added a Brief-73 paragraph documenting the collapsed-row additions. **Files modified:** `apps/web/app/workorders/_components/DueDatePill.tsx` (new, ~75 lines), `apps/web/app/workorders/_components/WorkOrdersTabsClient.tsx` (threaded `tab` prop, conditional Due column, age-stacked priority cell, expanded-row Age block dropped, `daysSince` removed). **Validation:** `pnpm typecheck` 14/14 green (caught a `verbatimModuleSyntax` error on the initial `ReactElement` import — fixed to `import type { ReactElement }`). `pnpm --filter @splash/web build` succeeds; `/workorders` route at 3.46 kB First Load JS (was 3.12 kB after Brief 72 — delta ~340 bytes). **Latent issues / forward flags:** (a) SSR/CSR hydration mismatch theoretically possible if the server renders at 23:59 UTC and the client renders past 00:00 UTC — `now` prop default is evaluated at render time on each side; brief explicitly accepted this as v1 edge-case noise. (b) If operators eventually want the Reactive tab to surface its own Due column (e.g., MaintainX flow changes so reactive `dueDate` becomes meaningful), the change is one line — drop the `tab === "preventive"` guard. (c) Sorting by due date inside the Preventive tab is out of scope; today the Brief 71 sort (priority desc, then `updatedAt` desc) holds, so an overdue LOW-priority WO can sit below a future-dated MEDIUM. (d) Distinct mobile-card layout still deferred — narrow viewports get the existing horizontal scrollbar from Brief 71. | claude-code |

The page rendered the existing `truncated` banner but the worker wasn't actually walking the cursor to capture the missing rows. **Files modified:** `apps/workorders-worker/src/maintainx.ts` (refactored to a `fetchOnePage` inner helper + a top-level `fetchMaintainXWorkOrders` that branches on `paginate`; new `MAX_PAGE_ITERATIONS = 10` defense-in-depth ceiling with `console.warn` on hit; `nextCursor` extraction now coerces empty string + non-string to `null` consistently; `FetchInput` gains `paginate: boolean` + `maxWorkOrders: number`; `FetchResult` gains `pageCount: number`), `apps/workorders-worker/src/index.ts` (replaced the single `MAINTAINX_TIMEOUT_MS` const with the four Brief-72 module-locals — `MAX_WORK_ORDERS_SINGLE = 200`, `MAX_WORK_ORDERS_MULTI = 1000`, `TIMEOUT_SINGLE_MS = 8_000`, `TIMEOUT_MULTI_MS = 30_000`; `handleList` now computes `shouldPaginate = mappedMxIds.length > 1` and passes the corresponding cap + timeout; one-line `console.log` of email / mappedMxIds / paginate / pageCount / workOrders / truncated added below the bucketing block for ops visibility; `ListResponse` shape gains `pageCount: number` and the early-return path for `mappedMxIds.length === 0` returns `pageCount: 0`), `apps/web/app/workorders/_lib/worker-fetch.ts` (`WorkOrdersListResponse` type extended with `pageCount: number`), `apps/web/app/workorders/_components/WorkOrdersTabsClient.tsx` (`TruncatedNotice` copy reworded to "Showing first 1000 work orders. Older items aren't visible here — log into MaintainX directly for the full list." with the soft amber styling per brief — `bg-amber-50 text-amber-900 border border-amber-200 rounded-md px-4 py-2 text-sm` — replacing the former Brief 71 sudsy-blue treatment that read "Showing the first 200 work orders.…"). Worker log is `[observability.logs]`-covered (Brief 63) so the per-list-call line surfaces in the Workers Logs view automatically. **Decisions made on the operator's behalf:** (a) followed the brief's exact banner copy verbatim including the "1000" figure even though single-location truncation (implausible — would require >200 open WOs at one site) would still surface that copy; the alternative of parameterizing the figure on `pageCount`/cap added code without operator-visible benefit. (b) Skipped the optional Phase 3.2 footer surface ("Fetched {pageCount} pages from MaintainX") — the value is already on the response shape and Workers Logs has the same data; surfacing it under the "As of …" timestamp would be operator noise on the dominant single-call path. (c) Page handler still passes only `truncated` (not `pageCount`) to `WorkOrdersTabsClient` — the type was extended on the response shape per the brief, but no UI consumes it; future visibility addition is one line in `Body`. (d) The `nextCursor` extraction was tightened — now requires a non-empty string before treating it as a cursor (the previous shape accepted any truthy non-null value). MaintainX's documented field is a string per the API sample, so this is conservative correctness, not a behavior change. **Latent issues / forward flags:** (i) The `truncated=true` case where pagination ran but didn't reach 1000 (i.e., `MAX_PAGE_ITERATIONS=10` was hit while cursor remained non-null) is technically possible if MaintainX returns a buggy cursor that loops or the queue exceeds 2000 WOs; the helper console.warns and force-breaks but the page can't distinguish "hit the 1000 cap" from "hit the 10-iteration ceiling" — both render the same banner. Acceptable per the brief's defense-in-depth posture. (ii) No live API calls were made against MaintainX from headless to validate the cursor contract; the brief's Report section asks for empirical observation but the operator-side smoke test (Phase 4.5) is the validation gate. (iii) The `console.log` line adds one log entry per `/workorders` page load; at expected scale (a handful of operators, a handful of loads/day) this is fine, but if `/workorders` ever gains a polling client this should drop to a `console.debug` or sample. **Validation:** `pnpm typecheck` passes 14/14 packages (12 cached + 2 fresh: workorders-worker, web). `pnpm --filter @splash/web build` succeeds; `/workorders` route bundle is 3.12 kB / 105 kB First Load JS (no observable delta from Brief 71). `pnpm --filter @splash/workorders-worker exec wrangler deploy --dry-run` succeeds: 732.31 KiB uncompressed / 139.37 KiB gzip total upload (well within Cloudflare's 3 MiB compressed limit). No new bindings, no new env vars, no new secrets. | Claude Code |

new `[triggers] crons = ["30 11 * * *"]` block — 11:30 UTC, before damage-worker's 13:00 UTC summary cron), `apps/web/app/workorders/_lib/worker-fetch.ts` (response-shape types regenerated to match the new server shape; old `WorkOrdersGroup.location_code` / `UnmatchedWorkOrder` / `WorkOrdersListResponse.scope` / `missingMaintainxIds` / `unmatchedWorkOrders` removed; new `WorkOrdersBucket` interface + `accessibleLocationCount` / `mappedLocationCount` / `email` fields), `apps/web/app/workorders/page.tsx` (rewritten as a thin shell — fetches via `fetchWorkOrdersList`, hands both buckets to `WorkOrdersTabsClient`; `IN_PROGRESS` rendered with `bg-amber-100 text-amber-900` for sharper visual distinction; header docblock extended to call out the Brief 71 fix), `CLAUDE.md` (new constraint #8 covering the `maintainx_users` / `maintainx_teams` read-only-from-worker-code-except-sync rule; Workorders-worker endpoints entry rewritten to reflect email-on-locations gate + new `POST /sync-maintainx-users` endpoint + `{ fetch, scheduled }` shape + Reactive/Preventive bucketing + group-header preference of MX `expand=location.name` with fallback to `locations.location`; Glossary "Work Orders" entry rewritten to remove dc_role and document the Brief 71 surfaces), `BRIEFS/INDEX.md` (Brief 71 row appended), `BRIEFS/QUEUE.md` (Brief 71 line moved to completed-tombstone block), `BUILD_STATE.md` (this entry + prioritized work list row + Last-updated bump). **Files deleted:** `apps/web/app/workorders/_components/EmptyState.tsx` (Brief 70 component, no longer imported — `WorkOrdersTabsClient` owns its own no-access / no-bucket empty states inline). **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (1) **Manual sync trigger gating:** new `POST /workorders/api/sync-maintainx-users` endpoint accepts the operator-defined hardcoded email allow-list (Josh, Noah, Alexandro, Jacob, rwilliams — the super_admin list called out in CLAUDE.md "operator preferences") AND falls back to `session.dcRole === "super_admin"` to keep CLAUDE.md "operator preferences" the single source of truth without breaking if the hardcoded list drifts from reality. Per the brief: "If that gate is too rigid, fall back to checking `session.dcRole === 'super_admin'` only." (2) **Group header preference:** MaintainX's `expand=location.name` first, then Splash-side `locations.location` (postal address) as the fallback, then "(unknown location)" if neither is available. The brief said "MaintainX's name preferred"; I added the postal-address fallback rather than letting "(unknown location)" appear when MX returns location.id but no name. (3) **Header upgrade pass within `groupByLocation`:** if a later WO in the same MX-location bucket carries a non-null MX-side name (and the bucket header was set to the postal-address fallback or "(unknown location)"), upgrade the header. (4) **`bucketByType` PREVENTIVE-only filter:** strictly checks `wo.type === "PREVENTIVE"` per the brief. Comment block calls out the canonical filter and warns the next reader that adding `type?.startsWith("PREVENT")` is the upgrade path if MX adds e.g. "PREVENTIVE_DAILY". (5) **`getLocationsByContactEmail` uses `eq` not `ilike`:** West Haven sample (`am_email = "jfrank@splashcarwashes.com"`) is already lowercase; `eq` is sufficient for current data per the brief's spot-check note. Switch path documented in the helper's docblock. (6) **`matched_via` priority order am → rm → site:** mirrors the brief's spec (operator can change later if it surfaces). (7) **Sync endpoint pagination:** 100-page protective cap (= 20k upper bound at 200/page); MAX_PAGES safety to prevent infinite loops if MX returns an unexpected envelope shape. (8) **`SyncResult.errors[]`** carries every per-page / per-batch error so the operator can grep `[scheduled]` log lines for issue diagnosis without reading every payload. (9) **`upsertBatch` uses `Prefer: resolution=merge-duplicates,return=minimal`** so the response body is empty (faster + smaller logs); status 201 / 200 indicates success. (10) **`assignees[].type` on raw WO body** is read directly without an `expand` parameter — matches the operator's MX sample where `type` is on the assignee body without an expand. (11) **`WorkOrdersTabsClient` keeps the `expanded` set across tab flips** per the brief — keys are `String(wo.id)` and unique across both buckets. (12) **`StatusPill` lookup is now case-insensitive** (`.trim().toUpperCase()`) — defense-in-depth against MX returning `"in_progress"` or `"In Progress"` or otherwise case-shifted; the original Brief 70 implementation already used the same uppercase keys but a case-mismatch on the input would have rendered without a pill. (13) **No `Set DC Role` reference in the no-access copy** — the page no longer cares about dc_role; the copy points at the locations editor (sysadmin Update Location, Brief 27) which is the canonical fix path for both "missing email" and "missing maintainx_id" failures. (14) **`ExpandedRow` shows description even when null** ("(no description)" placeholder) — the row is open because the operator wants the detail; an empty drawer is a worse UX than acknowledging the absence. (15) **Categories rendered as small badges** in the expanded drawer rather than as a separate column — keeps the table row scannable while making the data accessible on demand. **Latent issues / forward flags:** (a) **Operator must run Phase 4.1 SQL before deploy** to create `maintainx_users` and `maintainx_teams` Supabase tables — additive, no impact on existing reads, but the read endpoint will silently render `User #N` / `Team #N` for every assignee until the tables exist + first sync runs. (b) **First-deploy operator action:** hit `POST /workorders/api/sync-maintainx-users` once via curl + super_admin cookie immediately after the new code lands, otherwise the first 24h of `/workorders` views show synthetic `User #N` placeholders until the 11:30 UTC cron fires. (c) **MX cursor pagination empirically untested in headless** — the sync helper handles `nextCursor` and the URL-based `nextPageUrl` envelope shape, but the actual MX response shape isn't formally locked in repo yet. If MX returns an unexpected envelope the sync stops on page 0 and surfaces a clear `"users page 0 returned ${status}: ${body}"` error. (d) **`assignees[].email` on the WO list response** is a defense-in-depth bonus — the page renders names today, but a future row-action surface (e.g., "email this assignee") could consume it directly without another sync round. (e) **`UserAccessibleLocation.matched_via`** is unused by v1 rendering but persisted in the wire shape for an audit/debug surface later (e.g., "this user sees Williamsville because their email matches `rm_email`"). (f) **No `accessibleLocationCount` < `mappedLocationCount` empty-state** — if the user has 5 accessible locations but 0 maintainx_id mappings, they see the same "no work orders to show" copy. The copy explicitly distinguishes "your email isn't on a location" from "your locations aren't yet mapped to MaintainX" so the user can self-diagnose. (g) **The Brief 70 `getMaintainXLocationId` forward helper is unchanged** and still consumed by damage-worker's WO-create path — the deletion was strictly the BULK helpers from Brief 70. (h) **`expand=thumbnail` dropped without breaking anything** — Brief 70's `projectThumbnail` helper had been emitting `thumbnailUrl` in the response shape, but apps/web's page never rendered it; the entire shape field is gone from `WorkOrderItem` now. Re-add when operators ask for thumbnails. (i) **`isSyncTriggerAllowed` falls back to `session.dcRole`** — that field is still populated and valid even though the rest of the read path no longer consumes it; treating it as a one-off admin fence here matches the brief's recommendation. (j) **Sync endpoint runs synchronously inside the request** — the upstream MX iteration takes O(seconds) per phase; CF Workers' default request timeout (30s on free / configurable on paid) is a hard ceiling. The 30s `PHASE_TIMEOUT_MS` per phase + 100-page protective cap keep total wall-clock under the request limit even with two phases (users + teams). (k) **Scheduled handler uses `ctx.waitUntil`** so the sync completes even if the cron-event loop returns; matches damage-worker post-Brief 65. (l) **`bucketByType` rule is in code, not config** — adding new MX types is a code change. The comment block flags the upgrade path. (m) **`StatusPill` defaults to ON_HOLD styling on unknown statuses** — same as Brief 70; if MX adds a new active status (unlikely; the four "active" statuses are stable), the new value renders with gray pill + raw label until the helper is extended. **Validation:** `pnpm typecheck` 14/14 packages green; `pnpm --filter @splash/web build` succeeded; `pnpm --filter @splash/workorders-worker exec wrangler deploy --dry-run --outdir=.tmp-build-wo` succeeded with the new `[triggers]` block parsed cleanly. **Operator follow-up / action items:** (i) **Run the Phase 4.1 SQL** in Supabase to create the two new tables (additive — no impact on existing reads). (ii) **Hit the manual sync endpoint** once via curl + super_admin cookie immediately after the code deploys: `Invoke-WebRequest -Uri "https://splash-workorders.<acct>.workers.dev/workorders/api/sync-maintainx-users" -Method POST -Headers @{ Cookie = "sb-access-token=<DevTools value>" }` — populates `maintainx_users` / `maintainx_teams` so assignee names render on the first `/workorders` load. (iii) **Smoke test** the page as a user with email on at least one `am_email` / `rm_email` / `site_email` — confirm Reactive tab populates with grouped + sorted results, click a row to expand and confirm description + created date + age, flip to Preventive tab and confirm the WOs there are correctly bucketed. (iv) **Confirm the Brief 70 zero-padding diagnosis is sidestepped** — West Haven (or any site whose `pricing_simple.site` is zero-padded) should now appear correctly grouped. (v) **Document any unexpected `wo.type` values** in production for follow-up consideration — REACTIVE / PREVENTIVE / CYCLE_COUNT are the three known values; anything else lands in the Reactive bucket today. | Claude Code |

Fail modes: `MAINTAINX_API_KEY` unbound → 503 (page surfaces "integration not configured"), MaintainX non-2xx → 502, network/abort → 504, helper exception → 500. Response shape includes `scope`, `missingMaintainxIds`, `groups[]` (each with `location_code` / `location_pretty` / `maintainx_id` / `work_orders[]` sorted by priority HIGH→MEDIUM→LOW→NONE then `updatedAt` desc), `unmatchedWorkOrders[]` (global only), `truncated` flag (set when MaintainX returns a non-null cursor — page surfaces a soft "showing first 200" banner; v2 candidate to follow the cursor), and a server `fetchedAt` timestamp. Helper never throws. **Phase 5 — `packages/db-supabase/src/locations.ts`:** two new bulk helpers added at end of file. `getMaintainXIdsForLocationCodes(env, codes[])` does the same two-step pricing_simple → site → locations chain as Brief 62's `getMaintainXLocationId`, but in two PostgREST GETs total (IN-clauses with deduped distinct values), preserving caller's input set in the result. `getLocationCodesByMaintainXIds(env, mxIds[])` does the reverse direction — `locations.maintainx_id IN (...)` → site_numbers → `pricing_simple.site IN (...)` → location_code/pretty — and returns a `Map<number, MaintainXLocationInfo>`. Both fail-soft (any throw / non-2xx returns sentinel empty result). New `MaintainXLocationInfo` interface and a private `escapeForInClause` helper (defensive double-quoting on PostgREST IN-clause values; sanitized inputs already pass the location_code regex but the quote provides defense-in-depth). Returns a discriminated union (`ok | denied | not_configured | error`) so the page can render distinct UX for each branch. **Phase 8 — page:** `apps/web/app/workorders/page.tsx` is a force-dynamic server component. Renders inline gated message on 401/403, "integration not configured" copy on 503, generic error card on other non-2xx, EmptyState when no WOs match, otherwise per-group `<table>` with PriorityPill/StatusPill/relative-time formatter. Soft-warning banner when `missingMaintainxIds.length > 0`. Truncated banner when `data.truncated`. Global-scope users also get an `<UnmatchedSection>` footer block. No client islands required for v1 — relative-time is a server-rendered helper (`formatRelativeTime`). **Phase 9 — components:** `_components/PriorityPill.tsx` (4 states: HIGH=red, MEDIUM=yellow, LOW=blue, NONE=muted; same rounded-full pill shape as Brief 68/69's AgePill), `_components/StatusPill.tsx` (3 states: OPEN=blue, IN_PROGRESS=yellow, ON_HOLD=gray), `_components/EmptyState.tsx` ("No open work orders for your locations" + link out to MaintainX). **Phase 10 — dashboard tile:** new fifth tile in `apps/web/app/admin/dashboard/page.tsx`'s TILES array (`href: "/workorders"`, eyebrow "MaintainX", title "Work Orders") using a wrench-shaped SVG. **Phase 11 — CLAUDE.md updates:** intro block now mentions six workers; new entry documenting workorders-worker endpoints, fail modes, and the bulk Supabase helper pattern; apps/web Real-pages list extended with `/workorders` (top-level, NOT under /admin/*); new Glossary entry "Work Orders". **Validation:** `pnpm typecheck` 14/14 packages green (one cache-miss after fixing a `noUncheckedIndexedAccess` lookup default in `priorityRank`); `pnpm --filter @splash/web build` succeeded — `/workorders` route appears in the manifest at 131 B / 102 kB First Load JS (no client weight); `pnpm --filter @splash/workorders-worker exec wrangler deploy --dry-run --outdir=.tmp-build-wo` succeeded — bundle 724.79 KiB / gzip 137.59 KiB; `pnpm --filter @splash/damage-worker exec wrangler deploy --dry-run --outdir=.tmp-build-d` still succeeds at 1720.07 KiB / 389.39 KiB gzip (no damage-worker code changed; sanity check). **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (1) **`SUPABASE_URL` documented as a wrangler secret rather than a `[vars]` literal** — none of the existing five workers carry it as a plaintext `[vars]` entry, so the new worker matches that pattern. The brief said "copy from apps/damage-worker/wrangler.toml" but the value isn't there; operator binds it via `wrangler secret put` (or via the CF dashboard) at deploy time. (2) **`workOrderScopeForSession` duplicated, not extracted** — the brief explicitly delegated this judgment call; six-line helper in domain-isolated workers, lifting to a package would couple them on a stable API for marginal gain. (3) **Description truncation = 500 chars** per the brief; uses an ellipsis suffix only when over the cap. (4) **Empty `dc_locations` for a gm/rm returns 200 with empty groups, not 403** — operator can grant locations later without the page short-circuiting on a misleading "no access" message. (5) **Truncated handling = surface flag, not chase pagination** — v1 doesn't follow MaintainX's nextCursor; the page banner tells operators to log into MaintainX directly for the full list when expected ≤200 WOs is exceeded. Pagination flagged as a follow-up brief if `truncated` ever fires in production. (6) **PriorityPill / StatusPill copy:** "—" for NONE priority, "On Hold" / "In Progress" for the multi-word statuses; matches MaintainX's own labeling conventions. (7) **Unmapped MaintainX IDs surfaced for global users only** — scoped users already pre-filter by `locations=` query param, so any drift would be a defense-in-depth catch (not user-actionable); only super_admin / admin's full-org view benefits from the data-hygiene visibility. (8) **No `PRE_DEPLOY_WORKORDERS.md` written** — brief leaves the choice to executor judgment; the existing per-worker PRE_DEPLOY_*.md files are largely smoke-test runbooks tied to historical legacy parity (Brief 51's PRE_DEPLOY_WEB.md was the explicit ask). Worker's surface is a single GET endpoint; smoke testing is "navigate to /workorders, verify the table renders for the operator's dc_role". A stub PRE_DEPLOY_WORKORDERS.md is a small follow-up if cutover-day discipline benefits from it. **Latent issues / forward flags:** (a) **Operator must configure CF Workers Builds for the new worker** before the first auto-deploy from the GH push — splash-workorders doesn't exist in the CF dashboard yet; first-time setup is "create new Worker → connect to GitHub repo → set build dir to apps/workorders-worker → deploy". (b) **Operator must bind 3 secrets on splash-workorders** post-create: `MAINTAINX_API_KEY` (same value as on splash-damage), `SUPABASE_SERVICE_KEY` (same value as on splash-damage / splash-sysadmin), `SUPABASE_URL` (same project URL — non-secret but bound the same way the other workers do). `SUPABASE_ANON_KEY` is also required by `authenticate()`'s /auth/v1/user round-trip (mirrors the other workers' deploy-time secret binding). (c) **MaintainX list endpoint response shape isn't formally locked** in this repo yet — `extractWorkOrders` tries multiple envelope shapes; the empirical probe deferred to operator (headless cannot exercise live MaintainX). If MaintainX's list shape is, say, `{ workOrders: [...], pagination: {...} }`, the helper picks it up automatically; if it's a different shape, edit `extractWorkOrders`. (d) **MaintainX assignee object shape unknown** — `expand=assignees` returns assignee objects whose name fields could be `firstName`/`lastName`, `fullName`, or something else; the projection tries `fullName` first, falls back to `firstName + lastName`, then `User #<id>`. (e) **No global-scope unmapped warning UX for scoped users** — by design; if a scoped user's dc_locations include a location whose maintainx_id is null, they see the soft warning, but unmatched MX WOs (locations not in their dc_locations at all) won't appear regardless of the global pretransform. (f) **Email-on-locations gating intentionally skipped** per operator's 2026-05-07 decision — operators who own a location's contact emails but were never granted a `dc_role` see an empty page; sysadmin Set DC Role tool (Brief 61) is the canonical remediation. **Operator follow-up / action items:** (i) Create the `splash-workorders` Worker on CF (Workers Builds wired to the GitHub repo), bind the 4 secrets, push to deploy. (ii) After first deploy, navigate to `https://splash-web.<account>.workers.dev/workorders` (cookie-authed) and confirm the page renders for various dc_roles (super_admin sees global; gm/rm sees scoped; user with no dc_role sees the gated message). (iii) When ready for cutover, uncomment the production routes block in `apps/workorders-worker/wrangler.toml` and bind `splashcarwashes.info/workorders/api/*` alongside the existing per-worker bindings. | Claude Code |
| 2026-05-07 | **Brief 69 completed:** Age pill — collapse Brief 68's four-tier curve to a two-tier curve per operator's 2026-05-07 review. **One-file change:** `apps/web/app/admin/damage/_components/AgePill.tsx`. **Tier model:** `Tier` type narrowed from `"neutral" | "amber" | "orange" | "red"` to `"neutral" | "yellow" | "red"`. `TIER_CLASSES` map drops `amber` and `orange` keys; `amber`'s slot is renamed to `yellow` with the same Tailwind classes (`bg-yellow-100 text-yellow-900`) — purely a key rename for clarity, no visual change at the 4-7 day band. `tierFor` ternary collapsed: `≥15 red / ≥8 orange / ≥4 amber / neutral` → `≥11 red / ≥4 yellow / neutral`. **Net effect on existing claims:** 0-3d still neutral; 4-7d still yellow (was amber, same color); 8-10d **flipped from orange to yellow** (these were the brightest non-red tier under Brief 68, are now the upper band of yellow); 11-14d **flipped from orange to red** (new red threshold); 15+d still red. **Brief 68 behaviors preserved verbatim:** lifecycle gating (Closed renders static muted neutral); pill shape/font/position (`inline-flex items-center rounded-full px-2 py-0.5 text-[11px] font-semibold`); title attribute copy (`${days} day(s) since submission` for Open, `Closed; ${days} day(s) from submission to last status change` for Closed); `<1d` label for `age_days === 0`; `Math.max(0, Math.trunc(ageDays))` defensive sanitation. Header docblock extended with a Brief 69 paragraph explaining the curve change so the next reader doesn't "restore" the orange tier. **Why two-tier:** operator confirmed 2026-05-07 — fewer color changes makes the urgency signal clearer at a glance; the legacy three-band (green/yellow/red) was already a two-tier-of-warning shape, and the four-band Brief 68 escalation introduced a band (orange) that the operator's eye treated as a duplicate of red without adding actionable signal. Yellow at >3d / red at >10d maps cleanly to "still in normal quote-gathering window" / "needs escalation". **Validation:** `pnpm typecheck` (13/13 packages green; only @splash/web cache-missed since it's the touched package), `pnpm --filter @splash/web build` (route `/admin/damage` still 169 B First Load JS — unchanged from Brief 68 baseline; the AgePill component compiles to the same RSC payload size, classnames are static strings). No worker change, no API change, no schema change, no env vars. **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (1) Kept Brief 68's named `tierFor` helper instead of inlining the ternary into `AgePill` per the brief's `const tier = … ? "red" : …` example — the helper's three early-return branches are easier to grep/tune for the next operator who wants to shift thresholds. Single-edit point preserved. (2) Header docblock rewritten rather than appended: the now-stale "Thresholds (4 / 8 / 15 days)" line would mislead anyone reading the comment, so it's replaced by a Brief 69 paragraph covering the two-tier rationale. **Latent flag:** the daily-summary email (Brief 65) sends raw `age_days` integers in its JSON payload; if the operator wants matching color bands in the email body, that's a Power Automate flow edit (out of scope here per the brief). **Operator follow-up:** navigate `/admin/damage`, confirm Open-lifecycle claims aged 11+ days now render red (claims that were orange under Brief 68's 8-14 band are now split — 8-10d yellow, 11-14d red). | Claude Code |
| 2026-05-07 | **Brief 68 completed:** Age pill on `/admin/damage` claims list (legacy parity). **Phase 1 — `packages/db-d1/src/claims.ts`:** `CLAIMS_LIST_COLS` extended with `CAST((julianday('now') - julianday(submitted_at)) AS INTEGER) AS age_days`; new exported `ClaimsListRow` type alias (`Pick<ClaimRow, …> & { age_days: number }`) replaces the previous inline-Pick return type on `listClaims`. Header docblock above the constant explains why on-the-fly is correct — generated columns require deterministic expressions and `julianday('now')` is non-deterministic, and a stored column would need a daily refresh cron for zero benefit at our scale (single arithmetic op per row at query time, negligible at hundreds-of-claims scale). **Phase 2 — damage-worker:** zero-change pass-through; `getClaimsList` already does `json(claims)` so `age_days` rides on the JSON response. Verified the `/manage/api/claims` consumer in apps/web is the only consumer; no other re-declared row type to update. **Phase 3 — apps/web:** new `apps/web/app/admin/damage/_components/AgePill.tsx` (single `<AgePill ageDays lifecycle>` export, `<span>` rounded-full pill, four-tier `tierFor` helper at thresholds 4 / 8 / 15 days; Open = colored escalation, Closed = static neutral so the column stays aligned without escalating terminal claims). Tier classes use existing splash tokens where they exist (`bg-gray-light/60 text-splash-navy/70` for neutral, `bg-splash-deny/20 text-splash-deny` for red) and standard Tailwind palette for amber/orange (`bg-yellow-100 text-yellow-900`, `bg-orange-100 text-orange-900`) — no token-set additions needed. `apps/web/app/admin/damage/page.tsx`: local `ClaimListRow` type extended with `& { age_days: number }`; the Submitted column cell wraps the date + pill in a `flex items-center gap-2` so the pill sits to the right of the YYYY-MM-DD text (Option B from the brief — append rather than add a column, keeps table column count stable and matches legacy layout). Pill carries a `title` attr with absolute day count for hover. Detail page intentionally untouched per Phase 3.4 (list-page glance is the operator's primary use case). **Why:** legacy `legacy/damagemanager.js:2901 renderAgeBadge` rendered a color-escalating pill on the manager's claims overview; the new monorepo port shipped only the YYYY-MM-DD date, forcing operators to mentally diff against today to spot stuck claims. Brief 68 restores the "this one's been sitting two weeks" visual scan. **Validation:** `pnpm typecheck` (13/13 packages green), `pnpm --filter @splash/damage-worker exec wrangler deploy --dry-run --outdir=.tmp-build-brief68` (1720.07 KiB raw / 389.39 KiB gzipped bundle, identical structure to Brief 67's bundle modulo the SELECT projection extension; cleaned up after), `pnpm --filter @splash/web build` (route `/admin/damage` 169 B First Load JS unchanged from baseline; the AgePill component compiles into the same RSC payload as the existing list cells). **Color thresholds:** chosen per the brief (4 / 8 / 15 days for amber / orange / red), not legacy's 3 / 7 (legacy used green / yellow / red at ≤2 / ≤6 / 7+). The brief's curve gives a slightly more permissive "amber" zone for typical multi-day quote-gathering and pushes "red" out to the two-week mark where finance escalation would normally have already kicked in. Operator can tune by editing the `tierFor` constants in `AgePill.tsx`. **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (1) Closed-claim pill chosen as muted neutral (rather than skipping entirely) so the table column doesn't go uneven across mixed Open/Closed result sets; (2) "<1d" label for `age_days === 0` mirroring legacy's `Math.floor(...)` 0-day handling; (3) `Math.max(0, Math.trunc(ageDays))` defensive sanitation in case D1 ever returns a fractional or negative value (clock skew on submitted_at vs julianday('now') boundary). **Latent flag:** apps/web does not depend on `@splash/db-d1` (it's a worker-only package), so `ClaimsListRow` is duplicated in apps/web as a local intersection type rather than imported. Keep `CLAIMS_LIST_COLS` and apps/web's `ClaimListRow` aligned — same convention used by Brief 5a's original Pick<>. **Operator follow-up:** navigate `/admin/damage`, confirm pills render with correct color tiers; tune thresholds in `apps/web/app/admin/damage/_components/AgePill.tsx` if 4/8/15 isn't the right escalation curve. | Claude Code |
| 2026-05-07 | **Brief 67 completed:** Reporting page (`/admin/damage/reporting`) gains four enhancements operator flagged on 2026-05-07: (1) Cost column on the global Approved-by-damage-type table — `byDamageTypeApprovedSql` rewritten as a JOIN-based variant (`LEFT JOIN claim_photos … AND cp.deleted_at IS NULL`, `COUNT(DISTINCT c.claim_id)`, `COALESCE(SUM(CASE WHEN photo_type IN ('Quote','Receipt') AND amount IS NOT NULL THEN amount END), 0) AS cost`); (2) per-location drill-down via inline expandable rows — new `byLocationDrilldownSql` returns one row per (location_code, outcome_bucket, damage_type) using a 5-bucket CASE (`open / denied / approved / closed_approved / closed_other`); apps/web `_components/ByLocationTableClient.tsx` (`"use client"`, `useState<Set<string>>` keyed by location_code, mirrors Brief 53's audit-log pattern) renders four damage-type breakdown panels per row (Open / Closed / Approved-with-cost / Denied), aggregating `denied + closed_approved + closed_other` for the Closed view and `approved + closed_approved` for the Approved view; (3) Avg Days Open column on the By Location table between Denied and Cost — new `byLocationAvgDaysOpenSql` uses `AVG(julianday('now') - julianday(submitted_at))` filtered to `lifecycle_state = 'Open' AND deleted_at IS NULL`, rendered as `${Math.round}d` or `—` when null; (4) footer reworded to drop the v2 double-count limitation disclaimer per operator's 2026-05-07 decision that summing approved quotes + receipts is correct framing (receipts paid out + quotes committed = total exposure). Response shape extended: `by_location[].avg_days_open: number \| null`, `by_damage_type_approved[].cost: number`, new top-level `by_location_drilldown` array. D1 batch grew from 11 to 13 statements (same `baseBindings`). `DamageTypeTable` helper gains optional `showCost` prop. No schema change, no new env vars, no new endpoints. **Why:** operator's 2026-05-07 review of the Reporting page identified four gaps blocking pre-cutover sign-off — quote-amount visibility per damage type, per-location drill-down without filter changes, claim-age signal for stuck open claims, and corrected cost-framing copy. **Validation:** `pnpm typecheck` (13/13 packages), `pnpm --filter @splash/web build` (route `/admin/damage/reporting` 1.59 kB First Load JS), `pnpm --filter @splash/damage-worker exec wrangler deploy --dry-run` (1719.99 KiB raw / 389.35 KiB gzipped bundle, well under 3 MiB free-tier ceiling). **Decision:** chose 5-bucket SQL over 4-bucket — gives renderer exact control over Approved (`approved + closed_approved`) and Closed (`denied + closed_approved + closed_other`) views, matching the per-location table's column semantics. **Latent flag:** Closed drill-down panel intentionally overlaps with Approved + Denied panels (`closed_approved` in both Approved and Closed; `denied` in both Denied and Closed) — operator explicitly requested this redundancy ("yes, redundant by design"); future v2 candidate if stricter mutual-exclusion becomes useful. **Operator follow-up:** navigate `/admin/damage/reporting`, confirm new columns + drill-down render; spot-check global Approved-by-damage-type cost matches sum of per-location Approved drill-down costs. | Claude Code |
| 2026-05-07 | **Brief 66 completed:** RM revert paths from `Approved — Pending Quotes` and `Pending RM Quote Approval`. **Why:** operator confirmed 2026-05-07 (testing as RM dc_role across cortland/cicero/leray/binghamton/oswego) that an RM reviewing a GM-approved claim could not send it back without admin escalation — three "send back" buttons rendered greyed out with "Requires admin or higher". Use case: GM erroneously approves a claim, RM catches it during quote-gathering, RM needs to bounce it back to GM Review, RM Review, or deny outright. **Worker `apps/damage-worker/src/transitions.ts` (canonical state machine):** three existing rows in the "Admin escape hatches" block widened from `role: "admin"` → `role: "rm"` — `Approved — Pending Quotes → Pending GM Review`, `Approved — Pending Quotes → Pending RM Review`, `Pending RM Quote Approval → Pending GM Review`. Two new RM-allowed transitions added — `Approved — Pending Quotes → Closed — Denied` (positioned in the "From `Approved — Pending Quotes`" per-status block, after the existing two forward transitions) and `Pending RM Quote Approval → Pending RM Review` (positioned in the "From `Pending RM Quote Approval`" per-status block, after the existing forward + Closed — Denied transitions). All five send-back paths carry `requiresNote: true` + `clearApprovalDetails: true` to preserve Brief 20's invariant — the worker NULLs `approved_amount` / `approved_quote_id` / `parts_ordered` / `vendor_name` and resets gm/rm/ceo audit stamps as part of the same UPDATE so the detail page's Approval Details box doesn't render stale data on the reverted claim. The "Admin escape hatches" docblock extended with a Brief 66 note explaining that three rows shifted to RM per the 2026-05-07 operator decision; the remaining admin-only entries in that block (Parts Ordered → Pending GM Review, Submitted for Payment ↔ Check Request Submitted / Pending RM Quote Approval, Check Issued → Check Request Submitted, plus the `Pending RM Quote Approval → Approved — Pending Quotes` forward-revert) stay admin-only. **apps/web mirror `apps/web/app/admin/damage/_lib/transitions.ts`:** five matching edits to keep structural parity with the worker. The three widened entries kept their position in the apps/web "Admin escape hatches" section but lose the `(admin)` suffix on their labels — `Send back to GM Review (admin)` → `Send back to GM Review`, `Send back to RM Review (admin)` → `Send back to RM Review`. The two new entries landed in the per-status forward blocks: `Close — Denied` (label) for `Approved — Pending Quotes → Closed — Denied`, and `Send back to RM Review` (label) for `Pending RM Quote Approval → Pending RM Review`. Comment blocks added at every Brief 66 edit site cross-referencing the operator decision date and the worker file. **What stays admin-only:** the `Pending RM Quote Approval → Approved — Pending Quotes` path (forward step past where RM might want to revert from), every post-approval / finance-touched revert (`Approved — In House — Parts Ordered → Pending GM Review`, `Approved — In House — Repaired ↔ Approved — In House — Parts Ordered`, `Approved — Check Request Submitted → Pending RM Quote Approval`, `Approved — Submitted for Payment ↔ Approved — Check Request Submitted` / `Pending RM Quote Approval`, `Approved — Check Issued → Approved — Check Request Submitted`), and every `Closed —` state reopen. Admin oversight on those still warranted because work is in flight or finance has touched the row. **Validation:** `pnpm typecheck` 13/13 ✓; `pnpm --filter @splash/damage-worker exec wrangler deploy --dry-run --outdir=.tmp-build` bundles cleanly at 1717.17 KiB / gzip 388.88 KiB (~+2 KiB over Brief 65 baseline from the added comments + two new transitions; `.tmp-build` cleaned up post-run); `pnpm --filter @splash/web build` 13/13 routes ✓. **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (a) the three widened transitions stayed in the worker's "Admin escape hatches" block rather than relocating to the per-status forward blocks — moving them would split the comment block documenting the `clearApprovalDetails` rationale; the block docblock now explicitly notes that three rows are RM-allowed per Brief 66 with cross-reference to the operator decision date. (b) the apps/web mirror followed the same positioning to keep diff-friendly side-by-side review with the worker file. Side-by-side post-edit confirmation: every from/to pair has identical (role, requiresNote, clearApprovalDetails) values across the two files. **Out of scope (deferred):** widening RM access on the post-approval chain (Parts Ordered, Repaired, Check Request Submitted, Submitted for Payment, Check Issued); reopens from any `Closed —` state; new RM revert paths for `Closed — Approved/No Response`; GM revert access on Approved-family states. **Operator follow-up:** re-test the same RM user (4309ed6f-95f6-4927-b8e6-715d9aca5f95) on a claim in `Approved — Pending Quotes`; confirm Send back to GM Review / Send back to RM Review / Close — Denied render enabled (and the `Pending RM Quote Approval → Pending RM Review` button on appropriate-state claims). | Claude Code |
| 2026-05-07 | **Brief 65 completed:** Daily open-claims summary cron — bolted onto damage-worker as a sibling `scheduled` handler on the default export. **Why bolt-on (not a new worker):** all bindings (`env.DB`, Supabase env, MaintainX env) already exist; reuses `listClaims` from `@splash/db-d1`; one fewer worker to provision/secret-bind/observability-toggle. CF Workers support `{ fetch, scheduled }` on the same default export and observability differentiates by `eventType: scheduled` (Brief 63's block covers both automatically). **Implementation (3 modified files + 1 new):** (1) `packages/db-supabase/src/summary.ts` (NEW) — two helpers: `listSummaryRecipients(env)` reads `auth_unified` via PostgREST, filters server-side on `dc_role IN (gm,rm,admin,super_admin)`, orders dc_role asc + email asc for grep-able cron logs, fail-soft returns []; `fetchLocationRoster(env)` reads `pricing_simple` for `location_code, location_pretty, am_email, area_manager, rm_email, regional_manager`, collapses on first-seen by code (multiple package rows per location collapse via the location-level columns being identical post-trigger-sync), returns `Map<location_code, LocationRosterEntry>` for O(1) lookup. (2) `packages/db-supabase/src/index.ts` — re-export `./summary.js`. (3) `apps/damage-worker/wrangler.toml` — append `[triggers] crons = ["0 13 * * *"]` block (13:00 UTC = 8 AM ET) above `[observability.logs]`; comment block documents fail-soft posture and operator's wrangler-secret bind step. (4) `apps/damage-worker/src/index.ts` — import the new helpers + `DamageRole` type; add `DAILY_SUMMARY_WEBHOOK_URL?: string` to the `Env` interface; default export becomes `{ fetch, scheduled }` with `scheduled(_controller: ScheduledController, env, ctx)` calling `ctx.waitUntil(runDailySummaryCron(env))`. **`runDailySummaryCron` flow:** (a) check webhook secret bound — if not, log + return; (b) parallel fetch recipients + locationsByCode + listClaims({lifecycle:"Open", limit:5000}); catastrophic failure logs and re-throws so CF marks the invocation failed (per-user errors stay swallowed); (c) for each recipient, filter (admin/super_admin: unrestricted; gm/rm: filter to dc_locations) → skip-on-empty → `buildDigestPayload` → `postDigest`; (d) accumulate sent/skipped_empty/failed_post counts; (e) final `console.log("[daily-summary] batch complete", {…})`. **`buildDigestPayload`** groups claims by RD email → RM email → location_code, materializes the hierarchy with counts rolled up at each level, sorts by count desc + name asc within each level, claims sorted submitted_at asc, age_days computed (floor at 0) from `as_of`, vehicle assembled as `"<year> <make> <model>"` with `"—"` fallback. Locations with null rd_email/rm_email bucket under sentinel name `(unassigned)`. **`postDigest`** uses `AbortSignal.timeout(15_000)` + Content-Type JSON; non-2xx and thrown both log and return false (caller increments failed counter). **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (1) **`name` field on `SummaryRecipient` is always null today** — the `auth_unified` view does NOT expose a name column (verified via `packages/db-supabase/src/auth-context.ts`'s `AuthUnifiedRow` shape). The brief explicitly anticipated this with "null otherwise"; PA's email template can fall back to the email's local-part for greetings, or operator can later extend the view to project `auth.users.raw_user_meta_data->>'full_name'` and the helper's interface stays compatible since the field is already nullable. (2) **Helper file is a new sibling `summary.ts`** rather than appended to `auth-context.ts` — brief explicitly allowed "(or sibling new file `summary.ts` if cleaner — executor's call)"; the sibling keeps the auth-context module focused on session shape. (3) **`listClaims` limit override = 5000** — passed `{lifecycle:"Open", limit:5000}` to override the helper's default 100. Realistic open-claims volume (~50/loc/year × 67 locations × ~0.5 still-open ≈ 1675) fits comfortably; a single fetch covers admin/super_admin's full view without pagination. v2 candidate: chunk by RD or page the claims list if super_admin volume eventually exceeds PA's inbound JSON limit (~4 MB). (4) **`scheduled(_controller: ScheduledController, …)` not `ScheduledEvent`** — TypeScript's `ExportedHandlerScheduledHandler` types the first arg as `ScheduledController` not `ScheduledEvent` (caught by `pnpm typecheck` on first attempt). (5) **In-code `SUMMARY_DC_ROLES` constant** — single edit point for opt-out at the role level. The brief's wording is "every gm/rm/admin/super_admin"; this constant documents that policy in code (and is referenced in CLAUDE.md's new Daily-summary glossary entry). (6) **Multi-row pricing_simple collapse via first-seen** — `pricing_simple` has multiple rows per location_code (one per package); the columns we read are denormalized FROM `locations` BY `trg_sync_pricing_simple` and so are identical across all rows for a code. First-seen collapse is correct and avoids a SELECT DISTINCT round-trip. **Latent issues / forward flags:** (a) **Operator must bind `DAILY_SUMMARY_WEBHOOK_URL`** via `pnpm --filter @splash/damage-worker exec wrangler secret put DAILY_SUMMARY_WEBHOOK_URL` after the next CF Workers Builds deploy lands; until then the cron logs `"[daily-summary] DAILY_SUMMARY_WEBHOOK_URL unbound — skipping"` and exits cleanly. (b) **Operator must build the PA flow** per the sample payload in the brief Outcome (HTTP request received → schema gen from sample → Send email V2 with To = `triggerBody()?['user']?['email']`, Subject = "Daily damage-claim summary — {N} open", Body = HTML with nested loops over regional_directors → regional_managers → locations → claims). (c) **No headless smoke test possible** — `wrangler dev --test-scheduled` runs locally but a true E2E requires the real D1 binding; defer to first 13:00 UTC tick post-deploy. (d) **Open-claim definition is `lifecycle: "Open"` for v1** — mirrors Brief 59's bucket (any claim_status NOT starting with `Closed —`). v2 candidate: subdivide into "Pending Review" (more actionable for GMs) vs "Approved in-flight" (waiting on parts/checks). (e) **Single 13:00 UTC tick for all recipients** — no per-user timezone preference today; v2 could read a `timezone` column from auth_unified or a user-prefs table. **Bundle delta:** dry-run **1715.21 KiB / gzip 388.18 KiB** (Brief 64 baseline ~1705 KiB / ~386 KiB; +~10 KiB raw / +~2 KiB gzip — within budget). **Validation:** `pnpm typecheck` 13/13 successful (4.071s after fix; first attempt failed with TS2322 on `ScheduledEvent` vs `ScheduledController`); `pnpm --filter @splash/damage-worker exec wrangler deploy --dry-run --outdir=.dryrun` bundled cleanly; `.dryrun` cleaned up afterward. **Operator follow-up:** (i) bind `DAILY_SUMMARY_WEBHOOK_URL` via wrangler secret post-deploy; (ii) build the PA flow (HTTP → email) with the sample payloads in the brief Outcome; (iii) verify the next 13:00 UTC tick produces emails for currently-active gm/rm/admin/super_admin users with open claims; (iv) flag any per-user opt-out requests for v2 (today's path is "remove their dc_role" or "edit `SUMMARY_DC_ROLES`"). | Claude Code (Brief 65 session) |
| 2026-05-07 | **Brief 64 completed:** One-line fix (with cascading cleanup) for Brief 61's `setDcRole` helper in `packages/db-supabase/src/users.ts`. **Latent defect (Brief 61):** the helper modeled its `damage_claim_user_roles` write after `setRole`'s `user_permissions` write and assumed an `email` column on the dc_role table. The actual schema is `(user_id, dc_role)` — email lives on `auth.users` and is joined by the `auth_unified` view at read time (consistent with the contract documented in `packages/db-supabase/src/auth-context.ts` and confirmed by Brief 011b's view-definition audit). **Symptom:** every Set DC Role submission 500ed with Postgres 42703 (`column damage_claim_user_roles.email does not exist`; CF requestId `5ZAAKPF23IPE2KMG` per operator's 2026-05-07 log). **Fix scope (extended beyond brief's literal Phase 1.1):** the brief asked to drop `email` from the upsert payload only, but the helper also had two `.select("user_id,email,dc_role")` calls — the unconditional before-snapshot read at users.ts:366 (which would 42703 BEFORE the upsert was reached) and the upsert RETURNING select at users.ts:421 (which is part of the same INSERT-ON-CONFLICT-RETURNING statement, also 42703). Both selects had to drop `email` for the function to succeed; the operator follow-up step in the brief — re-test the failing user and confirm a 200 response — is otherwise unreachable. Also dropped `email` from the local `DamageRoleRow` interface (no longer matches a real row shape with it included), updated the helper docblock with a Brief 64 schema note ("writes pass only `(user_id, dc_role)`; email lives on auth.users"), and corrected the sysadmin-worker `handleSetDcRole` docblock + the inline "Resolve email — damage_claim_user_roles.email is NOT NULL" comment to drop the bogus claim (the email arg is still resolved + passed to the helper for future audit-log surfacing; the helper just doesn't write it to the table). CLAUDE.md sysadmin/audit-log paragraph gains a Brief 64 schema note that future helpers touching `damage_claim_user_roles` / `damage_claim_user_locations` must NOT model their writes after `user_permissions` (which legitimately denormalizes email for fraud-detection lookups). **Lesson:** `setRole`'s payload shape isn't a template for the dc_role tables. The two domains have different denormalization rules — `user_permissions` carries email; `damage_claim_user_roles` does not. **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (1) **Extended the fix beyond the brief's literal Phase 1.1** — the brief's Phase 1.4 said "No other change in this file" but the rationale clause specifically referred to `damage_claim_user_locations` writes, not the in-table `email` selects. Strict literal compliance would leave the function still 42703-failing on the first SELECT and the operator follow-up step unreachable; the brief's evident intent is "stop referencing a non-existent column," so all three references were dropped. (2) **Function signature kept** — `email: string` stays on the args type per brief Phase 1.2; callers continue to pass `session.email` for any future audit-log surfacing. (3) **Sysadmin-worker comment touched** — the brief's Phase 3.3 only called out CLAUDE.md updates, but leaving the misleading "damage_claim_user_roles.email is NOT NULL" comment in `handleSetDcRole` would propagate the same wrong assumption to the next reader; one-line correction. **Latent issues / forward flags:** (a) **PostgREST behavior question** — the brief author identified the upsert as the failing call, which suggests the operator's CF log showed the error message tagged to the upsert specifically. If PostgREST's column-resolution short-circuits on the first SELECT (as PostgreSQL semantics suggest it should), the line-366 SELECT should have been the failing call — the discrepancy is unresolved here but doesn't affect the fix correctness. (b) **No headless smoke test possible** — operator must redeploy splash-sysadmin (CF Workers Builds on push to `main`), then re-exercise Set DC Role on the user that triggered the 500 and confirm a 200 with both `damage_claim_user_roles` and `damage_claim_user_locations` writes landing. (c) **Audit-log entries pre-Brief-64 do not exist** — failed submissions never wrote any rows; the audit-log filter `target_type = damage_claim_user_roles` will have zero rows until the first successful invocation post-fix. **Validation:** `pnpm typecheck` passes (13/13, 3.504s, 6 cache hits + fresh rebuilds on `@splash/db-supabase` and downstream consumers). `pnpm --filter @splash/sysadmin-worker exec wrangler deploy --dry-run --outdir=.tmp-build` bundles cleanly at **760.66 KiB / gzip 143.35 KiB** (Brief 61 baseline 760.70 / 143.35 — essentially identical, since the diff removes a few lines from a single helper). `.tmp-build` directory removed afterward. **Diff size:** ~6 functional lines removed (3 references to `email` dropped from the helper + 1 interface field dropped) plus ~10 lines of docblock/comment rewrite across 2 files. **Operator follow-up:** re-test Set DC Role on the same user that triggered requestId `5ZAAKPF23IPE2KMG` and confirm a 200 response. | Claude Code (Brief 64 session) |
