For `super_admin`/`admin`, the locations DELETE still runs but the INSERT is skipped — prevents stale-data leakage on downgrade. Multi-picker entries are de-duped before insert. (2) `apps/sysadmin-worker/src/index.ts` — new `handleSetDcRole`; new module constants `VALID_DC_ROLES`, `DC_ROLE_MAX_LOCATIONS`, `DC_ROLE_PATH_RE`. The dispatcher gains a regex match for `POST /sysadmin/api/users/{userId}/dc-role` (the only dynamic-path endpoint on this worker — every other endpoint matches an exact path Set; chose surgical regex match over a router refactor to keep the diff focused). Validation: role allow-list, UUID-shape on path userId, `LOCATION_CODE_RE` per location_code, max 50 entries. For `gm`/`rm`, location_codes is required; for super_admin/admin/null it's silently dropped. Audit-log writes use `action: "set_dc_role"` and `target_type: "damage_claim_user_roles"`. The audit-log allow-lists were extended in three places: `ALLOWED_AUDIT_ACTIONS` gains `"set_dc_role"`, `ALLOWED_AUDIT_TARGET_TYPES` gains `"damage_claim_user_roles"`, and `USER_TARGET_TYPES_CSV` gains `damage_claim_user_roles` so the audit-log `audit_user_id` filter surfaces DC-role rows. (3) `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/_components/LocationCodeMultiPicker.tsx` — new `"use client"` sibling to Brief 39's single-select picker. Multi-select typeahead hits the same `GET /sysadmin/api/pricing-simple/locations?q=` endpoint; selected entries render as removable `bg-splash-blue` chips above the input. Submit shape: one `<input type="hidden" name={name} value={code}>` per selected location_code so the action reads `formData.getAll("location_codes")` as `string[]`. On-mount resolution call for any pre-filled `defaultValues` so existing dc_locations would surface as pretty-labelled chips on first render — fail-soft, falls back to raw code if resolution fails. (4) `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/_components/SetDcRoleFields.tsx` — small `"use client"` wrapper that owns the chosen role's `useState` and renders the locations picker conditionally. Visible for `gm`/`rm`; hidden with inline hint copy for `admin` / `super_admin` ("super_admin and admin bypass location scoping — no locations needed.") and `null` ("This will revoke all /admin/damage access."). The picker stays a separate client island handed in as a `locationPicker` ReactNode prop. (5) `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/_components/SetDcRoleCard.tsx` — server component containing an `<ActionForm>` (Brief 19 pattern). User picker + DC role select + locations multi-picker, posting to the new `setDcRoleAction`. Card mounted on `/admin/sysadmin?mode=users` immediately after `<SetRoleCard>` for visual symmetry. (6) `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/actions.ts` — new `setDcRoleAction` server action. Builds `{ role, location_codes }` JSON body and POSTs to `/sysadmin/api/users/{userId}/dc-role` via the existing `sysadminPostJson` helper (path takes the dynamic userId as URL segment, encoded with `encodeURIComponent`). Empty `location_codes` is sent verbatim for super_admin/admin/null; the worker ignores the field for those roles and rejects empty arrays for gm/rm. Result message reflects role + chosen-locations count. (7) `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/_sections/UserOperations.tsx` — imports `<SetDcRoleCard>`, renders it after `<SetRoleCard>`. Header comment updated from "Five user-management cards" to "Six". (8) `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/_sections/AuditLogPanel.tsx` — `ALLOWED_ACTIONS` and `ALLOWED_TARGET_TYPES` extended in the same positions as the worker-side allow-lists. **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (1) **Dynamic path dispatch via regex** — the worker's other endpoints all use exact-path Sets; added a single `DC_ROLE_PATH_RE` and `isOwnedDcRolePost` short-circuit rather than refactoring every endpoint to a router pattern. (2) **`UUID_RE` and `LOCATION_CODE_RE` re-used** — both consts already exist in the file (audit-log handler + create-location handler); reused them. (3) **`SetDcRoleFields` is a separate client wrapper** — opted for show/hide rather than a disabled state because the locations field is *strictly* irrelevant for super_admin/admin (the worker ignores it). (4) **Multi-picker dedupes silently** before insert — defense-in-depth at helper level handles any race or programmatic re-submit. (5) **Always wipe `damage_claim_user_locations`** when setting role to super_admin/admin (not just when clearing) — matches the brief's "stale-data leakage on downgrade" guidance. (6) **Audit-log `before` / `after` shapes** are `{ role, location_codes }` objects (not raw row arrays) — stable to render in the existing AuditLogPanel diff view. (7) **No Create User integration** (brief Phase 5 explicitly out-of-scope) — operator runs Create User → Set DC Role as a two-step flow. **Latent issues / forward flags:** (a) **Operator backfill required** — every user created or role-changed via the sysadmin tool pre-Brief-61 currently has empty `damage_claim_user_roles` / `damage_claim_user_locations` and sees no claims. The new tool is the supported way to remediate. (b) **`damage_claim_user_roles.email` column assumed** — the brief specifies the upsert row shape `(user_id, email, dc_role)`; the helper writes it verbatim. If the live table schema doesn't carry `email`, the upsert will throw and the `set_dc_role` audit row never lands. Operator's first invocation on a real user is the empirical confirmation. (c) **Multi-picker on-mount resolution issues N requests** for N pre-filled defaultValues — dormant since the card today doesn't pre-fill, but if a future "edit existing assignment" UX wires defaultValues, the helper's per-code fetch could be batched into a single `or=(eq.code1,eq.code2,...)` request. (d) **No headless smoke test possible** — operator must redeploy splash-sysadmin (CF Workers Builds on push to `main`) and apps/web, then exercise the new card from `/admin/sysadmin?mode=users` to confirm: gm with no locations is rejected; super_admin with submitted locations succeeds, locations are dropped, and any pre-existing dc_locations rows are wiped; clearing access deletes both rows; the Activity log surfaces a `set_dc_role` row with before/after dc_role + location_codes side-by-side via Brief 53's full-width diff panel. (e) **Bundle deltas** — sysadmin-worker dry-run **760.70 KiB / gzip 143.35 KiB** (Brief 54 baseline 755.79 / 142.42; +4.91 KiB / +0.93 KiB gzip — expected from the new handler + helper + audit-list extensions). apps/web `/admin/sysadmin` route bundle **9.2 kB / 114 kB First Load JS** (Brief 53 baseline 8.38 kB / 113 kB; +0.82 kB / +1 kB FLJ from the new `<LocationCodeMultiPicker>` + `<SetDcRoleFields>` client islands). Comfortably within budget. (f) **CLAUDE.md updated** in the sysadmin glossary entry to describe the Set DC Role tool and the user_permissions/dc_role independence rule — the load-bearing context for why the brief added a new card rather than folding dc_role into the existing Set Role flow. **Validation:** `pnpm typecheck` 13/13 successful (4.002s; 10 cache hits + fresh builds on `@splash/db-supabase` + downstream consumers including `@splash/sysadmin-worker` and `@splash/web`). `pnpm --filter @splash/web build` succeeded — Next 15.5.15 compiled in 5.7s, all 13 routes generated; `/admin/sysadmin` 9.2 kB / 114 kB First Load JS. `pnpm --filter @splash/sysadmin-worker exec wrangler deploy --dry-run --outdir=.tmp-build` succeeded — Total Upload **760.70 KiB / gzip 143.35 KiB**. `.tmp-build` cleaned up. **Operator follow-up:** (i) backfill existing super_admins / damage-team users via the new tool so they regain `/admin/damage` access; (ii) post-redeploy, exercise the four scenarios in (d) above; (iii) verify the audit-log diff panel renders the new `set_dc_role` rows correctly when filtered by user, action, or `damage_claim_user_roles` table. | Claude Code (Brief 61 session) |
| 2026-05-06 | **Brief 60 completed:** The Reporting page's "By Damage Type" section gained a third column. Brief 59 shipped two side-by-side tables (Approved + Denied) — both terminal-outcome breakdowns. Operator's 2026-05-06 follow-up: the operationally most useful slice is "what's currently in flight" — the Open pipeline — because it answers "where's the volume coming from this period?" without waiting for claims to close. **Why:** terminal-outcome breakdowns lag the actual operating picture; managers need to see in-flight damage-type volume to triage. **Implementation (one new SQL query + one response field + one apps/web grid swap):** (1) `apps/damage-worker/src/index.ts` `getReporting` — added a sibling `byDamageTypeOpenSql` next to the existing `byDamageTypeApprovedSql` / `byDamageTypeDeniedSql` blocks. Same `submitted_at BETWEEN ?1 AND ?2 AND location_code IN (...)` filters as the rest of the reporting batch; the open-specific predicate is `lifecycle_state = 'Open'` (matches the existing KPI tile + per-location pivot definition — claim_status NOT starting with `Closed —`). `COALESCE(damage_type, '(none)')` mirrors the F/G pattern (legacy rows pre-Brief 41 lack `damage_type`). `ORDER BY n DESC` for visual consistency. The query is appended to the same `env.DB.batch` array (now 11 statements instead of 10) so total reporting wall-time picks up at most one D1 round-trip's worth of latency on the parallel batch. (2) Response shape: new `by_damage_type_open: ReportingByDamageTypeRow[]` field added to the `ReportingResponse` interface alongside the existing `by_damage_type_approved` / `by_damage_type_denied`. `emptyReportingResponse` extended with `by_damage_type_open: []` so the no-data branch shape matches. The new `byDamageOpen` mapping fold (n → count) is identical to the existing approved/denied folds. (3) `apps/web/app/admin/damage/reporting/page.tsx` — `ReportingResponse` TypeScript interface gains `by_damage_type_open: Array<{ damage_type: string; count: number }>`; the `By Damage Type` section grid changes from `md:grid-cols-2` to `md:grid-cols-3`; the existing `<DamageTypeTable>` helper (already factored out in Brief 59) is invoked three times in **Open / Approved / Denied** order. **Order rationale:** Open first because that's the "what's coming in" view operators glance at most often; on mobile (single-column stack) the order matters more, and Open surfaces above the terminal lookbacks. Approved + Denied keep their relative order. The footnote at the bottom of the page was untouched — it talks about cost-aggregation methodology, not the breakdown count, so it didn't need to change. **Files modified:** 2 — `apps/damage-worker/src/index.ts`, `apps/web/app/admin/damage/reporting/page.tsx`. Plus `BRIEFS/INDEX.md` (Brief 60 row appended) and this file. **Files created:** none. **Out of scope (per brief):** sub-segmenting the Open column by stage (New / Pending GM / Pending RM / Approved-but-not-paid) — single Open bucket is the v1 ask; v2 if operator wants finer granularity. Adding an Open KPI tile or per-location Open column — those already exist on the Reporting page from Brief 59. Reordering or restyling the existing Approved/Denied tables beyond what's needed to fit a third sibling. **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (1) **Filter expressed as `lifecycle_state = 'Open'`** rather than re-deriving from `claim_status NOT LIKE 'Closed —%'` — the column is already exposed and Brief 59's KPI tile + per-location pivot use the same predicate; consistency wins. (2) **Brief's suggested per-location intersect (`AND lifecycle_state = 'Open'` rather than approved-style claim_status pattern) ships verbatim** so the new column counts the same set of claims that the Open KPI counts. (3) **No new helper component** — the brief's Phase 2.2 said "factor out a DamageTypeTable helper if Brief 59 inlined the two tables" but Brief 59 already extracted the helper at the bottom of the page. Reused as-is. (4) **Order Open / Approved / Denied** matches the brief's specified order; preserved verbatim including the rationale comment about mobile single-column stack. (5) **`by_damage_type_open` is the SECOND key in the response object** (between `by_location` and `by_damage_type_approved`) so a future reader sees it grouped with its siblings. **Latent issues / forward flags:** (a) **Approved + Denied subset relationship to Open** — by design, an Open claim can later transition to Approved (counted by Approved column once it terminates) or Denied. The three columns are NOT mutually exclusive over time, but for any given snapshot `submitted_at BETWEEN from AND to`, an individual claim row contributes to exactly one of {Open, Approved-family-closed, Denied-closed} based on its current `claim_status`. Sum of (Open + Approved + Denied) ≤ total claims for the window because some closed states (e.g., `Closed — Withdrawn`) are neither Approved-family nor Denied. (b) **Bundle delta** — `apps/damage-worker` total upload **1705.76 KiB / gzip 385.98 KiB** (Brief 55 baseline 1688.34 / 382.64; +17.42 KiB / +3.34 KiB gzip; the larger-than-expected delta reflects the cumulative effect of Briefs 56-59 on the damage-worker bundle, not Brief 60 specifically — Brief 60's diff is one ~10-line SQL block + ~5-line interface + result mapping). `apps/web` `/admin/damage/reporting` route 172 B / 105 kB First Load JS — unchanged from Brief 59 baseline (same component tree, same client surface, just one extra `<DamageTypeTable>` instance). (c) **No headless smoke test possible** — operator must navigate to `/admin/damage/reporting` after the next CF Workers Builds redeploys both `splash-damage` and `splash-web`, click QTD (or any window with claims in flight), and confirm the third Open column populates with non-zero rows. Open should be the leftmost column on desktop (`md:grid-cols-3`) and the topmost on mobile (single-column stack). (d) **No schema change, no new env var, no new endpoint** — per brief Phase 3.4. **Validation:** `pnpm typecheck` 13/13 successful (5.15s, 11 cache hits + fresh `@splash/web` + fresh `@splash/damage-worker` rebuilds). `pnpm --filter @splash/web build` succeeded — all 14 routes generated, `/admin/damage/reporting` route 172 B / 105 kB First Load JS. `pnpm --filter @splash/damage-worker exec wrangler deploy --dry-run --outdir=.tmp-build` succeeded — Total Upload **1705.76 KiB / gzip 385.98 KiB**, all 6 bindings (DB / R2_BUCKET / IMAGES / MAINTAINX_MODE="test" / MAINTAINX_BASE_URL / APPS_WEB_BASE_URL) resolved cleanly. `.tmp-build` cleaned up. **Operator follow-up:** navigate `/admin/damage/reporting` (any window) and confirm the third Open column populates next to Approved and Denied. | Claude Code (Brief 60 session) |

| 2026-05-06 | **Brief 54 completed:** `update_packages_bulk` audit-log entry now writes a real `before` snapshot instead of the legacy `before: null`. Brief 53's full-width diff panel surfaced the gap — the rendering layer was correct but the upstream `before` payload had never been captured for bulk requests. Fix is one new fetch block + a typed interface in `apps/sysadmin-worker/src/index.ts handleUpdatePackagesBulk`: between the per-entry validation loop and the per-row PATCH loop, a single `pkg=in.(pkg1,pkg2,…)` GET against `pricing_simple` fetches the current state of every row about to be patched (focused select list `pkg`, `pkg$`, `single`, `sort` — only the four fields the bulk endpoint can touch). Result is typed as `BulkBeforeRow[]` (new module-scoped interface alongside `BulkUpdateEntry`/`BulkRowResult`) and folded into the `logSysadminAudit` call as `before: { location_code, rows: [...] }`, mirroring the `after` shape's `location_code`+list pattern but with `rows` (current state) rather than `updates` (patches). The `pkg$` column-name's `$` requires PostgREST double-quoting in select lists; URL uses `&select=pkg,%22pkg%24%22,single,sort`. **Fail-soft:** the GET is wrapped in try/catch; on fetch throw or non-2xx status, `beforeSnapshots` stays null and the audit-log entry falls back to the legacy `before: null` shape — audit-log degradation never blocks the bulk update itself. `Array.isArray` narrowing on the parsed JSON ensures malformed Supabase responses (e.g., error object instead of array) don't produce a malformed audit entry. **Cost:** one extra Supabase round-trip per bulk request, regardless of `entries.length` — `in.()` returns all matching rows in a single GET. Bulk cap is 20 rows; well under PostgREST query-length limits. **Files modified:** 1 — `apps/sysadmin-worker/src/index.ts` (new `BulkBeforeRow` interface, new before-snapshot fetch block ~L1454, audit-log `before:` field rewritten to use the snapshot, docblock above the handler updated to describe the new audit shape). Plus standard updates to `BRIEFS/INDEX.md`, `BRIEFS/QUEUE.md`, this file, and the brief itself. **Out of scope (per brief):** per-row before-snapshots inside the patch loop (pre-loop GET is simpler + atomic-ish + consistent with Brief 26's single-row pattern); server-side diffing of changed-only fields (rendering layer's `DiffBlock` handles that visually); backfilling historical `update_packages_bulk` rows (data is gone); other `before: null` writes (`create_user`, `create_location`, `grant_tool` when `was_new`, `reset_password` — inserts have no meaningful before state). **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (1) **`BulkBeforeRow` declared at module scope** alongside the existing two bulk-shape interfaces — keeps the three colocated, grep-able. (2) **`(await beforeResp.json()) as unknown` + `Array.isArray` narrowing** rather than direct `as BulkBeforeRow[]` cast — defends against Supabase returning a non-array shape under unexpected conditions. (3) **Empty `rows: []` is preserved as a non-null snapshot** — `before: { location_code, rows: [] }` is informationally distinct from `before: null` (fetch-failed) and helps the operator distinguish "no rows matched" from "we couldn't read them". (4) **`encodeURIComponent` on each `pkg`** in the `in.(...)` list mirrors the per-row PATCH URL's existing posture; defends against legacy pkg names with special chars. (5) **No new endpoint, no schema change, no new env var** — the audit-log column was already `jsonb` accepting null; this brief just makes `before` actually populated for bulk. **Latent issues / forward flags:** (a) **Two-step audit cost** — bulk request now N+1 Supabase calls instead of N. +5% wall-time at the 20-row cap; +14-25% for the common 4-7 row request. Acceptable for audit fidelity. (b) **Race window** between the snapshot GET and the per-row PATCH — another operator PATCHing the same `(location_code, pkg)` via the single-row endpoint would mean `before` reflects a state that didn't exist at PATCH time. Multi-operator concurrent edits on the same location are rare. (c) **`before` payload size** ~80-200 bytes for 20 rows × 4 fields; well within `jsonb` row limits. (d) **`pkg$` URL-encoding correctness** — `%22pkg%24%22` decodes to `"pkg$"` (PostgREST double-quoted column-name syntax). Operator's smoke test (rows have `pkg$` field present, not undefined) is the empirical confirmation. (e) **No headless smoke test** — operator must redeploy and re-run a small bulk edit, then click View on the new audit row to confirm BEFORE/AFTER side-by-side renders. **Validation:** `pnpm typecheck` 13/13 successful (1.714s, 12 cache hits + fresh `@splash/sysadmin-worker` rebuild — only modified package); `pnpm --filter @splash/sysadmin-worker exec wrangler deploy --dry-run --outdir=.tmp-build` succeeded — Total Upload **755.79 KiB / gzip 142.42 KiB** (Brief 52 baseline 755.11 / 142.30 → +0.68 KiB / +0.12 KiB gzip; expected delta from new fetch block + interface + audit reshape + docblock). `.tmp-build` cleaned up. **Operator follow-up:** after splash-sysadmin redeploys (CF Workers Builds on push to `main`), re-run a small bulk edit on any underscored location_code (`batavia_ii`, `batavia_veterans`, etc.) via Update Package's bulk-edit UI; navigate to `/admin/sysadmin?mode=tables`; find the new `update_packages_bulk` row; click View → confirm the diff opens with BEFORE and AFTER side-by-side instead of AFTER-only. | Claude Code (Brief 54 session) |

Operator confirmed 2026-05-06 that large diffs (e.g., the recent `update_packages_bulk` on williamsville, a multi-row payload) were rendering as a vertical column of single characters under the prior `<details>/<summary>` toggle — effectively unreadable. **Implementation:** one new `"use client"` component (`apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/_components/AuditRowExpandable.tsx`, ~70 lines) holds per-row `useState<boolean>` for expansion; `AuditLogPanel.tsx` keeps its server-component shape and passes the four primary cell contents (When / Actor / Action / Target) plus a server-rendered `DiffBlock` element to the client island as React-node props. Server-side helpers (`relativeTime`, `TargetCell`, `DiffBlock`, `jsonOrDash`) stay on the server, so `Date.now()`-derived relative time strings don't hydration-mismatch. **Layout:** primary row carries only the toggle button (▶ View / ▼ Hide with `aria-expanded`); expansion row renders BEFORE / AFTER as side-by-side `<pre>` blocks on md+ viewports (single full-width column on mobile or when only one side has data), each capped at `max-h-96` with `overflow-auto` so huge diffs scroll within their pre rather than pushing the rest of the table off-screen. Notes (when present) render below the diff, preserving the prior `<details>` block's behavior. Empty case (both before/after null) shows an italic "No diff payload recorded for this entry." hint. **Border treatment:** primary row drops its bottom border when expanded (no separator between primary + expansion); expansion row carries the bottom border to separate from the next audit entry; expansion row gets a `bg-sudsy-blue-soft/40` tint to visually anchor the diff to its parent. **Multiple rows expand independently** because each row owns its own boolean state — no parent-managed Set required. **Toggle button** carries `aria-expanded={isExpanded}` and the arrow indicator flips inline (▶ → ▼) so the affordance is visually obvious. **Files created:** 1 — `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/_components/AuditRowExpandable.tsx`. **Files modified:** 1 — `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/_sections/AuditLogPanel.tsx` (deleted inline `DiffCell` with its `<details>` renderer; added server-side `DiffBlock` helper; rewrote `AuditRow` from a `<tr>` returner to an `AuditRowExpandable` invoker passing four cell nodes + diff content; added new component import). Plus standard updates to `BRIEFS/INDEX.md`, `BUILD_STATE.md`, and the brief itself. **Out of scope (per brief Section "Out of scope"):** worker-side change, API change, separate diff modal, color-coded added/removed diff highlighting (a future jsondiffpatch / react-diff-viewer integration could ship as v2 if operators ask). **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (1) **Per-row local `useState<boolean>` rather than a panel-level `Set<string>`** — the brief mentioned a Set as the "likely existing pattern" but the prior code didn't track expansion state at all (it used native `<details>`), so there's no incumbent Set to preserve. Per-row state is simpler, meets the "multiple rows independently expandable" requirement, and avoids threading state-setter callbacks through the row mapper. (2) **Server-rendered `DiffBlock`** rather than moving JSON formatting to the client — keeps `JSON.stringify` work off the client bundle, and the only thing the client island actually toggles is a boolean. (3) **Cell contents passed as React-node props** rather than passing raw row data into the client component — the helper functions (`relativeTime`, `TargetCell`, `jsonOrDash`) are pure rendering utilities that run server-side; passing pre-rendered React nodes through the prop barrier keeps them server-side and the relative time string is "frozen" at request render. (4) **Empty-payload hint** ("No diff payload recorded for this entry.") added as a new shape — the prior `<details>` block rendered an empty grid when both before/after were null, so an operator clicking View on a no-op row saw nothing. The new hint is more informative without changing which rows have payload data. (5) **Notes preserved below the diff grid** — the prior `<details>` block included `notes` as a final paragraph; kept verbatim in the new layout under the BEFORE/AFTER grid. **Latent issues / forward flags:** (a) **No headless smoke test** — operator must verify on the next CF Workers Builds redeploy that clicking View on the williamsville `update_packages_bulk` row expands the diff to full width with legible side-by-side BEFORE/AFTER. The brief's Phase 4 smoke-test guidance enumerates the test cases (open / verify side-by-side render / multiple-rows-independent / collapse). (b) **Bundle delta** — `/admin/sysadmin` route bundle grew from 7.06 kB → 8.38 kB / 113 kB First Load JS (+1.32 kB / +1 kB FLJ). Acceptable for the readability win; the new client island is the only addition. (c) **`AuditRowExpandable` returns a Fragment** wrapping two `<tr>`s — React in Next.js 15 / React 19 handles this cleanly inside a `<tbody>`, but if a future refactor wraps each row in a new component layer, fragments-of-`<tr>`s should not be wrapped in a `<div>` (would break HTML table semantics). The new component's leading comment block flags this. (d) **Hydration time formatting** — `relativeTime(row.occurred_at)` is computed once on the server during render; the string is shipped to the client island as a static React node and stays "5m ago" until the next page render. Operator cares about absolute timestamps via the `title` attr on the When cell, so this is consistent with Brief 30's posture. **Validation:** `pnpm typecheck` 13/13 successful (5.725s, 12 cache hits + fresh `@splash/web` rebuild — the one package modified). `pnpm --filter @splash/web build` succeeded — `next build` compiled in 5.1s, all 11 routes generated; `/admin/sysadmin` route 8.38 kB / 113 kB First Load JS (was 7.06 kB / 112 kB pre-Brief-53). Middleware bundle 34.1 kB unchanged. **Operator follow-up:** after CF Workers Builds redeploys apps/web on push, navigate to `/admin/sysadmin?mode=tables`, find the williamsville `update_packages_bulk` row in the Activity log (substring filter on `update_packages_bulk` action or `pricing_simple` table), click View → confirm the diff opens as a full-width row underneath with BEFORE / AFTER side-by-side; click ▼ Hide → confirm the row collapses; expand two rows simultaneously → confirm independence. | Claude Code (Brief 53 session) |
| 2026-05-06 | **Brief 52 completed:** Latent defect in Brief 26's sysadmin pricing-simple search sanitization patched. `handleSearchPricingSimple` (Brief 26 ~L901) and `handleSearchPricingSimpleLocations` (Brief 39 ~L954) both sanitized user-supplied `q` with `escaped = raw.replace(/[%_,()*]/g, "")` — a strip set covering PostgREST `or()` separators (`,` `(` `)` `*`) and ILIKE wildcards (`%` `_`). The `_` in that set was over-defensive: the query is wrapped in `%...%` for ILIKE, and treating an internal `_` as a single-char wildcard is behaviorally indistinguishable from matching it literally for the operator's substring-search intent. Stripping it corrupted any query containing a literal underscore (e.g., `batavia_ii` → `bataviaii` → `%bataviaii%` matched zero rows). **Why it was dormant until now:** picker-only typing in the original Brief 26 use case (operators typing `batavia`, `binghamton`, `122`) never put a `_` in `q`. Brief 36's bulk-edit UI (`UpdatePackageCard`) introduced an auto-fetch step that passes the FULL canonical `location_code` (often underscored: `batavia_ii`, `batavia_veterans`, `cherry_hill`, etc.) to the search endpoint after a row is picked — that's where the bug bites. Operator confirmed 2026-05-06 that picking Batavia II correctly identified the location_code (4 packages reported in the picker preview) but the auto-fetch returned 0 rows and the UI surfaced "No packages found at this location" even though all 4 packages existed. **Fix:** dropped `_` from the strip regex in both handlers (now `/[%,()*]/g`); pattern is now identical between them. The two surrounding docblocks were updated to spell out the new semantics and cross-reference Brief 52 so a future reader doesn't "restore" the underscore strip thinking it's a SQL-injection guard (it never was — PostgREST is parameterized; the strip exists to prevent the user-supplied substring from accidentally constructing a different `or(…)` clause or expanding into multi-char wildcard semantics). **Files modified:** 1 — `apps/sysadmin-worker/src/index.ts` (two regex literals + two docblock paragraphs). **Files created:** none. **Files deleted:** none. **Out of scope (intentionally not patched per brief):** `handleSearchLocations` at line 1550 (Brief 27 locations editor — same strip-set pattern; the brief explicitly defers any other strip-set sites to their own briefs because `_` impact varies per consumer) and the audit-log `actor_email` filter at line 1893 (Brief 30 — same strip-set; out of scope; email addresses in this org don't contain `_` anyway, so dormant). **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (1) **Build-equivalent validation substitution** — the brief's Definition of Done called for `pnpm --filter @splash/sysadmin-worker build`, but the worker's `package.json` has no `build` script (workers don't compile; wrangler bundles at deploy time, like every other worker in this monorepo). Substituted `pnpm --filter @splash/sysadmin-worker exec wrangler deploy --dry-run --outdir=.tmp-build` as the build-equivalent: it runs the same bundling pipeline `wrangler deploy` would but without uploading. Cleaned up the temp output dir afterward. This matches the pattern Briefs 46/47/48/49 took for damage-worker dry-run validation. (2) **Docblock cross-reference to Brief 52** — added "(Brief 52 fix to Brief 26's defect)" to the inline comment so the historical context is grep-able from inside the file. The comment also explicitly explains why `_` is NOT in the strip set anymore and what the operator-intent semantics are, so a defensive future executor doesn't add it back thinking it's a hardening pass. (3) **No new endpoint, no schema change** — per the brief's "Out of scope," a dedicated `/sysadmin/api/pricing-simple/by-location-code` endpoint would be cleaner architecturally (the auto-fetch step is logically a "list packages by location_code" call, not a substring search), but the existing search endpoint with the underscore fix handles the case correctly and the brief explicitly defers the cleaner endpoint as more invasive than the bug warrants. **Latent issues / forward flags:** (a) **`handleSearchLocations` (line 1550)** is the same code shape and likely affected by the same defect class — if an operator searches Brief 27's locations editor by full `location_code` (e.g., `batavia_ii`), they'll see 0 results until that endpoint gets the same fix. The current operator workaround is to type a non-underscore substring (`batavia`); the brief explicitly defers patching it to its own brief because the impact pattern needs to be confirmed first. (b) **Audit-log `actor` filter (line 1893)** strips `_` from `actor_email` substrings; emails in this org are `firstname.lastname@splashcarwashes.com`-shape (verified by inspection — `user_permissions` table doesn't include `_`-bearing email addresses), so the strip is dormant. If anyone is added later with an underscore in their email local-part, the filter will silently mismatch. (c) **PostgREST query construction posture** — the strip-set pattern across the worker is consistent but not centralized; if a future brief touches this, lifting the sanitization to a `packages/http` helper (alongside `isOriginAllowed`) would make audits like Brief 52 unnecessary because all consumers would pick up the fix simultaneously. Not done here — the brief's scope is two specific lines, not a refactor. **Validation:** `pnpm typecheck` 13/13 successful (1.56s, 12 cache hits + fresh `@splash/sysadmin-worker` rebuild — the one package modified). `pnpm --filter @splash/sysadmin-worker exec wrangler deploy --dry-run --outdir=.tmp-build` succeeded — Total Upload **755.11 KiB / gzip 142.30 KiB**. Bundle assembles without diagnostics. Confirmed regex pattern is identical in both handlers post-change (Grep returned both lines as `/[%,()*]/g`). Temp output dir removed. **Operator follow-up:** after splash-sysadmin redeploys (CF Workers Builds on push to `main`), retry "Update Package" → select Batavia II (or any location_code containing `_`) and confirm the package list populates. The auto-fetch step inside `UpdatePackageCard` will now successfully match `%batavia_ii%` against `pricing_simple.location_code`. If Brief 27's locations editor surfaces the same "0 results when searching by full location_code" symptom, that's a separate brief (apply the same one-line fix to `handleSearchLocations`). | Claude Code (Brief 52 session) |
| 2026-05-06 | **Brief 50 completed:** Two open audit items closed in a single small apps/web-only brief — (item 8) signup customer flow ownership formalized, and (item 12) root `/` page filled in. **Why bundled:** the two were the only remaining customer-facing-route ownership decisions in the audit, both touched only `apps/web`, both were unblocked once Brief 23's "customer forms live in their owning worker" precedent was set, and operator confirmed 2026-05-06 that the same logic extends to signup. One deploy; one source-of-truth update. **Item 8 — Signup customer flow ownership.** The original audit assumed `apps/web` would own `/signup/[location]` as a Next.js page; a Step-7 placeholder was scaffolded and never filled in. Brief 23 redrew the line for damage claims (customer-facing forms live in their owning worker), and the same logic applies to signup: the legacy `splash-signup` worker (and its successor `splash-signup-next`, renaming to `splash-signup` at cutover) renders the customer signup form server-side, the customer URL `/signup/{location}` is load-bearing per CLAUDE.md constraint #1 (hundreds of bookmarks on customer/admin home screens), and inserting apps/web into that path adds an OpenNext-on-CF-Workers indirection with no UX win. Decision: keep signup customer rendering in the worker, retire the apps/web placeholder. **Item 12 — Root `/` page.** Was a single-line `redirect("/admin/dashboard")` placeholder; rewritten as a server-component auth-aware redirect that reads the `sb-access-token` cookie and 307s to `/admin/dashboard` (cookie present) or `/login` (absent). Standard SaaS landing pattern; eliminates the prior unauth flow's two-redirect chain (`/` → `/admin/dashboard` → middleware → `/login?return=…`) for the common case where the user just wants to land somewhere sensible from a fresh visit. **Files modified:** 2 — `apps/web/app/page.tsx` (rewritten as a server component with `dynamic = "force-dynamic"`, awaits `cookies()`, branches on `cookieStore.has("sb-access-token")`), `CLAUDE.md` (Constraint #1 extended with the customer-facing-routes ownership callout — `/signup/{location}`, `/q/{location}`, `/join/{location}`, `/claims/{site}` are served by their owning workers, NOT by apps/web; "Working with apps/web" page lists reorganized into Real / Placeholder / Redirect-only categories — `/signup/[location]` removed, `/` moved from placeholder to Redirect-only with Brief 50 cross-reference). **Files deleted:** 1 — `apps/web/app/signup/[location]/page.tsx` (the never-filled-in Step-7 placeholder). Empty parent directories `apps/web/app/signup/[location]/` and `apps/web/app/signup/` also removed. **Files unchanged (verified per brief Phase 1.2 + 1.3):** `apps/web/wrangler.toml` (no `/signup/*` route claim, per CLAUDE.md constraint #6 production routes stay commented; the staging catch-all `staging.splashcarwashes.info/*` outranks-by-most-specific-match against the per-worker `signup/*`/`q/*`/`join/*` patterns and so doesn't need explicit `/signup/*` exclusion); `apps/web/next.config.mjs` (the dev rewrite map already explicitly comments that `/signup/*`, `/q/*`, `/join/*` customer routes aren't proxied through apps/web per decision 8 — there's no entry to scrub); `apps/web/middleware.ts` (matcher list `/admin/:path*`, `/sysadmin/:path*`, `/change-password`, `/login`, `/logout` excludes `/` already, so the root-page redirect runs in the page component itself before any /admin/* middleware fires — no loop risk). **Behavior matrix:** unauth `/` → 307 `/login`; authed-with-valid-cookie `/` → 307 `/admin/dashboard`; authed-with-EXPIRED-cookie `/` → 307 `/admin/dashboard` → middleware re-validates and 308s to `/login?required=true` (one extra hop on stale-cookie cases, acceptable trade for keeping `/` cookie-presence-only). **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (1) **Cookie name inlined as the literal string `"sb-access-token"`** rather than imported from `@splash/auth`'s `ACCESS_TOKEN_COOKIE` constant. `@splash/auth` is server-only Node and breaks Edge runtime; `apps/web/middleware.ts` already takes the same posture (inline `const ACCESS_TOKEN_COOKIE = "sb-access-token"` at top of file with a comment noting the duplication is intentional). The root page's runtime is also Edge-bounded post-OpenNext build, so the same trade-off applies. Comment in the page body explains the duplication so a future reader doesn't try to "fix" it. (2) **`export const dynamic = "force-dynamic"`** added — Next.js 15 will attempt to statically analyze server components by default; reading `cookies()` at request time requires the dynamic opt-out so the redirect runs per-request rather than getting a stale build-time bake. (3) **No loader UI / spinner / shell** rendered before the redirect — the brief explicitly out-of-scoped a UX shell, and the SSR redirect lands as a 307 before any HTML ships, so there's no flicker to mask. (4) **Empty parent directories deleted** — both `apps/web/app/signup/[location]/` and `apps/web/app/signup/` had no other children once the placeholder was deleted; leaving empty dirs would have shown up in future grepping for signup-related code in apps/web and seeded confusion. (5) **No `next.config.mjs` rewrite scrubbing** — the existing rewrite map already explicitly comments that customer-facing signup URLs are NOT proxied through apps/web (per decision 8), so there's no entry to remove. The dev rewrites that DO proxy through signup-worker (`/api/submit-signup`, `/admin/api/:path*`) stay — those are admin/API surfaces, not the customer signup form itself. (6) **CLAUDE.md callout placed inside Constraint #1's body rather than as a new constraint** — keeps the customer-URL-immutability rule and the worker-ownership rule colocated; a future reader looking at constraint #1 sees both pieces in the same paragraph. **Latent issues / forward flags:** (a) **No headless smoke test possible** — Phase 5's three smoke tests (incognito visit to staging `/` lands on `/login` instantly; sign-in then `/` lands on `/admin/dashboard` without flicker; `/signup/oswego` renders the customer signup form from `splash-signup-next`) require operator-side staging redeploy and live browser verification; CLAUDE.md headless rule prohibits this session from deploying. Operator runs the three smokes after the next CF Workers Builds redeploy. (b) **The `apps/web/wrangler.toml` staging catch-all relies on per-worker route patterns being more specific** — confirmed working in Brief 16's outcome notes; no change needed for Brief 50, but flagged for awareness because cutover (Brief 13) will need to replace the staging catch-all with explicit production routes. (c) **`apps/web/app/admin/error.tsx`'s `UnrecognizedActionError` boundary (Brief 31) does NOT cover `/`** — the boundary lives at `app/admin/error.tsx`, scoped to the `/admin/*` segment. The root `/` page is a pure redirect with no server actions, so it has no failure mode that requires a boundary. (d) **No new pattern abstraction** — the cookie-presence redirect could in theory be extracted to a shared helper for future "redirect-only pages", but the single use case is `/`. CLAUDE.md guidance: "Three similar lines is better than a premature abstraction." If a second redirect-only page lands later, lift the pattern then. **Validation:** `pnpm typecheck` 13/13 successful — fully cached on the workspace pass; explicit fresh check via `pnpm --filter @splash/web typecheck` re-ran fresh and succeeded with zero errors. `pnpm --filter @splash/web build` succeeded — `next build` compiled cleanly, all 11 routes generated (down from 12 pre-Brief-50 because `/signup/[location]` is gone); `/` route bundle 127 B / 102 kB First Load JS (the placeholder also called `redirect()` so the bundle shape is essentially unchanged; the only delta is the cookie-presence branch added to the server function, which doesn't ship to the client). All other route bundles unchanged. **Operator follow-up (smoke tests on next staging redeploy):** (i) `https://staging.splashcarwashes.info/` in incognito → instant land on `/login`; (ii) sign in → land on `/admin/dashboard`; manually navigate back to `/` → instant land on `/admin/dashboard`; (iii) `https://staging.splashcarwashes.info/signup/oswego` → renders the customer signup form from `splash-signup-next` (proves the edge route binding for `/signup/*` is live and apps/web is no longer in the path). | Claude Code (Brief 50 session) |

Two-step lookup mirrors Brief 42's `getMaintainXLocationId`: step 1 hits `pricing_simple` filtered by `location_code=eq.<sanitized>` and reads `site` (text); step 2 hits `locations` filtered by `site=eq.<site>` and reads `site_email`. **Why two steps?** the `locations` table has no `location_code` column; the unique business key is `site_number`, and the `trg_sync_pricing_simple` trigger denormalizes `locations.site_email` INTO `pricing_simple` keyed by `site` text. The denormalized copy on `pricing_simple` would be a one-step alternative, but the trigger writes one direction only (locations → pricing_simple) and the brief specifies "from Supabase `locations`" — querying the authoritative source avoids any future divergence and matches Brief 42's pattern for the same reason. Catch defaults to `null` and logs a warning ("site_email Supabase resolution failed (defaulting to null)") so an unexpected helper-side throw doesn't prevent the webhook from firing — matches Brief 32's overall fail-soft posture (PDF generation failure does not fail submission; webhook lookup failure does not fail webhook delivery). The helper itself is already fail-soft (returns null on every failure path), so the try/catch is defense-in-depth. `fireCustomerClaimWebhook` signature gained a `siteEmail: string \| null` parameter; the constructed payload object now includes `site_email: siteEmail` alongside the existing `claim_id`, `submitted_at`, `location_pretty`, `location_code`, `customer_name`, `customer_email`, `customer_phone`, `vehicle`, and `summary_pdf_url` keys. The base64 attachment branch (`payload.summary_pdf_base64`) remains unchanged — the new field is plain JSON and adds at most ~50 bytes to the payload. **Phase 3 — CLAUDE.md:** `CUSTOMER_CLAIM_WEBHOOK_URL` glossary entry under "Working with workers" extended to (a) list `site_email` in the field roster, (b) document the Reply-To Power Automate semantics, (c) note that null falls back to the From mailbox for replies, and (d) cross-reference Brief 48 + the `getLocationContactInfo` helper. **Files modified:** `packages/db-supabase/src/locations.ts`, `apps/damage-worker/src/index.ts`, `CLAUDE.md`, `BRIEFS/INDEX.md`, `BRIEFS/QUEUE.md`, `BUILD_STATE.md`, `BRIEFS/brief-048-customer-webhook-add-site-email.md` (Status + Outcome). **Files created:** none. **Files deleted:** none. **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (1) **Sibling helper rather than extending `getActiveLocationByCode`** — the brief explicitly delegated this to the executor and provided both options. `getActiveLocationByCode` queries `pricing_simple` and returns customer-URL slug resolution semantics; reaching `locations.site_email` from there would force either (a) extending it to also do the second hop, conflating concerns and changing its return-shape contract, or (b) reading the denormalized `pricing_simple.site_email` copy, which is a downstream copy of the locations source. Brief 42 took the same call (`getMaintainXLocationId` is a sibling, not an extension) for the same reason — different concerns, different read frequencies (slug resolution fires on every page load, contact-info fires only on customer submission), different return shapes. (3) **Defense-in-depth try/catch at the call site** — the helper is already fail-soft and returns null on every failure path, so the call-site try/catch is technically redundant. Keeping it because (a) Brief 33's `getActiveLocationByCode` resolution at line 1746 follows the same pattern (warn + ignore), and (b) it documents at the call site that this lookup is allowed to fail without affecting the webhook. | 2026-05-06 | **Brief 45 completed:** `apps/web/app/admin/damage/_components/EquipmentOverrideModal.tsx` now renders its `<div role="dialog">…</div>` block through `createPortal(…, document.body)` instead of inline. **Why:** Brief 43 shipped a working UI but operator confirmed 2026-05-06 that the modal's Confirm only triggered the underlying transition POST ~1/5 times — the other 4/5 attempts produced no transition write, only the post-`setOpen(false)` `router.refresh()` GET. **Root cause (per the brief's diagnosis):** the modal's `<form onSubmit={handleConfirm}>` was nested inside the parent transition `<form>` in the live DOM, because `EquipmentOverrideSubmit` is rendered INSIDE the parent transition form (so `submitButton.form` resolves correctly via the React `buttonRef`) and the conditional modal block is its sibling under a React fragment — when `open === true` the dialog DOM mounted as a sibling of the button, which is a sibling of the parent form's submit pipeline, but the dialog itself contains a `<form>` so the inner form ended up inside the outer one. Browser recovery from invalid nested-form HTML is implementation-defined: sometimes the inner form is detached/inert and events bubble correctly to the outer form (the 1/5 success), sometimes the inner form's submit handler interferes with the outer form's React 19 `useActionState` dispatch and the action never runs (the 4/5 failure). **Fix:** wrap the dialog block in `createPortal(…, document.body)` so the modal DOM lives outside the parent form. The submit button (`buttonRef`) stays in the parent form so `submitButton.form` continues to resolve; only the dialog's DOM moves. SSR-guarded via `typeof document !== "undefined"`. **Files modified:** `apps/web/app/admin/damage/_components/EquipmentOverrideModal.tsx` (one file, ~10 lines changed: added `import { createPortal } from "react-dom"`, wrapped dialog in `createPortal(…, document.body)`, extended leading comment block with the portal rationale + a "do not simplify back" warning so a future reader doesn't reintroduce the bug), `BRIEFS/INDEX.md`, `BUILD_STATE.md`, `BRIEFS/brief-045-equipment-modal-portal-fix.md`. **Files created:** none. **Files deleted:** none. **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (1) **No retreat to `submitButton.click()`** — the brief listed this as a possible follow-up if the portal fix alone didn't resolve flakiness (Phase 2.2). I'm landing the portal-only fix first per the brief's primary scope; if the operator's smoke test still shows non-determinism (e.g., 4/5 succeed), Brief 46 can revisit. The brief's analysis predicts portal alone is sufficient because the React state ordering of `setOpen(false)` → `requestSubmit` was never the bug — the DOM-level interference was. (2) **Inner JSX indentation is mildly inconsistent** post-edit (the `<form>` and its descendants kept the original 10-space indent, while the wrapping `<div role="dialog">` is now indented 12 spaces inside `createPortal(…,`). Decision: leave the inner block verbatim per the brief's "the contents of the modal's `<form>` … stay verbatim. Only the wrapping changes." Re-indenting 90 lines would inflate the diff for cosmetic-only reasons; TypeScript/JSX whitespace-insensitive parser doesn't care. (3) **No worker-side changes** — damage-worker is not redeployed by this brief; the bug was purely client-side. **Latent issues / forward flags:** (a) **Operator must verify 5/5 submissions reach the worker after the next apps/web CF Workers Builds deploy** — headless cannot exercise the live browser interaction; only the operator can confirm the portal fix actually resolves the flakiness. The brief explicitly flags this as a Phase 2 smoke-test action item. (b) **Future modals rendered inside parent forms should portal-by-default** — recording the gotcha here so future briefs adding a similar pattern (e.g., a confirm-cancel dialog with its own `<form>` inside a transition or upload form) don't recreate this bug. The "render the dialog block via `createPortal`" rule applies any time a child component renders both a submit button (which has to live inside the parent form to be a submitter) AND a dialog containing its own form/event handlers (which need to NOT nest inside the parent form). (c) **No worker-side or PA Parse JSON impact** — Brief 45 is purely a client-side DOM-positioning fix; the override-related FormData payload, the worker validation, and the activity-log shape are all unchanged from Brief 43. **Validation:** `pnpm typecheck` 13/13 successful (4.11s, cache hit on 12, fresh build on `@splash/web` only — only one package modified); `pnpm --filter @splash/web build` succeeded — `next build` compiled in 5.9s, all 12 routes generated; `/admin/damage/[id]` route bundle 4.11 → 4.15 kB (+0.04 kB) / 109 kB First Load JS unchanged (`createPortal` is already in `react-dom`, so the bundle delta is just the conditional shape change). **Operator action items:** (1) After CF Workers Builds redeploys apps/web on push, find a claim with `equipment_related === 0` in "Pending GM Review" status, click the approve button → modal opens → Yes → equipment piece select → Confirm. Verify in damage-worker CF Observability that a POST/PATCH fired to the transition endpoint AND the claim's `claim_status` updated AND the activity log got two entries (`status_change` + `[maintainx]` `note`). Repeat 5 times. If 5/5 succeed the brief is fully validated. If <5/5, file a Brief 46 with the `submitButton.click()` follow-up per Brief 45 Phase 2.2. | Claude Code (Brief 45 session) |
| 2026-05-06 | **Brief 43 completed:** GM/RM/admin/super_admin can now flip a claim's `equipment_related` no→yes when approving into one of the two active-repair branches ("Approved — Pending Quotes" or "Approved — In House — Parts Ordered"). The customer claim form's employee section captures `equipment_related` at submit time (Brief 25); some claims slip through with `equipment_related === 0` even when the damage IS equipment-related (employee didn't notice or didn't want to flag it). Brief 43 adds a second-chance gate at GM-side approval. **Phase 1 — worker:** `apps/damage-worker/src/index.ts handleStatusTransition` now reads two new optional form fields, `override_equipment_related` ("yes"/"no" or absent — back-compat with existing transition buttons) and `override_equipment_piece` (required when override=yes, ≤200 chars). Server-side validation: yes-only on the two target statuses, only no→yes flips (rejecting yes→yes-with-override avoids accidentally defeating the dedupe gate), piece required + length-capped. When the override fires, `equipment_related = 1` and `equipment_piece = ?` are folded into the same UPDATE that lands the status change (atomic with the status_change activity-log INSERT in `env.DB.batch([updateStmt, activityStmt])`). The status_change activity row's notes field gets a `[Equipment override] {actor} flipped equipment_related to yes during "{finalTo}" approval (equipment_piece: P)` sentinel suffix on the same row — no new activity_type introduced (D1 CHECK constraint still rejects new types per Brief 42's same finding). After the batch commits, a new module-local `tryCreateMaintainXIfMissing` helper fires Brief 42's `createMaintainXWorkOrder` with the same fail-soft posture (8 s `AbortController` timeout, helper never throws past the handler, `[maintainx]` `note`-typed activity-log entry on success/failure). The helper re-reads `claims.maintainx_workorder_id` via `getClaimById` BEFORE issuing the POST so a concurrent writer that already landed a WO short-circuits this path (defense-in-depth dedupe on top of `updateMaintainXWorkOrderId`'s "UPDATE … WHERE maintainx_workorder_id IS NULL" semantics). The transition response now optionally includes `maintainx_attempted` + `maintainx_ok` flags (only on the override path) so apps/web can distinguish the create-failed branch. **Phase 2 — apps/web modal:** new `apps/web/app/admin/damage/_components/EquipmentOverrideModal.tsx` exports `EquipmentOverrideSubmit` — a client island that renders the transition form's submit button + a modal-gated pre-flight. Modal asks "Was this damage equipment related?" with segmented No/Yes radios (No defaulted) and reveals a `<select required>` of 6 equipment-piece options (Top Brush / Side Wraps / Conveyor / Dryer / Wheel Blaster / Other — hard-forked from `EQUIPMENT_CHOICES` at `apps/damage-worker/src/render/claim-form.ts:18` with an in-file comment pinning the worker as canonical). Click handler intercepts only when `modalEnabled && enabled`; otherwise the form submits normally. On confirm, `appendHiddenInput` writes (or updates in place) `override_equipment_related` ("yes" or "no") + `override_equipment_piece` (when yes) hidden inputs onto the parent `<form>`, then `form.requestSubmit(submitButton)` re-uses the existing `<ActionForm>` + React 19 `useActionState` pipeline unchanged. ESC + click-outside both cancel without submitting. `apps/web/app/admin/damage/[id]/page.tsx` — `TransitionSection` + `TransitionForm` now accept `equipmentRelated: 0 | 1`; new module-local `EQUIPMENT_OVERRIDE_TARGETS` set holds the two target statuses; the inline `<button type="submit">` was swapped for `<EquipmentOverrideSubmit>` with `modalEnabled={equipmentRelated === 0 && EQUIPMENT_OVERRIDE_TARGETS.has(to)}`. **Phase 3 — server action:** `apps/web/app/admin/damage/[id]/actions.ts transitionAction` already forwards every form field via `damagePostForm` (FormData → URLSearchParams), so the override hidden inputs reach the worker without plumbing changes. Added one read of `result.body` to detect `maintainx_attempted === true && maintainx_ok === false` and emit "Status updated to X, but the MaintainX work order couldn't be created — see activity log" on the failure path. **Files modified:** `apps/damage-worker/src/index.ts`, `apps/web/app/admin/damage/[id]/page.tsx`, `apps/web/app/admin/damage/[id]/actions.ts`, `BRIEFS/INDEX.md`, `BRIEFS/QUEUE.md`, `BUILD_STATE.md`, `BRIEFS/brief-043-gm-equipment-related-modal-on-approve.md`. **Files created:** `apps/web/app/admin/damage/_components/EquipmentOverrideModal.tsx`. **Files deleted:** none. **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (1) **Equipment-piece options hard-forked into the modal file** rather than hoisted into `@splash/types` — the brief explicitly delegated this to executor judgment. The list is short (6 entries), rarely changes, and an in-file comment pins the worker as canonical so future edits coordinate both sides. The cross-package alternative would have required converting `@splash/types` from types-only to runtime-export and a re-export through both worker and apps/web — three touch points instead of two. (2) **Modal-show condition is purely client-side** (`equipmentRelated === 0` + target ∈ {two statuses} + button enabled). Worker re-validates as defense-in-depth (rejecting yes-with-other-target and yes-on-already-yes). The `appendHiddenInput` helper dedupes by name so re-opening the modal after a previous confirm updates in place rather than appending duplicates. (3) **`form.requestSubmit(submitButton)` invoked with the button as the submitter** so React 19's form-action pipeline picks up the `useActionState` formAction identically to a native click. (4) **Defense-in-depth dedupe re-read** in `tryCreateMaintainXIfMissing` short-circuits when `maintainx_workorder_id != null` already — the loaded claim row's snapshot is pre-update, and a concurrent retry could in theory land a WO meanwhile. Brief 42's `updateMaintainXWorkOrderId` UPDATE-only-when-NULL is the canonical write-time gate; the re-read avoids the wasted MaintainX POST on the read path. The ad-hoc dialog matches the visual posture of the existing confirm-delete banner pattern on the same page. (7) **Worker response shape extension is conditional** — `maintainx_attempted` + `maintainx_ok` only emit on the override path; non-override transitions return the same shape they always did. PostgREST has no SQL `DISTINCT` projection, so the worker fetches up to 200 raw rows ordered by `site.asc` and dedupes server-side by `location_code` (first occurrence wins), capping the response at 50 distinct entries. Empty/whitespace-only `q` → `[]`. **Phase 2 — apps/web client island:** new `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/_components/LocationCodePicker.tsx` mirrors the UserPicker contract — props `{ name, inputId, required?, initialValue? }`, 250 ms debounced fetch via `setTimeout` + `fetchSeqRef` race-guard, hidden `<input type="hidden" name={name}>` carrying the canonical `location_code`, combobox/listbox aria roles + arrow nav + Esc + outside-click dismiss + `aria-activedescendant`. Each dropdown row renders `#{site} {location_pretty} {location_code}` with the canonical code in mono font for visual distinction. **Phase 3 — wire into Set Role card:** `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/_sections/UserOperations.tsx` swapped the `<input name="location_code">` for `<LocationCodePicker name="location_code" inputId="set-role-location-code" />`; `FieldLabel` text changed from "Location code" to "Location" with helper "Required for location_admin role — search by site #, name, or code"; help text below the picker preserved verbatim; `setRoleAction` unchanged (the picker writes to the same `location_code` form field name the action already reads). **Files modified:** `apps/sysadmin-worker/src/index.ts`, `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/_sections/UserOperations.tsx`. **Files created:** `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/_components/LocationCodePicker.tsx`. **Files deleted:** none. **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (1) **Visible input is replaced by the selection chip rather than sitting alongside it** — UserPicker shows both (input + chip below) because the input is the primary search affordance and the chip is informational. For LocationCodePicker the chip *is* the resolved selection; leaving an empty input below it would be misleading (typing more would replace the resolved value). The "× Clear" link in the chip restores the input. Cosmetic-only divergence from the UserPicker exact spec. (2) **`FieldLabel` relabeled "Location code" → "Location"** — the visible affordance is no longer a code-typed input. Hidden form field name is still `location_code` (worker contract unchanged). (3) **Server-side dedupe rather than client-side** — brief specified DISTINCT location-level fields as the response shape; doing it server-side keeps the client component thin and the response contract clean. The 200-row raw fetch / 50-row deduped cap matches the brief's example. **Latent issues / forward flags:** (a) **Create User card has the same problem and is intentionally out of scope** — `brief-040-create-user-location-code-picker.md` is already queued to fold the same picker into that card (file at line 106-117 of `_sections/UserOperations.tsx`). No new follow-up needed. (b) **Operator should run `pnpm --filter @splash/sysadmin-worker exec wrangler deploy` from the appropriate session to ship the new endpoint** — per CLAUDE.md headless constraint, this session does not deploy. The picker depends on the new endpoint, so a missed worker deploy would leave the picker fetching a 404 from the old worker bundle (the picker would render an inline "Search failed (404)" red note under the input but the card would otherwise function — empty dropdown, no crash). **Validation:** `pnpm typecheck` 13/13 successful, 6.045s (11 cached, 2 cache-miss — `@splash/web` and `@splash/sysadmin-worker` ran fresh as expected); `pnpm --filter @splash/web build` succeeded — `next build` compiled in 6.7s, all 12 routes generated; `/admin/sysadmin` bundle 7.06 kB → 8.14 kB / 112 kB → 113 kB First Load JS (+1.08 kB / +1 kB, in the brief's "+1-2 kB" predicted range). sysadmin-worker has no `build` script per the existing pattern (`typecheck` is the build verification). **Operator action items:** (i) on next sysadmin-worker deploy, smoke-test the picker — open `/admin/sysadmin?mode=users`, click "Set role", type `111` / `Binghamton` / `batavia_v` and verify the dropdown returns one row per matching location; pick a row and verify the chip shows `#{site} {location_pretty}` with the canonical `location_code` in mono; submit with role=location_admin and confirm `set_role` audit log row records the picked code. (ii) Brief 40 (Create User picker fold-in) is already queued. | Claude Code |

| 2026-05-06 | **Brief 37 completed:** Mobile quote-upload bug closed by retiring the Next 15 server-action path for the multipart upload. **Root cause confirmed via legacy code read:** `legacy/damagemanager.js:2446` `handleDocumentUpload` is a vanilla CF-Worker handler that calls `request.formData()`, validates fields, calls the existing `uploadToR2(file, claimId, photoType, seq, env)` helper (line 260 — HEIC→JPEG via `env.IMAGES.input(file.stream()).output({ format: "image/jpeg" })`, fallback to passthrough on conversion failure), inserts the `claim_photos` row, then `Response.redirect(\`${url.origin}/manage/claim/${claimId}\`, 303)` (line 2620). The shape "browser POST → worker → 303 → SSR detail page" is exactly what worked on iPhone Safari in the 2026-05-05 operator test (~2 seconds end-to-end). The new monorepo port had the same handler shape on the worker side (apps/damage-worker uses the same `uploadClaimPhoto` helper from `@splash/storage-r2`, which mirrors the legacy `uploadToR2` line-for-line including HEIC handling) BUT routed the multipart through `apps/web/app/admin/damage/[id]/actions.ts uploadDocumentAction` — a Next 15 server action invoked through the OpenNext-on-Cloudflare-Workers runtime. That extra hop reframed the multipart body into a server-action FormData payload and re-encoded the file across an apps/web → service-binding → damage-worker subrequest, which is what produced the digest 924441341@e394 white-page on iPhone (Brief 36 Part B). **Fix:** apps/web's `UploadDocumentCard` was rewritten as a plain `<form action="/manage/api/claim/{id}/document" method="POST" enctype="multipart/form-data">` that POSTs directly to the damage-worker's existing endpoint (relative URL — works in prod via CF same-zone routing, in dev via the existing `/manage/api/:path*` rewrite in `next.config.mjs` when `NEXT_PUBLIC_DAMAGE_WORKER_URL` is set). The damage-worker's `handleDocumentUpload` was changed to 303-redirect on every branch (success → `${appsWebOrigin}/admin/damage/{claimId}`; validation/storage failure → same path with `?upload_error=<msg>`) — new helper `buildUploadRedirect(request, claimId, errorMessage?)` reads the apps-web origin from the request's `Origin` header (set by the browser on every form POST), falling back to `new URL(request.url).origin` for the same-zone production case. The apps/web side now reads `?upload_error=...` searchParam in `apps/web/app/admin/damage/[id]/page.tsx` and renders an `UploadErrorBanner` above the upload card; this re-introduces the param-based banner pattern Brief 19 retired, scoped strictly to this one form (Brief 19's pattern flip was for Next-server-action `redirect()` calls, which are unreliable on OpenNext-on-CF-Workers — raw HTTP form POSTs from a plain `<form>` to a Cloudflare Worker have no such issue, browser handles the redirect natively). The deleted-on-retirement code: `uploadDocumentAction` in `apps/web/app/admin/damage/[id]/actions.ts` (with its Brief 36 Part B defensive try/catch — both no longer load-bearing) and `damagePostMultipart` in `apps/web/app/admin/damage/_lib/worker-fetch.ts` (sole consumer was the deleted action). **Phase 3 anchor button:** added `<a href="#upload-document">Add document</a>` next to the existing `<a href="#add-note">Add note</a>` button in `RecentNotesBox`. Same Tailwind classes, same shape — only the href target and label differ. The smooth-scroll CSS rule (`html { scroll-behavior: smooth; }`) was already in place from Brief 22; `id="upload-document"` was already on `UploadDocumentCard`'s root from Brief 20. **HEIC handling:** unchanged. `packages/storage-r2/src/index.ts uploadClaimPhoto` already mirrors the legacy `uploadToR2` line-for-line (HEIC→JPEG via `env.IMAGES.input(file.stream()).output({ format: "image/jpeg" })`, fallback to passthrough). Verified during Phase 1 read; no code change needed. **Files modified:** `apps/damage-worker/src/index.ts` (handleDocumentUpload + new buildUploadRedirect helper), `apps/web/app/admin/damage/_components/UploadDocumentCard.tsx` (full rewrite — drop ActionForm, plain form posting to relative URL), `apps/web/app/admin/damage/[id]/actions.ts` (delete uploadDocumentAction + drop damagePostMultipart import + retitle docblock), `apps/web/app/admin/damage/_lib/worker-fetch.ts` (delete damagePostMultipart), `apps/web/app/admin/damage/[id]/page.tsx` (read ?upload_error + render UploadErrorBanner; add "Add document" anchor button in RecentNotesBox), `BRIEFS/INDEX.md` (Brief 37 row marked Completed), `BRIEFS/QUEUE.md` (Brief 37 line moved to completed-tombstone block), `BUILD_STATE.md` (this entry + Last updated bump + prioritized work list row 37), `BRIEFS/brief-037-mobile-upload-legacy-port-plus-add-doc-anchor.md` (Outcome filled in, Status set to Completed). **Files created:** none. **Files deleted:** none (logical deletions of `uploadDocumentAction` and `damagePostMultipart` are line-level edits inside surviving files; the source files themselves stay). **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (1) **Worker returns 303 unconditionally** — every error branch redirects with `?upload_error=<msg>` rather than returning JSON. The brief explicitly chose Option A; JSON return values would only matter for non-browser API consumers, and the only consumer was the deleted `uploadDocumentAction`. (2) **Form action is a relative URL** rather than absolute env-resolved — works in prod via CF same-zone routing, works in dev via `next.config.mjs` rewrite. Avoids passing a server-resolved URL prop into the client component. (3) **Apps-web origin for redirect Location is read from Origin header**, falling back to `new URL(request.url).origin`. (4) **`?upload_error=<msg>` re-introduces the param-based banner pattern Brief 19 retired**, scoped to this one form because it bypasses `<ActionForm>` entirely. (5) **`damagePostMultipart` deleted entirely** — sole consumer was `uploadDocumentAction`; both retired together per "If you are certain that something is unused, you can delete it completely." (6) **No new pattern abstraction** — brief explicitly out-of-scoped a generalized "raw worker form" helper. (7) **HEIC handling left as-is** — verified `uploadClaimPhoto` already matches legacy. (8) **`UploadErrorBanner` rendered above `UploadDocumentCard`** rather than at top of page — error is contextually about the upload action. (9) **`docType`/`payToType` client state preserved** — Brief 20's conditional `required` UX still gates Quote-row fields. (10) **"Add document" button structure mirrors "Add note" exactly** — pattern continuity. **Latent issues / forward flags:** (a) **Cross-origin dev without Next rewrites is unsupported** — pre-existing limitation, no regression. (b) **The submit-then-redirect pattern is page-reload-based, not optimistic** — slightly less polished than the prior `<ActionForm>` flow's spinner + inline result banner; brief explicitly chose this trade-off (matches legacy iPhone path that took 2 seconds). (c) **`?upload_error=<msg>` persists in URL after the user dismisses by clicking elsewhere** — a hard-reload re-shows the banner. Future polish could `router.replace()` away the param via a small client island wrapping the banner; not worth a separate brief unless operators complain. (d) **No mobile smoke test executed in headless** — operator runs the smoke test on next deploy. (e) **Bundle delta on damage-worker:** 1661.70 KiB → 1662.94 KiB uncompressed (+1.24 KiB) / 376.97 KiB → 377.28 KiB gzip (+0.31 KiB). Slightly above the brief's "+0.3-1 KiB" estimate — the `buildUploadRedirect` helper + the explicit per-branch redirect calls add a touch more than expected. Comfortably within CF's 3 MiB compressed limit. (f) **Bundle delta on apps/web:** post-Brief-37 `/admin/damage/[id]` is 3.1 kB / 108 kB First Load JS. Removing `<ActionForm>`'s `useActionState`/`useEffect` plumbing from the upload card nets a small reduction vs. the pre-Brief-37 baseline; server-side action count drops from 5 to 4 in the route. (g) **Brief 36 Part B's defense-in-depth try/catch on `uploadDocumentAction` is now retired alongside the action itself** — root cause excised, wrap no longer load-bearing. The pattern remains on `editDocumentAction` (text-only metadata edit; doesn't need the bypass). (h) **No remaining `uploadDocumentAction` consumers** — grep across `apps/web` confirms zero imports post-deletion. **Validation:** `pnpm typecheck` 13/13 successful, 8.517s (11 cached, 2 cache-miss — `@splash/web` + `@splash/damage-worker` ran fresh as expected). `pnpm --filter @splash/web build` succeeded — `next build` compiled in 7.9s, all 12 routes generated; `/admin/damage/[id]` route bundle 3.1 kB / 108 kB First Load JS. `pnpm --filter @splash/damage-worker exec wrangler deploy --dry-run --outdir=./dist` succeeded — bundle 1662.94 KiB / 377.28 KiB gzip. **Operator action items:** (i) on next staging deploy, smoke-test HEIC upload from iPhone Safari — should succeed in <5 seconds; (ii) verify `?upload_error=<msg>` banner renders correctly by intentionally uploading an unsupported file type (e.g., `.txt` or oversized 11 MB JPEG); (iii) verify the "Add document" anchor button at the top of the claim detail page smooth-scrolls to the upload card on mobile + desktop. | Claude Code (Brief 37 session) |
| 2026-05-05 | **Brief 36 completed:** Test batch — three independent items grouped by the 2026-05-05 staging test session. **Part A (PDF humanize labels) — landed as specified:** `apps/damage-worker/src/render/claim-summary-pdf.ts` gained a `humanizeLabel(v: string \| null \| undefined): string` helper next to the existing `dash()` helper. Splits on `[_-]`, drops empty parts (defense against double-underscore tokens like `a__b`), title-cases each remaining part (first-letter uppercase, rest lowercase), joins with a single space. Null/undefined/whitespace-only → `"—"` to match `dash()`'s null-rendering. Wired into Staff Assessment in two places: `equipmentRelated` (replaced the inline `=== "yes" / === "no"` ternary with one `humanizeLabel(...)` call for one consistent path) and `determination` (replaced `value || "—"` with `humanizeLabel(value)`). Smoke-trace of the brief's enumerated determination values: `caused_by_car_wash` → "Caused By Car Wash", `not_caused_by_car_wash` → "Not Caused By Car Wash", `partial_responsibility` → "Partial Responsibility", `no_responsibility` → "No Responsibility", `under_review` → "Under Review", `yes`/`no` → "Yes"/"No", null → "—". The `dash()` helper still owns Customer Information rendering (plain "show value or em-dash"); humanization is strictly the enum-shaped path per the brief's A.3. **Part B (mobile quote upload digest `924441341@e394`) — defense-in-depth fix landed; root-cause investigation deferred:** the brief's B.1 step ("find the unscrubbed error message keyed by digest in CF dashboard logs") requires Cloudflare dashboard access, which headless Claude Code does not have. The unscrubbed exception text is **NOT** documented in this Outcome — operator action item is to open Cloudflare dashboard → Workers & Pages → splash-web → Logs → search digest `924441341@e394` (Real-time Logs while a tester reproduces, or Logpush archive if enabled) and forward the unscrubbed message + stack trace; the targeted patch (per the brief's likely-culprits list — multipart parse / HEIC / oversized photo) lands in a follow-up brief. **What did land** is the defense-in-depth try/catch wrap mandated by the brief's B.3 constraints: `apps/web/app/admin/damage/[id]/actions.ts` `uploadDocumentAction` body wrapped in `try { … } catch (err) { console.error(...); return { ok: false, error: \`Document upload failed: \${msg}\` }; }`. Mirrors the Brief 20 Bug 7 pattern already on `editDocumentAction`. Why this is a real fix even without root-cause knowledge: in Next 15 / OpenNext-on-Cloudflare-Workers, an uncaught throw inside a server action escapes the action and fails the surrounding server-component render boundary — that render-boundary failure is exactly the user-visible symptom (the production build scrubs the message and emits the digest white-page). The wrap converts every throw into an inline `<ActionForm>` red banner — page stays mounted, operator sees the actual underlying error message (not a digest), and the next session can identify the root cause from the inline banner text rather than CF logs alone. **Part C (Update Package multi-select rewrite) — landed as specified:** **Worker (`apps/sysadmin-worker/src/index.ts`):** new `PATCH /sysadmin/api/pricing-simple/packages-bulk` route — added to `OWNED_PATCH_PATHS`, dispatched in the PATCH switch, audit-action `update_packages_bulk` added to `ALLOWED_AUDIT_ACTIONS`. New `handleUpdatePackagesBulk` (~120 LOC) validates `location_code` against `LOCATION_CODE_RE`, validates `updates` array length 1..20 (`BULK_MAX_UPDATES = 20`), per-entry: requires non-empty `pkg`, rejects `BULK_REJECTED_FIELDS` (Brief 26's denorm columns + the scoped-out `pkg_new`/`flash2`/`flash5`/`pricing`/`location_pretty`), validates `pkg$` non-negative non-null, `single`/`sort` non-negative-or-null with positive-int check on `sort`, dedupes pkg names within the request, requires at least one editable field per entry. Per-row PATCH loop issues one Supabase REST PATCH per entry with `Prefer: return=representation`; partial failures don't abort peers; response shape `{ ok: true, location_code, results: [{pkg, ok, error?}], updated, failed }` for the UI's per-row breakdown. One audit log entry per request (`action: "update_packages_bulk"`, `target_id: location_code`, `after: { location_code, updates, updated, failed }`) — per-row entries would spam the log per the brief's spec. Existing `handleUpdatePackage` (Brief 26 single-row) gets a deprecation note in its docblock per §C.3; handler stays routed for SQL/curl fallback consumers. **apps/web actions (`apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/actions.ts`):** new `updatePackagesBulkAction` server action reads `bulk_pkg_<i>_selected` keys to discover selected indices, then `bulk_pkg_<i>_pkg` (travels), `bulk_pkg_<i>_pkg_dollar` (required, non-negative), `bulk_pkg_<i>_single` (empty → null), `bulk_pkg_<i>_sort` (empty → null; positive int when present). Caps at `BULK_PACKAGES_MAX_SELECTED = 20` (matches worker). Builds `UpdatePackagesBulkBody`, PATCHes via existing `sysadminPatchJson`, surfaces worker's per-row results in the success message: zero-failure → "<n> packages updated at <code>"; partial-failure → "<u> updated, <f> failed: <pkg names>" still ok-status (per brief: "Even with partial failure, return 200 with ok: true"). **apps/web client islands:** `PackageSearchPicker.tsx` repurposed from per-row composite-PK search to one-option-per-location dedupe — hits the existing `GET /sysadmin/api/pricing-simple/search?q=...` endpoint and dedupes via Map keyed by `location_code` (no new endpoint added; brief preferred reuse to a thinner `/pricing-simple/locations` endpoint). New exported types `PricingSimpleSearchRow` (kept for the package-list consumer) + `BulkLocationOption` (the deduped option shape). `UpdatePackageCard.tsx` full rewrite — 4-step flow: (1) `<PackageSearchPicker>` location pick; (2) `useEffect` fetches all packages at the chosen `location_code` via the same search endpoint, filters strictly to `r.location_code === code` (defends against substring cross-location matches), sorts by `(sort ASC, pkg ASC)`; (3) renders a table with header "select all" checkbox + per-row checkboxes; checked rows reveal `<input type="number">` for pkg$/single/sort pre-filled with current values, unchecked rows show read-only labels; (4) submit fires `updatePackagesBulkAction` via `<ActionForm>`. Submit button is `disabled` when 0 or >20 rows selected. On success, the card flips `location` identity to refetch the post-save state. **page integration:** `_sections/TableOperations.tsx` card title changed `"Update package"` → `"Update package pricing"` and description rewritten to describe the multi-select scope; `_sections/AuditLogPanel.tsx` `ALLOWED_ACTIONS` extended with `update_packages_bulk` so the new audit-action surfaces in the activity-log filter dropdown. **Files modified:** `apps/damage-worker/src/render/claim-summary-pdf.ts`, `apps/web/app/admin/damage/[id]/actions.ts`, `apps/sysadmin-worker/src/index.ts`, `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/actions.ts`, `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/_components/PackageSearchPicker.tsx`, `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/_components/UpdatePackageCard.tsx`, `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/_sections/TableOperations.tsx`, `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/_sections/AuditLogPanel.tsx`, `BRIEFS/INDEX.md`, `BRIEFS/brief-036-test-batch-pdf-humanize-mobile-upload-multi-pkg.md`, `BUILD_STATE.md`. **Files created:** none. **Files deleted:** none. **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (1) **Part B's targeted root-cause patch is deferred, not guessed at.** Per CLAUDE.md's "When in doubt: ask the operator before guessing. The cost of a clarification question is small; the cost of building the wrong thing is large." and the brief's B.4 ("Outcome must include: The unscrubbed error message + stack trace from CF logs"), this session does not pick from the likely-culprits list (multipart parse / HEIC / oversized photo / etc.) without the actual exception in hand. The defense-in-depth fix is independent of root cause and the brief explicitly mandated it as a constraint regardless. (2) **Reused `/pricing-simple/search` for location dedupe** rather than adding a new `/pricing-simple/locations` endpoint — brief explicitly preferred this order; client-side dedupe of ≤50 returned rows is microsecond-cost. (3) **`pkg$` is REQUIRED on every selected row, not nullable** — both worker and action enforce this. `pricing_simple.pkg$` is NOT NULL on the DB; making it nullable would surprise the operator more than help. (4) **Substring filter strictness** — card filters fetched packages to `r.location_code === code` exactly because the worker's `ilike` substring search can match across locations when the code substring-appears elsewhere. (5) **20-row cap matches worker exactly** — both surfaces use 20; UI disables submit when over. (6) **Brief 26's single-row endpoint stays alive but deprecated** per §C.3. (7) **Per-row failures don't roll back peers** per brief's partial-success spec. (9) **Sort key for the package list is `(sort ASC, pkg ASC)`** — mirrors the worker-side sort on Brief 26's search.
