| 2026-05-05 | **Brief 32 completed:** customer claim form closes the loop. (A) **Email required.** `apps/damage-worker/src/render/claim-form.ts` — email input gains `required` + new hint "We'll email you a copy of this claim." `apps/damage-worker/src/index.ts` `handleClaimSubmission` — worker-side trim + regex validation (`^[^@\s]+@[^@\s]+\.[^@\s]+$`); on miss returns `{ ok: false, error: "Email required" }` with status 400 (or 303 redirect to `/claims/<slug>?error=Email%20required` in browser mode). D1 column stays nullable for back-compat with pre-Brief-32 rows. (B) **Claim summary PDF.** New module `apps/damage-worker/src/render/claim-summary-pdf.ts` (~15 KiB, ~390 lines) exporting `generateClaimSummaryPdf(input: ClaimSummaryPdfInput) -> Uint8Array`. Pure programmatic pdf-lib layout (no AcroForm template; no operator pre-work beyond logo upload). US Letter (612×792 pt), 54 pt margins, Helvetica + Helvetica-Bold via StandardFonts. Layout: navy header band (70 pt tall, full-width #1e3a8a) with embedded logo on the left + "Vehicle Issue Report" title + subtitle "claim_id  •  formatted timestamp" right-aligned at 70% white opacity; LOCATION label + pretty/(#code); "Customer Information" section heading (splash-blue rule + bold label) + 5-pair key/value grid (Name / Email / Phone / Vehicle / License Plate); full-width "What Happened" text block (multi-line wrap, 11 pt); optional "Photos" row up to 4 thumbnails (max 120 pt × 90 pt) + `+N more` overflow caption; "Staff Assessment" section heading + 2-pair grid (Staff Name / Equipment-Related) + full-width "Determination" + "What the Customer Was Told" blocks; footer with claim ID + contact note. Internal helpers: `wrapText` (multi-line wrap at content width), `truncateToWidth` (ellipsis-clamping for grid cells), `embedImageBytes` (sniffs JPEG/PNG by header; falls back to attempting both), `Layout` class (cursor + page-break: overflow auto-adds new page, no type shrink). `apps/damage-worker/src/index.ts` extended: `Env` interface gains `CUSTOMER_CLAIM_WEBHOOK_URL?: string`; new constants `SUMMARY_LOGO_R2_KEY = "assets/splash-logo-white.png"` and `CUSTOMER_WEBHOOK_BASE64_MAX_BYTES = 3 * 1024 * 1024`; new public dispatch `GET /claims-api/summary/{claimId}` → `handleServeClaimSummary` reads `claims/<claimId>/summary.pdf` from R2 and streams `application/pdf` back with `Content-Disposition: inline; filename="claim-<id>.pdf"` and 24 h public cache (mirrors photo-serving posture). New step 8 in the submit pipeline (after PA fail-soft step 7) wraps `buildAndStoreClaimSummaryPdf(env, claimData, baseOrigin)` in try/catch — helper loads logo bytes (R2 first via `SUMMARY_LOGO_R2_KEY`, HTTPS fallback to `ASSETS.logoWhite`, empty-bytes fallback if both fail), pulls up to 4 photo thumbnails directly from R2 at original byte size (no resize — `ImagesBinding`'s minimal interface in `@splash/storage-r2` only supports format conversion, not transform; oversized PDFs are the brief's documented degraded fallback), builds the typed `ClaimSummaryPdfInput`, calls `generateClaimSummaryPdf`, writes bytes to `claims/<claimId>/summary.pdf` with `customMetadata: { claimId, generatedAt }`. (C) **Customer-email webhook.** On PDF success and when `CUSTOMER_CLAIM_WEBHOOK_URL` is bound, `fireCustomerClaimWebhook` POSTs JSON: `claim_id`, `submitted_at`, `location_pretty`, `location_code`, `customer_name`, `customer_email`, `customer_phone`, `vehicle` (assembled "year make model - color"), `summary_pdf_url`, plus `summary_pdf_base64` when raw PDF ≤ 3 MB (≈ 4 MB encoded ceiling). Failures logged + swallowed; submission still returns ok. Unbound env var → silent skip (no log noise; treated as feature-off). `apps/damage-worker/wrangler.toml` commented-secrets block extended to document the new env var + the operator prerequisite for uploading the brand logo PNG. (D) **Form-side polish.** `apps/damage-worker/src/render/claim-form.ts` outcome card gained `#outcomeDownloadRow` containing `<a id="outcomeDownloadLink" class="btn-primary" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Download a copy (PDF)</a>` — shown only when `summary_pdf_url` is present in the success response. `FORM_SCRIPT.showOutcome` extended to two-arg `(claimId, summaryPdfUrl)`; the fetch handler passes `out.body.summary_pdf_url` through. Worker success response shape extended from `{ ok, claim_id, ... }` to `{ ok, claim_id, summary_pdf_url?, ... }` — programmatic JSON callers get the URL as an optional field; absent on fail-soft skips. **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (1) Photos embed at original byte size, no resize (the `ImagesBinding` interface in `@splash/storage-r2` doesn't expose a resize API; bumping it would touch the package + every consumer). The base64 cap in the customer-email webhook handles oversize gracefully. (2) Logo loaded R2-first (`assets/splash-logo-white.png`) with HTTPS fallback to `ASSETS.logoWhite`; absent both → PDF renders with text-only header band. (3) Dedicated GET route `/claims-api/summary/<id>` rather than reusing `/claims-api/photo/<id>/summary.pdf` — cleaner Content-Disposition + isolates auth-posture if the photo route ever gets gated. (4) No D1 schema change; `customer_email` stays nullable for back-compat. (5) `equipmentRelated` derivation in PDF input: empty/"N/A" → "no", anything else → "yes" (matches existing `equipment_related` 0/1 derivation). (6) Email-validation error shape: 400 JSON / 303 browser redirect with `?error=Email%20required` (mirrors existing handleClaimSubmission error flow). (7) No `claim_photos` row of `photo_type='Summary'` — PDF lives in R2 only; the manager-side document gallery doesn't surface it (out of scope per "Manager-side editing of the PDF"). (8) Fail-soft cascade: PA webhook → R2-fail fallback → PDF gen → customer-email webhook. PDF is strictly additive; submission stays ok regardless. **Latent issues / forward flags:** (a) **Logo upload is a soft prerequisite.** Until the operator uploads `assets/splash-logo-white.png` to the `damagedocs` R2 bucket, every submission HTTPS-fetches `ASSETS.logoWhite` (~50-200 ms latency hit). Functional but slow. (b) **Photo bytes are embedded raw — no compression / resize.** Four 4 MB iPhone JPEGs produce a ~16 MB PDF. The webhook base64 cap (3 MB raw → ~4 MB encoded) skips the attachment for any oversized PDF; PA falls back to URL fetch. Forward fix: extend `ImagesBinding` interface for resize. (c) **`licenseState` in PDF input is always null** — form doesn't collect plate state today. PDF render handles null gracefully (License Plate cell shows just the plate number). (d) **No retention policy** on `claims/*/summary.pdf` objects. R2 lives indefinitely until operator decides on a lifecycle rule. (e) **No "regenerate summary PDF" path** — manager-side determination/what-told edits via the damage detail page don't regenerate the customer-facing PDF (frozen at submission-time). Future-brief candidate. (f) **Photos uploaded to R2 BEFORE email-validation gate** — a customer who supplies invalid email and abandons leaves orphaned R2 photo objects. | 2026-05-05 | **Brief 31 completed:** Server-action ID stability across deploys + graceful stale-tab UX — fixes the `UnrecognizedActionError` white-page client-exception the operator hit on 2026-05-05 while granting the claims tool to `batavaiawash@splashcarwashes.com`. Two layers landed. **Layer 1 — root cause: stable build-time encryption key.** `apps/web/.env.example` extended with a `NEXT_SERVER_ACTIONS_ENCRYPTION_KEY` block documenting (a) why this matters (Next 15 hashes server-action IDs from action-body + key; without a stable key, every `next build` regenerates a fresh random key, invalidating all action IDs in every open tab; submits from pre-redeploy tabs trigger `UnrecognizedActionError` which propagates as the white-page client exception), (b) generation snippets for PowerShell (`RandomNumberGenerator.GetBytes` → `ToBase64String`) + bash (`openssl rand -base64 32`), (c) the build-time-vs-runtime-secret distinction (set in CF dashboard → Workers & Pages → splash-web → Settings → Build → Environment variables; `wrangler secret put` is the WRONG channel because Next reads the env at `next build` time, not at runtime). `apps/web/next.config.mjs` gained a top-of-file comment block flagging `NEXT_SERVER_ACTIONS_ENCRYPTION_KEY` as a build-time env var consumed implicitly by Next. **No code change beyond docs** — Next 15.5.15 honors the env var natively. **Operator action item:** generate the 32-byte base64 key once (PowerShell snippet from .env.example), store it in the password manager, set as a build-time env var on splash-web's CF Workers Builds config. The next deploy will produce deterministic action IDs from then on; subsequent deploys keep them stable until the key is rotated. **Layer 2 — defense in depth: segment-level error boundary.** New file `apps/web/app/admin/error.tsx` (`"use client"` Next.js error boundary) covering every `/admin/*` route: `/admin/sysadmin`, `/admin/damage/[id]`, `/admin/pricing/*`, and any future action-driven page. Detects `UnrecognizedActionError` via two-token message-substring check (`"Server Action"` AND `"was not found on the server"`) and renders an "App was updated / Reload" CTA bound to `window.location.reload()`; other errors fall through to a generic "Something went wrong / Try again" CTA bound to `reset()`. Includes `console.error` for the full error and `console.warn` of the parsed stale action ID (extracted from `Server Action "<id>" was not found...`) for forward debugging. **CLAUDE.md updates:** new Critical-constraints item 7 ("Never rotate `NEXT_SERVER_ACTIONS_ENCRYPTION_KEY` without coordination") with the rotation-during-maintenance-window guidance; new Working-with-apps/web bullet documenting both layers (the env var + the boundary's role + the placement rationale: covers all `/admin/*` action-driven pages from one boundary, don't move it to nested segments). **Files modified:** `apps/web/.env.example` (38 lines added — new section), `apps/web/next.config.mjs` (8-line comment block at file top), `BRIEFS/INDEX.md` (Brief 31 row marked Completed), `CLAUDE.md` (1 new Critical-constraints item + 1 new apps/web bullet), `BUILD_STATE.md` (Last-updated bump, this entry, prioritized work list row 31). **Files created:** `apps/web/app/admin/error.tsx` (~85 lines). **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (1) **Boundary placed at `/admin/error.tsx`, not `/admin/sysadmin/error.tsx` or per-page boundaries** — every server-action write surface (sysadmin, damage detail, future action-driven pages) lives under `/admin/*`, so a single boundary covers them in one shot per the brief's guidance. (2) **Two-token substring check (`"Server Action"` + `"was not found on the server"`) instead of `instanceof` check** — the `UnrecognizedActionError` class isn't exported from any public Next.js entry point, so message-substring is the only stable detection path. The two-token check minimizes false positives over a single-token check. (3) **`console.warn` with the stale ID alongside `console.error` of the full error** — sufficient telemetry hook per the brief's optional B.5; no beacon or external tracking added. (4) **No `global-error.tsx` added** — the segment-level boundary at `/admin/error.tsx` is sufficient for the action-error path because action errors throw inside the segment's render tree (`<ActionForm>` lives under `/admin/*`). If staging testing surfaces a case where the action error escapes past the segment boundary, follow-up brief can add `apps/web/app/global-error.tsx` per B.2's contingency. (5) **`window.location.reload()` for the Reload CTA** rather than `router.refresh()` — `router.refresh()` re-fetches RSC data but keeps the same client bundle in memory, so it would re-render with the same stale action IDs and re-trigger the error on next submit. A full reload picks up the new bundle. (6) **`<button type="button">` without `type` defaulting to submit** — the boundary isn't inside a `<form>` but the explicit `type="button"` prevents any future-form-wrapping accidents. **Latent issues / forward flags:** (a) **Operator-side action item is the gating step.** Until the operator generates the key + sets it as a build-time env var on CF Workers Builds, the next deploy will still produce non-deterministic IDs and Layer 1's protection doesn't activate. Layer 2 (the boundary) provides immediate UX softening regardless. (b) **Existing tabs opened before the key is first set will still hit the boundary on next form submit after the FIRST post-key-set deploy** — those tabs were rendered with the pre-key build's action IDs, which the post-key build doesn't recognize. The boundary catches it gracefully ("Reload" CTA) — operator hits Reload once, picks up the new build, action IDs become stable from then on. (c) **Layer 2 also catches non-action errors** (any uncaught exception thrown from server actions or client components in `/admin/*`). Generic "Something went wrong / Try again" UI is the right shape for those — `reset()` re-renders the segment, which usually clears transient errors. Per brief B.4, `reset()` won't recover from a stale-action error (the same ID would resubmit), so the action-specific path stays on the Reload CTA. (d) **No `global-error.tsx` means uncaught errors OUTSIDE `/admin/*`** (e.g., from `/login`, `/`, `/signup/[location]`) still hit Next's bare client-exception fallback. Those routes don't host server actions and have minimal client logic, so the practical exposure is small. Add a global boundary if/when a non-admin route grows action-driven write surfaces. (e) **Bundle delta unobservable.** `/admin/*` route First Load JS unchanged from Brief 30 baseline (per `pnpm --filter @splash/web build`: `/admin/sysadmin` 7.06 kB / 112 kB; `/admin/damage/[id]` 3.08 kB / 108 kB; `/admin/pricing/[location]` 3.65 kB / 109 kB; `/admin/dashboard` + `/admin/damage` + `/admin/pricing` 167 B / 105 kB each — all matching the post-Brief-30 snapshot). Next.js code-splits `error.tsx` boundary components into a separate chunk loaded only when the boundary fires, so the main route bundle stays the same size. The boundary chunk itself is well under 1 kB (one client component, two render branches, minimal JSX). (f) **Next 15.5.15 honors `NEXT_SERVER_ACTIONS_ENCRYPTION_KEY` natively** — the brief asked to confirm; verified against Next.js 14.2+ behaviour (the env var has been a documented stability lever since 14.2). No experimental-block plumbing required in `next.config.mjs`. (g) **Out-of-scope items in the brief stay out-of-scope:** Cloudflare Insights beacon SRI failure (analytics dropout, not a functional issue), the unrelated 404 on `/admin/damage/VES-20260505-181329-PLUF` (stale prefetch from earlier testing), and the broader migration away from server actions toward client-side `fetch()`. **Validation:** `pnpm typecheck` 13/13 successful, 8.625s (5 cached + 8 fresh; the workers were not edited, so their fresh runs are turbo-baseline behaviour for this session). `pnpm --filter @splash/web build` succeeded — Next 15.5.15 compiled in 7.3s, 12/12 static pages generated, all type checks green. **Bundle deltas:** zero observable change vs. post-Brief-30 snapshot (error boundary code-split into its own chunk; loaded on-demand only). | Claude Code (Brief 31 session) |
| 2026-05-05 | **Brief 30 completed:** Sysadmin UX overhaul — flat 8-card list reorganized into a two-mode hub (Manage Users / Manage Tables) with operations split into per-mode section files; new `GET /sysadmin/api/audit-log` endpoint and inline filterable Activity log panel surface `sysadmin_audit_log` content for the first time. **Files modified:** `apps/sysadmin-worker/src/index.ts` — header comment lists the new endpoint; `OWNED_GET_PATHS` extended with `/sysadmin/api/audit-log`; top-level fetch dispatch routes the path to a new `handleSearchAuditLog`. The handler validates filter params (allow-list on `action` and `target_type`; UUID regex on `user_id`; lowercase regex on `location_code`; `Date.parse` shape check on `since`/`until`; ilike-sanitization drops `,()*%_` from the actor substring), composes the PostgREST query with implicit AND across constraints, paginates via `limit` (default 50, max 200) + `offset`, orders `created_at.desc`, sends `Prefer: count=estimated` and parses the trailing number off the `Content-Range` header for `total_estimate`, and returns `{ rows, total_estimate, next_offset }` (next_offset null when fewer rows returned than limit). The `user_id` filter pins target_type to `user_permissions`/`user_tool_access`/`auth.users`; the `location_code` filter pins target_type to `pricing_simple`/`locations` and matches `target_id` eq or `code/%` (the second arm catches Brief 26's composite IDs like `binghamton/bath`). `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/page.tsx` — full rewrite. Reads `searchParams.mode` (default "users"); renders `<PageBanner>` (subtitle replaced with "Manage users and tables, with full activity history below." — the previous meta-commentary about `sysadmin_audit_log` was dropped per the brief), `<ModePicker>`, the active section (`<UserOperations>` or `<TableOperations>`), and `<AuditLogPanel>` always-visible at the bottom. The 5-card Brief-7 cards + Brief 24/26/27's table-mgmt cards are no longer hosted in `page.tsx`. `BRIEFS/INDEX.md` — Brief 30 row marked Completed (2026-05-05). `BRIEFS/QUEUE.md` — Brief 30 line moved to the completed-tombstone block. `CLAUDE.md` — sysadmin glossary entry extended to describe the two-mode hub, the audit-log surface, and the URL search-param conventions (`?mode=users\|tables`, `?audit_actor=`, `?audit_action=`, `?audit_table=`, `?audit_user_id=`, `?audit_location_code=`, `?audit_offset=`). `BUILD_STATE.md` — Last updated bumped, this entry, sysadmin row in the apps/web pages table updated to reflect the new structure + bundle size, prioritized work list row 30 added with full one-line summary. **Files created:** `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/_components/OperationCard.tsx` — extracts the shared `<details>`-based card primitive + `FieldLabel` + `inputClass`/`submitClass` tokens from the old `page.tsx` so both section files can re-import without duplication. `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/_components/ModePicker.tsx` — server-component picker (Option A). Two `<Link>` buttons (no JS, no client state); the active mode gets filled splash-blue background + `aria-current="page"`. Hrefs preserve every existing search param except `mode` itself, so the operator's audit-log filters survive a mode swap. Static "5 operations" / "3 operations" subtitles per the brief. `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/_sections/UserOperations.tsx` — server component rendering 5 user-mgmt cards (Create user / Set role / Grant tool / Revoke tool / Reset password). Lifted verbatim from the old `page.tsx`; the document-delegated `PASSWORD_MATCH_SCRIPT` came along with the Reset-password card. `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/_sections/TableOperations.tsx` — server component rendering 3 table-mgmt cards wrapping the existing client islands (`AddLocationCard` / `UpdatePackageCard` / `UpdateLocationCard`). Card titles + descriptions verbatim from the old `page.tsx`. `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/_sections/AuditLogPanel.tsx` — server component. Reads `audit_actor` / `audit_action` / `audit_table` / `audit_user_id` / `audit_location_code` / `audit_offset` from `searchParams`; calls `sysadminGetJson<AuditLogResponse>` (the existing service-binding-with-URL-fallback helper) against the new endpoint. Renders a filter row above the table (GET-method `<form>` posting back to `/admin/sysadmin`; preserves the active mode via a hidden input; resets `audit_offset` to 0 on every submit) with: actor text input, action select (allow-list + "(any)"), table select (allow-list + "(any)"), `<UserPicker name="audit_user_id">` (reuses Brief 18's client island), location_code text input with `pattern="[a-z0-9_]*"`. Below the form: a compact 5-column table (When / Actor / Action / Target / Diff) with offset pagination ("Showing N–M of ~XYZ" + "Load more" link bumping `?audit_offset=`). Empty state: italic "No audit entries match these filters." Error state (worker call throws or returns null on 401/403): scoped `role="alert"` banner; the rest of the page keeps rendering. Per-row details: When = relative time (server-rendered, ISO timestamp on title attr); Actor italicizes when `actor_email === "system"`; Action mono + `_noop` actions get a `(no-op)` pill; Target = `target_type/target_id` with UUIDs truncated to first 8 chars + ellipsis + full id on title attr; Diff = `<details>` summary "View" → before/after JSON in `<pre>` blocks side-by-side (or stacked on mobile via `md:grid-cols-2`), inserts show only "after", deletes show only "before", `notes` rendered as italic small text below. **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (1) **Option A (URL search-param-driven mode picker) chosen** per the brief's recommendation — clean URL, deep-linkable, no extra JS, plays nicely with the existing server-rendered + `router.refresh()` pattern. (2) **Relative-time formatter is server-rendered**, not a client island — sufficient for v1 because the page only updates on refresh; client hydration would add weight (a `RelativeTime.tsx` `"use client"` component is a follow-up if the operator wants tick-by-tick updates). The absolute ISO timestamp lives on the title attr for hover. (3) **`AuditLogPanel` always renders for both modes** — the brief's two-mode hub diagram shows the panel at page bottom regardless of mode; operators commonly want to verify a write they just made via the mutation UI without flipping modes. (4) **Filter changes always reset `audit_offset` to 0** via a hidden `<input type="hidden" name="audit_offset" value="0">` in the filter form — pagination only makes sense relative to the current filter set; preserving it across filter changes leads to confusing empty pages. (5) **Reset link clears every `audit_*` param but keeps `mode`** — operators expect to reset the log without losing their current mode context. (6) **`UserPicker` reused as-is for the user filter** — passes `name="audit_user_id"` instead of `name="user_id"`; on initial render with `audit_user_id` already in the URL, the picker is given `defaultValue` + a placeholder `defaultLabel="(filtering — clear to change)"` because we can't roundtrip the email server-side without a second worker call (and the picker's existing contract uses `defaultLabel` for display). Operators clear + retype to change. (7) **`AuditLogResponse.next_offset` is computed server-side as `rows.length < limit ? null : offset + rows.length`** — simpler for the panel to render "Load more" conditional vs. computing it locally. (8) **No CSV export, no live updates, no JSON structural diff** per brief out-of-scope. (9) **No audit-log entry written for the audit-log read** — explicitly out-of-scope; reading the log is a super_admin observation. (10) **Allowed-action list hardcoded in both worker and apps/web filter UI**; future briefs that add a new audit `action` string need to update both lists. The worker rejects unknown action filter values with 400, which is a useful early-warning if a typo or a forgotten audit constant slips through. (11) **`OperationCard` extracted to `_components/`, not `_sections/`**, because both sections re-import it; section files are mode-specific containers, components are reusable primitives. (12) **No bottom-of-section "Refresh" button** — the operator can hard-refresh, and `router.refresh()` is already triggered by every successful mutation via `<ActionForm>`. **Latent issues / forward flags:** (a) **`PostgREST` allows duplicate top-level filter params, AND-combining them**, which is what we lean on when `user_id` is set (it adds `target_type=in.(...)` AND `target_id=eq.{uuid}` ON TOP of any explicit `table=...` filter). If the operator selects table=`pricing_simple` + a user_id, the result is empty (correct, since user_id rows can't have target_type=`pricing_simple`) — UX could surface that more obviously, e.g. as an inline "filter combo yields nothing" hint. v1 just shows the empty state. (b) **`audit_user_id` round-trip**: when filtered, the picker shows a stale "(filtering — clear to change)" instead of the email. Resolving it would need a second worker call (`/sysadmin/api/users?id=<uuid>`) which the worker doesn't currently support. Follow-up brief if the operator finds this confusing. (c) **`Content-Range` header** is read off the response and parsed for the trailing total. If Supabase ever stops sending the header (Prefer ignored, schema misconfig), `total_estimate` falls through to null and the panel renders "Showing 1–N" without the total. The Load-more link still works (next_offset is computed from rows.length, independent of the header). (d) **Schema verification**: I trusted the brief's inferred shape for `sysadmin_audit_log` (id, created_at, actor_id, actor_email, action, target_type, target_id, before, after, notes). I didn't query the DB to verify — the audit handler reads `select=*` so any extra columns flow through harmlessly to the rendered diff. The rendered table only references the documented columns. (e) **`location_code` filter regex is `[a-z0-9_]+`** matching the worker's `LOCATION_CODE_RE`. The HTML input's `pattern="[a-z0-9_]*"` is a UX hint (also accepts empty so submit-without-filter doesn't fail). (f) **No worker-side rate limiting on audit-log GETs** — the endpoint is super_admin-gated, so the operator headcount caps the abuse surface. (g) **Bundle delta within estimate.** `/admin/sysadmin` post-Brief-30: **7.06 kB / 112 kB First Load JS** (was **6.94 kB / 112 kB** post-Brief-27) — **+0.12 kB / 0 kB**. The audit panel is server-rendered; the only client weight added is one extra `UserPicker` mount in the filter row + a couple of additional `<Link>` instances. Comfortably under the brief's "audit panel + JSON diff rendering will likely add weight" caveat. (h) **No new audit-log action strings observed** beyond the allow-list during static review (couldn't query live data from headless mode); operators should flag any unknown action that surfaces a 400 from the filter UI as either a typo or a forgotten future-feature constant. **Validation:** `pnpm typecheck` 13/13 successful, 4.956s (11 cached + 2 fresh — `@splash/sysadmin-worker` and `@splash/web` invalidated). `pnpm --filter @splash/web build` succeeded — Next 15.5.15 compiled in 5.6s, 12/12 static pages generated, all type checks green. **Bundle deltas:** `/admin/sysadmin` 6.94 kB → **7.06 kB** / 112 kB → 112 kB First Load JS (+0.12 kB / 0 kB). All other route bundles unchanged from Brief 29 snapshot. | Claude Code (Brief 30 session) |
| 2026-05-05 | **Brief 29 completed:** three post-test fixes from operator end-to-end testing earlier today. **(A) Tab title cosmetic.** `apps/web/app/layout.tsx` line 28 flipped from `title: "Splash MaxPass"` to `title: "Splash Tools"`. Audit of worker `<title>` tags surfaced only customer-facing strings: `apps/damage-worker/src/render/claim-form.ts` (Vehicle Issue Report, Claim Submitted, Location Not Found — all left as-is per brief scope, customer-facing and location-specific); `apps/signup-worker/src/render/picker.ts` and `apps/signup-worker/src/render/form.ts` (location-specific Choose Package / Sign Up titles — left as-is, customer signup flow). No other admin-tool tab titles found anywhere in `apps/`. The `metadata.title` field on the Next.js root layout was the single admin-tool tab title in the entire monorepo. **(B) Add Location now writes locations row + accepts address.** `apps/sysadmin-worker/src/index.ts` `handleCreateLocation`: new `address` body field (optional, trimmed, empty→null); `site` is now required and must parse to a positive integer (used as `locations.site_number`); new pre-check on `locations?site_number=eq.{n}` returns 409 "Location already exists in locations table" on hit; new `LocationsInsertRow` payload (site_number, site=location_pretty, location=address, area_manager, regional_manager, am_email, rm_email, site_email) POSTed to `/rest/v1/locations` with `Prefer: return=representation` so the inserted `id` round-trips back; pricing_simple INSERT now happens AFTER and includes `address` as a new field on each row (the `PricingSimpleInsertRow` interface gained an `address: string \| null` member); on pricing_simple insert failure the worker issues a best-effort `DELETE /rest/v1/locations?id=eq.{insertedId}` rollback (the locations row is the only thing committed at that point); if the rollback DELETE also fails it logs an audit row with `action: "create_location_failed"`, `needs_manual_cleanup: true`, and `orphan_location_id` so the operator knows manual cleanup is required, then surfaces both errors in the 500 response. The successful-path audit row's `after` payload now includes `location_id`, `site_number`, and `address` alongside the existing `location_code` / `location_pretty` / `package_count`. Worker response shape gained `location_id` and `site_number` fields. apps/web `AddLocationCard`: `site` input gained `required` + `inputMode="numeric"` + `pattern="[0-9]+"`; helper text reworded to "Positive integer. Used as locations.site_number." A new `address` input was added to the location-level grid with helper text "Street address. Stored on locations row + propagated to pricing_simple.address." apps/web `createLocationAction`: forwards the new `address` field as `body.address`; success message now reads `Location created: <code> (#<site_number>, <package_count> packages)`. **(C) Claim form overlay clear on success.** `apps/damage-worker/src/render/claim-form.ts` `FORM_SCRIPT.showOutcome`: `setSubmitting(false)` added as the FIRST line, before the `formPage.hidden = true; outcomePage.hidden = false;` lines. This hides the previously-orphaned `#submittingOverlay` div that sits OUTSIDE both #formPage and #outcomePage and was never being toggled off on the success path. Verified `showOutcome` is called from exactly one site (`form.addEventListener('submit', ...).then(...).if (out.ok && out.body && out.body.ok)` branch) — no other call sites; the early `setSubmitting(false)` is idempotent regardless. **Files modified:** `apps/web/app/layout.tsx` (1 line), `apps/damage-worker/src/render/claim-form.ts` (1 line in showOutcome), `apps/sysadmin-worker/src/index.ts` (handleCreateLocation extended with locations pre-check + INSERT + rollback DELETE on pricing_simple failure; new `LocationsInsertRow` interface; `PricingSimpleInsertRow` gained `address` field; header comment updated to reflect Brief 29's locations-row addition), `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/_components/AddLocationCard.tsx` (site → required + numeric pattern + reworded help text; new address input with helper text), `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/actions.ts` (`CreateLocationBody` interface gained `address?: string \| null`; `createLocationAction` reads address + forwards in body; success message includes `#site_number` and reads `respSiteNumber` from worker response), `BRIEFS/INDEX.md` (row 29 marked Completed; link points at `brief-029-...md`, the canonical filename after cowork's renaming), `CLAUDE.md` (sysadmin glossary entry extended to describe Brief 29's locations-row + address behaviour and the rollback-on-failure flow). **Files created:** none. **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (1) **`address` lands in both `locations.location` and `pricing_simple.address` on initial INSERT** rather than relying on the trigger to backfill — the trigger fires `ON locations AFTER UPDATE` (not INSERT) per Brief 27's spec, so a fresh INSERT wouldn't propagate without a follow-up UPDATE. (2) **`locations.site` gets `location_pretty`, not the form's `site` text input** — per the brief's payload spec; the type confirms `locations.site` is the display name (e.g. "Binghamton") while `site_number` is the integer. `pricing_simple.site` is left unchanged (still receives `stringOrNull(body.site)`, the form's site number text). (3) **`location_code`-on-locations pre-check skipped** — the type (`packages/types/src/locations.ts`) confirms `locations` doesn't carry a `location_code` column; only `site_number` is the unique business key on that side. The brief's "whichever column locations actually uses for code" caveat resolved to "site_number only." (4) **Best-effort rollback DELETE captures the inserted id from the `Prefer: return=representation` response** — the worker reads `locInsertedRows[0]!.id` straight off the POST response, so even if Supabase doesn't return an id (older/configured-off case) the worker degrades gracefully (`needs_manual_cleanup: true`, `orphan_location_id: null` in audit). (5) **`needs_manual_cleanup` audit row is best-effort with try/catch wrapping** — if the audit insert ALSO fails (cascading failure), we don't want to mask the original 500 with a third error; the audit failure is silently swallowed and the 500 still surfaces both the pricing_simple error and the cleanup DELETE error. (6) **Site-number `pattern="[0-9]+"` on the form input is a UX hint only** — server-side validation in `handleCreateLocation` does the real `Number.isInteger() && > 0` check; the `pattern` just nudges the browser keypad on mobile. (7) **`address` is optional in the worker** but the form input doesn't carry `required` — operators may leave address blank for new locations whose street address isn't known yet. (8) **Brief filename / numbering quirk surfaced**: the brief was originally filed under `BRIEFS/brief-028-tab-title-add-location-row-claim-overlay.md` and self-titled "Brief 28", but BUILD_STATE.md row 28 was already reserved for the still-open cross-worker cache invalidation brief. The cowork tooling renumbered the live brief to Brief 29 (file is `brief-029-...md`; `brief-028-...md` is now a one-line tombstone pointing at 029). Resolution: the new BUILD_STATE.md prioritized work list row is numbered 29 (matching INDEX.md), INDEX.md row 29's link points at `brief-029-...md`, and BUILD_STATE.md row 28 (cache invalidation) is unchanged. **Latent issues / forward flags:** (a) **No locations.site_number unique constraint verified at the DB level.** The pre-check returns 409 if a row already exists, but if the constraint isn't enforced (or is enforced under a different name), a TOCTOU race between two simultaneous create-location calls could insert two rows with the same site_number. Negligible at single-operator headcount; flag if multi-operator concurrent create becomes a thing. (b) **`trg_sync_pricing_simple` trigger fires `ON locations AFTER UPDATE`, not `AFTER INSERT`.** Brief 29's INSERT path doesn't trigger the cascade, but that's by design — pricing_simple is populated explicitly by the same handler with the denormalized fields. Future locations-side UPDATEs (via Brief 27) will fire the trigger and overwrite pricing_simple's denormalized columns to match locations. (c) **`pricing_simple.site` still receives the form's site text (e.g. "34"), but `locations.site` receives `location_pretty` ("Binghamton")** — the two columns named `site` mean different things by schema design. If `trg_sync_pricing_simple` syncs locations.site INTO pricing_simple.site on UPDATE, a Brief 27 site-edit would replace pricing_simple.site's number-string with the display name. This is consistent with the existing trigger spec (Brief 26 documents pricing_simple.site as locations-synced) but worth re-verifying once the operator exercises the full create-then-update cycle in production. (d) **`address` values are not normalized.** Whatever the operator types is what gets stored in both columns. No address-shape validation, no zip parsing, no postal-service lookup. (location, area_manager, regional_manager — no permissions-side cascade), and "Locations table only — no cascade." (hrt_email, rm_group). (2) **`mla_location` is shown read-only in the context block** rather than added as a 10th editable field. The brief's editable list doesn't include it; a future brief can promote it if operators ask. (3) **`hrt_email` is rendered alongside the cascading email fields** with a distinct "Locations table only — no cascade" hint so the operator can edit it without thinking it propagates anywhere. Same for `rm_group`. (4) **Empty form value → null** for every editable field. Clearing the column (rather than leaving the prior value untouched when blank) is the more predictable behaviour because the operator's mental model is "what I see is what gets saved." The worker accepts null on every editable field. (5) **Three help-text variants instead of one repeated hint** — visually distinguishes which fields cascade where. The worker passes `select=*` through verbatim, so the type stays accurate even if the schema evolves; apps/web only consumes the columns the picker + read-only block reference. The worker accepts that; intended behaviour. (b) **`created_at` / `updated_at` columns on `locations` are assumed to exist.** The brief's Read-only-fields section lists them; I show them in the read-only context block. The Supabase locations type in `packages/types/src/locations.ts` doesn't declare them (the type was inherited from the legacy schema, which omits audit columns). If they don't actually exist on the table, the read-only block renders em-dashes — no error. (c) **`hrt_email` and `rm_group` are confirmed real** per `packages/types/src/locations.ts:27,24`; same for the 4 manager/email fields the brief lists. The brief's "if columns don't exist, hide them" caveat is moot — they all exist. If the trigger names differ or are missing, the cascade won't fire — the worker's PATCH still succeeds (it's just an UPDATE on locations); operator would notice via stale pricing_simple. Smoke-test the cascade by editing an email and checking pricing_simple in production. (f) **TOCTOU on selector** — between the existence pre-check and the PATCH, another super_admin could delete the row. Worst case: PATCH affects 0 rows; Supabase returns `[]` and we surface a 200 with `after = null`. No data corruption. Negligible at operator headcount. | 2026-05-05 | **Brief 26 completed:** Sysadmin Update Package — search-then-edit one `pricing_simple` row by composite PK (`location_code`, `pkg`), second of three pricing_simple/locations management features. **Files modified:** `apps/sysadmin-worker/src/index.ts` — header comment lists two new endpoints (GET `/sysadmin/api/pricing-simple/search` + PATCH `/sysadmin/api/pricing-simple/package`); new `OWNED_PATCH_PATHS` set; `OWNED_GET_PATHS` extended; top-level `fetch()` dispatch refactored to (a) reject the request when the path is not in any owned set, (b) enforce method per category (POST → POST, PATCH → PATCH, GET → GET), (c) extend the `isOriginAllowed` CSRF gate to PATCH (state-changing) in addition to POST, (d) route GET path `/sysadmin/api/pricing-simple/search` to the new `handleSearchPricingSimple`, (e) route PATCH path to the new `handleUpdatePackage`. New `handleSearchPricingSimple` — empty/whitespace q → `[]`; sanitises q against PostgREST `or()` separators (`,()*%_`); raw fetch to Supabase REST `pricing_simple?or=(location_code.ilike.%q%,location_pretty.ilike.%q%,site.ilike.%q%)&order=location_code.asc,sort.asc,pkg.asc&limit=50` with service-role headers; returns the array verbatim. New `handleUpdatePackage` — validates composite PK (location_code regex `[a-z0-9_]+`, pkg non-empty); rejects any of the six denormalized fields (`area_manager`, `regional_manager`, `am_email`, `rm_email`, `site_email`, `address`) with 400 + a friendly "edit via Update Location" message; coerces editable fields into a partial-PATCH payload (`pkg$` required, `single`/`flash2`/`flash5`/`sort` accept null when blank, `pricing` constrained to the five `PRICING_MODES`, `location_pretty` non-empty when present); optional `pkg_new` rename validated for non-emptiness and only applied when it differs from `pkg`; refuses an empty payload with 400 ("No editable fields supplied"). Pre-checks: `select=*` SELECT 1 by composite PK returning 404 "Package not found" on miss, captures the row for the `before` audit snapshot; if renaming, second SELECT against `(location_code, pkg_new)` returning 409 "Target package name already exists" on collision. Issues a single PATCH to `${SUPABASE_URL}/rest/v1/pricing_simple?location_code=eq.X&pkg=eq.Y` with `Prefer: return=representation` so the response carries the after-row; audit log entry `action: "update_package"`, `target_id: "${location_code}/${final_pkg}"`, `before` = pre-PATCH row, `after` = post-PATCH row. Returns `{ ok, location_code, pkg: <new-or-unchanged>, updated_at }`. `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/_lib/worker-fetch.ts` — header comment extended with Brief 26 paragraph; `sysadminPostJson` refactored to delegate to a new private `sysadminWriteJson(method, ...)`; new public `sysadminPatchJson` thin-wraps the same helper with method=PATCH (avoids 70-line copy-paste); error-message string switched from hardcoded "POST" to the active method. `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/actions.ts` — header comment lists Brief 26 + the new endpoint; import adds `sysadminPatchJson`; new `updatePackageAction` reads `location_code`/`pkg_original`/`pkg`/`location_pretty`/`pricing`/`pkg_dollar` plus the four nullable numeric fields, builds an `UpdatePackageBody` with literal `"pkg$"` key, sets `pkg_new` only when the editable `pkg` differs from `pkg_original`, coerces blanks to `null` for nullable columns, validates `sort` as a positive integer when present, calls `sysadminPatchJson("/sysadmin/api/pricing-simple/package", body)`, surfaces `{ ok: true, message: "Updated <code>/<pkg>" }` (using the worker's response body for the post-rename pkg) or `{ ok: false, error }`. `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/page.tsx` — header lists 7 cards + Brief 26; `UpdatePackageCard` import added; new `UpdatePackageOperationCard` server component wraps the client island in the existing `OperationCard` shell. `BRIEFS/INDEX.md` — Brief 26 row marked Completed (2026-05-05). `CLAUDE.md` — sysadmin glossary entry rewritten: keeps the Brief 24 Add Location summary, adds the Brief 26 Update Package summary including the editable-vs-rejected field split driven by the `trg_sync_pricing_simple` trigger, points at Brief 27 (not yet started) for the denormalized-field path, repeats the cross-worker cache invalidation TODO. `BUILD_STATE.md` — Last updated bumped, this entry, sysadmin row in the apps/web pages table updated to reflect 7 cards + new bundle size, prioritized work list row 26 marked **completed** with full one-line summary. **Files created:** `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/_components/PackageSearchPicker.tsx` — client island. Mirrors `UserPicker` (Brief 18) shape: 250ms debounced fetch to `/sysadmin/api/pricing-simple/search?q=...`, dropdown with combobox/listbox aria roles + arrow key nav + Esc + outside-click dismiss, error banner on non-2xx. Differences from UserPicker: (1) selection state is held by the **parent** (UpdatePackageCard) via `selected` + `onSelect` props rather than internally, because the parent needs the full row data to inflate the form; (2) dropdown rows render `${location_code}/${pkg}` mono-bold on the left + an "Updated YYYY-MM-DD" badge on the right + a secondary line with the location_pretty + price summary `$pkg$/single $single`; (3) selection chip shows the composite PK + price summary instead of an email. Exports `PricingSimpleSearchRow` interface for the parent's typing. `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/_components/UpdatePackageCard.tsx` — client island. Owns selection state via `useState<PricingSimpleSearchRow \| null>`. Renders the picker + a "pick a package above" hint when nothing's selected; on selection inflates the editable form (8 fields in a 2-col grid: `pkg`, `location_pretty`, `pkg$`, `single`, `flash2`, `flash5`, `sort`, `pricing`) plus a `ReadOnlyContext` block listing the 9 denormalized/audit fields with an italic note "Edits here would be reverted by the sync trigger." Form has hidden inputs for `location_code` (composite PK) + `pkg_original` (the pkg name at selection time so the worker knows what to look up before applying any rename). Wraps in `<ActionForm action={updatePackageAction} resetOnSuccess={false} onResult={...}>` — `resetOnSuccess={false}` because the form is keyed on the selection and we clear selection in `onResult` on success (the form unmounts when selection becomes null, simpler than relying on key-trick remount with stale defaultValues). Cancel link clears the selection without submitting. Form uses `key={location_code/pkg}` so picking a different row remounts and re-binds defaultValues. **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (1) **Worker uses PATCH semantically + a new `OWNED_PATCH_PATHS` dispatch set** rather than reusing POST. The brief explicitly listed "PATCH" first and offered POST only as a fallback "if sysadminPostJson only does POST". The dispatch refactor is small (5 added lines + 1 method rename) and matches REST convention; the downstream Supabase REST call is also PATCH so the verbs line up end-to-end. The CSRF `isOriginAllowed` gate now applies to both POST and PATCH for symmetry. (2) **`sysadminWriteJson(method, path, body)` extracted from sysadminPostJson** rather than duplicating the 70-line dual-mode body for sysadminPatchJson. Behaviour is identical except for the HTTP method passed through the service-binding fetch and the URL fallback fetch, plus the error-message string. The public sysadminPostJson signature is preserved. (3) **PackageSearchPicker takes selection via props from the parent**, not held internally — this keeps the parent in control of "is the form inflated" state and avoids the picker needing to drive a sibling form's mount. UserPicker holds its own selection because the surrounding form just needs a hidden user_id; PackageSearchPicker's surrounding form needs the entire row to pre-fill 8+ inputs, which is impractical to thread through hidden inputs. (4) **Read-only context block uses `<dl>` semantics** with a 2-column grid; mono on `location_code` + `updated_at`, plain on the rest. Empty/null values render as a faded em-dash so missing data is visually distinct from blank-on-purpose. (5) **`pricing` mode select is required and pre-defaults to "full"** when the row's pricing column is null — the pricing column is non-nullable in the schema (Brief 24's create-location hardcodes "full"), so this is defensive only. Operator can switch between any of the 5 valid modes (`full`, `same`, `flash5`, `flash2`, `special`). (6) **`single`, `flash2`, `flash5`, `sort` accept blank → null** because the schema permits null on these (the brief's editable-fields spec listed them as nullable). `pkg$` and `pkg` are required (`pkg$` is the headline price, `pkg` is half the composite PK). `location_pretty` is technically schema-non-null but the action only sends it when present; an empty form-input value is filtered out and the worker leaves the column unchanged. (7) **`pkg_new` is sent only when the editable `pkg` differs from `pkg_original`** — sending `pkg_new === pkg` would force the worker into the rename branch unnecessarily (one extra collision pre-check). The action elides it when equal. (8) **On save success, the parent clears selection** (via `onResult` callback) rather than re-fetching the row to refresh values. The picker returns to its empty state; operator picks the next package. `revalidatePath("/admin/sysadmin")` + `router.refresh()` re-render the page server side, but client-side selection state is cleared explicitly. This avoids "save shows old defaultValue" UX confusion. (9) **`OperationCard` description copy** mentions the editable fields directly (`pkg$, single, flash2/5, sort, pkg name, pricing mode`) so a super_admin sees what the card edits without expanding it. (10) **Cancel button is `type="button"` with onClick**, not a `<a href>` — keeps the operator on the same scroll position and avoids a full page navigation. (11) **`PRICING_MODE_OPTIONS` hardcoded as a const tuple** rather than imported from `@splash/types`'s `PRICING_MODES` — `@splash/types` is transpiled by Next but importing a value (vs. type-only) into a client component pulls the whole module's exports into the bundle; the 5-string constant is cheaper inline. **Latent issues / forward flags:** (a) **Cross-worker cache invalidation gap (TODO)** — same as Brief 24. signup-worker caches `pricing_simple_resolved` for 5 minutes; package edits surface on the customer signup form within that window without operator action. A code-comment TODO points at the gap in `handleUpdatePackage`. (b) **`pkg_original` is operator-supplied via the form**, not a server-stored token — a malicious super_admin could edit a different row by lying in the hidden input, but a malicious super_admin can already issue any SQL via Supabase. The composite PK is the only auth-relevant identifier and it's checked at the worker. Acceptable for v1. (c) **Read-only context fields show what the picker fetched at selection time** — they're not re-read during edit. If the operator edits a package and another super_admin updates the location's am_email in a parallel session, the read-only block is stale until the next picker pick. Acceptable v1; a future enhancement could re-fetch on every Save. (d) **Rename pre-check has a TOCTOU window** — a concurrent rename to the same target name could slip in between the collision SELECT and the PATCH. Worst case: Supabase returns a constraint violation 500 wrapped in our `Supabase update failed: ...` shape. No data corruption (the constraint catches it). Negligible in practice given the operator headcount. (e) **`updated_at` audit column is auto-managed by the schema's row-update trigger** (the existing pattern for pricing_simple) — the worker doesn't set it explicitly. The PATCH response with `Prefer: return=representation` reflects whatever the trigger produced, which is what we surface in the action's success message and audit log. (f) **The brief's spec for the search return shape included `address` as a top-level field on `PricingSimpleSearchRow`**, which the worker's GET endpoint passes through verbatim from Supabase. If `pricing_simple` doesn't actually have an `address` column (the schema is the source of truth and I didn't verify), the field comes back as `undefined` and the read-only context block renders an em-dash. No type error because the rows are typed `unknown`-passthrough at the worker boundary; the apps/web TS interface declares it `string | null` but null + undefined render identically in `ReadOnlyField`. Verify in production smoke. (g) **`pkg_dollar` form-field name is mapped to `"pkg$"` JSON key in the action** — same convention Brief 24 used (HTML form-name avoids the `$` to sidestep tooling that might choke on it; the action does the rename once). (h) **Bundle delta within estimate.** `/admin/sysadmin` post-Brief-26: **5.6 kB / 111 kB First Load JS** (was **3.93 kB / 109 kB** post-Brief-24) — **+1.67 kB / +2 kB**, in line with the brief's "+1-2 kB" estimate. PackageSearchPicker (~1 kB) + UpdatePackageCard inflate state (~0.6 kB) account for the delta; the form pre-fill machinery is mostly defaultValue strings which compress well.
