Adds (1) `vehicleYear` `required` attribute; (2) "Managers only" amber pill rendered next to the `customer_get_quotes` determination radio via a new `managersOnly: boolean` flag on the `DETERMINATION_CHOICES` rows + new `.pill` / `.pill-warn` CSS; (3) equipment yes/no segmented toggle defaulting to No that conditionally reveals the equipment dropdown + the malfunction checkbox (when No, `equipmentInvolved` is forced to empty string so the worker's existing `equipmentRelated = (equipmentInvolved && !== "N/A") ? 1 : 0` derivation maps to 0); (4) PIN-gated employee section with a custom overlay modal (PIN=1981 hardcoded as customer-visible obfuscation per brief; correct PIN hides the "Continue" button + reveals the employee section + submit row); (5) multi-photo widget for fourCornersPhotos + damagePhotos with thumbnail previews via `URL.createObjectURL`, per-thumb Remove links, and "+ Add another photo" button labels; single-photo widget for vinPhoto + platePhoto with "Replace photo" label after the first add; (6) `capture` attribute dropped from every photo input — browser-native chooser between camera and gallery; (7) JS-driven submit with `e.preventDefault()`, `FormData` construction (text fields via `new FormData(form)`, photos appended from JS-tracked `photos` object under canonical worker field names), `fetch` with `Accept: application/json`, "Submitting claim, please wait..." overlay, success outcome card showing `claim_id` in monospace + "Submit another claim" reload button, inline error banner above the form on non-2xx. The `EQUIPMENT_CHOICES` tuple lost the explicit "N/A" entry — the toggle's No state is now the canonical "no equipment involved" path. Inline JS lives in a module-level `FORM_SCRIPT` constant (string concatenation throughout — no JS template literals — so the outer TS template literal cannot interpolate `${...}` patterns by accident); interpolated once into the rendered HTML via `<script>${FORM_SCRIPT}</script>`. `<form novalidate>` defers all validation to the JS submit handler so HTML5 bubbles don't fire on the "Continue" button click path. `apps/damage-worker/src/index.ts` — `handleClaimSubmission` JSON success branch now returns `{ ok: true, claim_id, ...legacy success/claimId/powerAutomateSuccess/d1Success/photosUploaded keys }`; failure branch returns `{ ok: false, error, success: false }` with status 500. Browser-mode redirect path (`Accept: text/html`) unchanged from Brief 23. **Files created:** none. **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (1) **`equipmentMalfunction` checkbox folded inside the equipment-yes block** rather than left as an always-visible top-level toggle — the malfunction question only makes sense when equipment was actually involved; toggle-No forces the hidden `equipmentMalfunction` input to "false". (2) **`employeeName` is required only after PIN unlock**, set via JS on the unlock event — keeping `required` on a hidden field would fire the bubble on the Continue path or before the operator could see the field. (3) **PIN gate uses a custom overlay div, not `<dialog>`** — broader browser compat (older iOS Safari especially), simpler styling, and no polyfill needed. Cancel + Continue buttons + Enter-key submit; wrong PIN shows inline error and clears the input; no rate limiting (PIN is obfuscation, not auth). (4) **JSON success shape mirrors both `ok`/`claim_id` AND legacy `success`/`claimId`** rather than picking one — the form's JS reads `body.claim_id || body.claimId`; future cleanup can drop the legacy keys when the operator confirms no programmatic caller is parsing them. (5) **Photo widget uses one hidden `<input type="file">` per section** rather than a single shared input that gets re-targeted — simpler event wiring, no race between concurrent clicks. `accept="image/*"` only; no `capture`; no `multiple` (one-at-a-time UX is deliberate per brief). (6) **`URL.createObjectURL` thumbnails are revoked on re-render** — hygiene, not load-bearing for short-lived form sessions. (7) **Submit-time validation order:** sync the equipment-toggle's conditional `required` first, then `form.checkValidity()` (browser bubble for missing required fields), then JS-side photo-count validation since photos aren't native form inputs anymore. Photo-missing errors render in the inline error banner above the form. (8) **"Submit another claim" reset is `window.location.reload()`** per brief — simplest correct path; the JS state machine (photos object, thumbUrls, PIN-gate visibility, equipment toggle) doesn't have a single reset hook. (9) **`form.action="/claims-api/submit-claim"` retained** even though submit is JS-driven — graceful degradation if JS is disabled (extremely unlikely in 2026 but cheap to keep). (10) **`novalidate` on `<form>`** — defers validation to the explicit `validateBeforeSubmit()` so HTML5 bubbles don't fire on the Continue button click path or while the employee section is hidden. (11) **Equipment "N/A" choice removed from the dropdown** — the toggle's No state is the canonical "no equipment involved" path; keeping "N/A" alongside Yes would be redundant and confusing. The worker's `!== "N/A"` clause stays intact (defense in depth) but no longer has anything to match. **Latent issues / forward flags:** (a) **No client-side photo size cap** — the worker delegates to the CF Workers 100 MB request body limit; a customer with a stack of HEIF originals could theoretically exceed that. Pre-resize via canvas or HEIC→JPEG conversion is out of scope per brief. (b) **PIN=1981 is in the worker bundle and rendered HTML's `<script>`** — visible in DevTools. Brief explicitly classified this as obfuscation, not auth. If a real auth flow lands later, a separate brief replaces this with a Supabase short-lived token gate. (c) **Submit overlay is a full-viewport opaque-ish overlay**, not a spinner — kept simple. If submission stalls (slow upload), the operator sees only "Submitting claim, please wait...". Future polish: progress indicator showing upload percentage. (d) **`form.checkValidity()` includes still-hidden fields** — mitigated by the linear PIN gate flow (employee section is the only conditionally-hidden block, and the submit button is hidden until PIN unlock); the conditional-required pattern on `equipmentInvolved` (toggled true only when equipment-yes is selected) handles the in-section conditional case. (e) **Worker JSON failure does NOT include partial state** (whether D1 succeeded, whether PA POSTed) — only `body.error`. If a future smoke test wants per-stage diagnostics for the customer-facing form, add an `?debug=1` opt-in branch. (f) **CSRF posture unchanged** — `POST /claims-api/submit-claim` remains public, no `isOriginAllowed` (intentional per Brief 23). The JSON failure shape change doesn't widen the surface. (g) **Bundle delta within estimate.** Damage-worker post-Brief-25: **1644.95 KiB / 372.70 KiB gzip** (was **1628.16 KiB / 368.53 KiB gzip** post-Brief-23) — **+16.79 KiB raw / +4.17 KiB gzip**, within the brief's 5-10 KiB estimate; comfortably under the 3 MiB compressed CF Workers free-tier limit. Driven by the inline `FORM_SCRIPT` (PIN gate + photo widget + equipment toggle + JS submit + outcome card) plus added CSS for the new visual components. **Validation:** `pnpm typecheck` 13/13 successful, 1.906s (12 cached + 1 fresh — only `@splash/damage-worker` invalidated by the renderClaimForm rewrite + index.ts JSON shape edit; all other packages cache-hit). `pnpm --filter @splash/damage-worker exec wrangler deploy --dry-run` succeeded. Bindings inventoried: DB / R2_BUCKET / IMAGES. | Claude Code (Brief 25 session) |
| 2026-05-05 | **Brief 24 completed:** Sysadmin Add Location feature — direct pricing_simple bulk insert from `/admin/sysadmin`, first of three planned pricing_simple/locations management features (Briefs 25 + 26 deferred). **Files modified:** `apps/sysadmin-worker/src/index.ts` — header comment block updated to list the new endpoint; `OWNED_POST_PATHS` extended with `/sysadmin/api/pricing-simple/create-location`; new switch arm dispatches to `handleCreateLocation`; new `handleCreateLocation` function with full validation per spec (location_pretty non-empty, location_code regex `/^[a-z0-9_]+$/`, uniqueness pre-check via Supabase REST `pricing_simple?location_code=eq.<code>&limit=1` returning 409 "Location code already in use" on hit, packages array length >= 1, per-package validation of pkg non-empty + pkg$/single/flash2/flash5 non-negative numbers + sort positive integer when present + duplicate-pkg detection, optional email shape validation `/^[^@\s]+@[^@\s]+\.[^@\s]+$/` for site_email/am_email/rm_email); on valid input, builds a `PricingSimpleInsertRow[]` (one row per package, location-level fields repeated, `pricing: 'full'` hardcoded, todaydate/updated_at omitted to fall through to Postgres defaults) and POSTs to `${SUPABASE_URL}/rest/v1/pricing_simple` with `Prefer: return=representation` (atomic — all rows or none); on 2xx writes a `logSysadminAudit` entry with `action: "create_location"`, `target_type: "pricing_simple"`, `target_id: location_code`, `before: null`, `after: { location_code, location_pretty, package_count }` and returns `{ ok: true, location_code, package_count }`; new module-local `nonNegativeNumber` helper (accepts numbers or numeric strings, rejects NaN/Infinity/negatives). `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/page.tsx` — header comment lists 6 cards; `AddLocationCard` import added; new `AddLocationOperationCard` server component wraps the client island in the existing `OperationCard` shell. `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/actions.ts` — header comment extended; new `createLocationAction` reads location-level fields + iterates the 7 standard package indices reading `pkg_<i>_include`/`pkg_<i>_pkg`/`pkg_<i>_pkg_dollar`/`pkg_<i>_single`/`pkg_<i>_flash2`/`pkg_<i>_flash5`/`pkg_<i>_sort`, skips rows whose include checkbox isn't checked, builds the JSON body with literal `"pkg$"` key, POSTs via `sysadminPostJson("/sysadmin/api/pricing-simple/create-location", body)`, surfaces `{ ok: true, message: "Location created: <code> (<count> packages)" }` or `{ ok: false, error }`; helper `STANDARD_PACKAGES` constant + `fieldNumber` + `fieldOptionalNullable` added. `BRIEFS/INDEX.md` — Brief 24 row marked Completed (2026-05-05); Briefs 25 + 26 added as Not started. `BRIEFS/QUEUE.md` — brief-024 line commented out. `BUILD_STATE.md` — bumped Last updated; this entry; pages table sysadmin row updated to reflect 6 cards + new bundle size; prioritized work list rows added for 24/25/26. `CLAUDE.md` — sysadmin glossary entry extended to mention Brief 24's Add Location feature + the cross-worker cache-invalidation TODO. **Files created:** `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/_components/AddLocationCard.tsx` — client island. `useState<boolean[]>` tracks per-row include state (default-on for the four standard-priced rows: bubble_bath/ultra_bath/bath/express; default-off for ext_exterior/extreme/works); the include checkbox's `onChange` flips the corresponding entry, which drives both `disabled={!isIncluded}` and `required={isIncluded}` on the per-row price inputs (mirrors the conditional-required pattern from UploadDocumentCard via Brief 20). Location-level fields (location_pretty, location_code with `pattern="[a-z0-9_]+"`, site, area_manager, regional_manager, site_email/am_email/rm_email) render as a 2-column grid. Packages render as a 7-row grid with desktop columns Include/Package/pkg$/single/flash2/flash5/sort; mobile collapses to 1 column. Default values pre-fill flash2 = "2.00" and flash5 = "5.00" on every row, sort 1-4 on the four standard-priced rows. Hidden `<input name="pkg_<i>_pkg">` carries the package name (read-only display). Submit wrapped in `<ActionForm action={createLocationAction}>` so the page-level UX matches the existing five cards (inline success/error, router.refresh on ok). **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (1) **Worker uses raw `fetch` to Supabase REST**, not the supabase-js client, for the bulk insert. Brief explicitly specified the REST POST shape with `Prefer: return=representation` and the array body; raw fetch makes the literal `"pkg$"` JSON key unmistakable in code review. The audit log write below it still uses `createServiceClient(env)` for parity with the rest of the worker. (2) **Pre-check uniqueness on `location_code` only**, not the full composite (location_code, pkg) PK. The PK constraint catches inserts with duplicate (code, pkg) anyway; the pre-check exists so the UI can render a friendly 409 "Location code already in use" instead of letting Supabase return its own constraint-violation text. The race window between pre-check and insert is harmless (a second concurrent create with the same code 500s on PK violation; no data corruption possible because the array POST is atomic). (3) **`nonNegativeNumber` accepts numeric strings** (e.g., "12.50") in addition to JS numbers — the apps/web client island's `<input type="number">` actually submits a string in `FormData` even though it renders a number widget; the action coerces in `fieldNumber`, but defensive coercion at the worker boundary is cheap. (4) **`sort` validation requires positive integer** — the brief said "positive integer; if blank coerce to null"; non-integer floats reject with 400. (5) **Email regex is intentionally simple** (`/^[^@\s]+@[^@\s]+\.[^@\s]+$/`); the brief allowed "or similar simple regex". Captures common typos without overreach (RFC 5322 is impractical; this is shape-only). (6) **Empty/whitespace optional fields coerce to null** via `stringOrNull` (worker) + `fieldOptionalNullable` (action) so the JSON body either includes a real value or omits the field entirely (the worker also accepts null for parity). (7) **`pkg$` literal column name preserved end-to-end:** TS interface `PricingSimpleInsertRow["pkg$"]`, JSON body literal `"pkg$"` key, FormData field name `pkg_<i>_pkg_dollar` (sanitized for HTML — `$` is legal in form names but ESM modules and most HTML5 validators treat it cleanly while bracket-notation in JS handles it; using `pkg_dollar` in form names sidesteps any tooling that might choke on `$`). The action maps `pkg_<i>_pkg_dollar` → `"pkg$"` exactly once at the JSON-body step. (8) **`pricing: 'full'` is hardcoded server-side**, never reads from body — operator has no way to create a location at any other initial mode (matches brief). (9) **`createLocationAction` returns the worker's `location_code` + `package_count` from the response body** (not just echoing the input); falls back to inputs if the worker's response shape is wrong. The success message is `"Location created: <code> (<count> packages)"`. (10) **Disabled price inputs on unchecked rows** rather than removing them from the DOM — keeps the column-aligned grid layout stable when toggling. The action treats `pkg_<i>_include = null` (unchecked) as "skip row entirely"; whether the disabled inputs submit a value is irrelevant. (11) **No client-side row count display** ("3 of 7 packages selected") — the worker already reports the count back in the success message; an inline counter would just duplicate. **Latent issues / forward flags:** (a) **Cross-worker cache invalidation gap (TODO).** signup-worker caches `pricing_simple_resolved` for 5 minutes via `caches.default`; sysadmin-worker has no way to bust that cache today. New locations created via this brief surface on the customer signup form within 5 minutes (cache TTL) without operator intervention. The brief flagged this and listed "manual cache-clear button" as a Brief item — not added to INDEX.md as a numbered brief yet (operator decision); a code-comment TODO in `handleCreateLocation` points at the gap. (b) **Custom (non-standard) package names not supported in v1.** The 7 standard packages (bubble_bath/ultra_bath/bath/express/ext_exterior/extreme/works) are baked into AddLocationCard. Operator who needs an ad-hoc package can use a one-shot SQL or wait for Brief 25 (Update Package). The form's help text mentions this. (c) **Edit-after-create flow** is Brief 25's responsibility — Brief 24 is create-only. The card has no "Modify location" affordance. (d) **`getActiveLocationByCode`-equivalent for pricing_simple does NOT exist** in `@splash/db-supabase`'s `pricing.ts`; the worker's pre-check is inlined raw fetch. Adding a typed helper (`locationExistsByCode(client, code) -> boolean`) is a small follow-up and would also benefit Brief 25's search-by-location_code path. Not added in this brief to keep scope tight. (e) **No CSV/Excel bulk import** — out of scope per brief. (f) **No soft-delete / archive flow** — out of scope. (g) **`PricingSimpleRowWithRawPrices` in `packages/types/src/pricing.ts` carries 'NAME UNVERIFIED' caveats from the original port** that no longer apply — `pkg$/single/flash2/flash5` are now confirmed real columns by virtue of Brief 24's INSERT contract. A small docs cleanup brief could remove those caveats; not done here to keep the scope tight. (h) **`logSysadminAudit` accepts `actor` as a `{ id, email }` object** matching `AuthUser`-lite; the worker's `actor` local already has that shape from the top-of-`fetch` `auth.session` extraction, so passing it directly works (no adaptation needed). (i) **Bundle delta:** `/admin/sysadmin` 161 B → 3.93 kB / 105 kB → 109 kB First Load JS (+3.77 kB / +4 kB). Driven by AddLocationCard's React-state include toggles (7 rows × 1 useState slot + onChange handler each; the React state machinery itself is the dominant cost, not the markup). The brief's estimate of "+1-2 kB" was low; the seven independently-toggleable rows + the conditional `required`/`disabled` propagation pushed it higher. **Validation:** `pnpm typecheck` 13/13 successful, 5.684s (10 cached + 3 fresh — apps/sysadmin-worker, apps/web, and the propagated turbo invalidations); `pnpm --filter @splash/web build` succeeded — Next 15.5.15 compiled in 5.9s, 12/12 static pages generated, all type checks green. **Bundle deltas:** `/admin/sysadmin` 3.93 kB / 109 kB First Load JS (was 161 B / 105 kB pre-Brief-24 in this build snapshot). All other routes unchanged. | Claude Code (Brief 24 session) |
| 2026-05-05 | **Brief 23 completed:** customer claim form ported to damage-worker — closes decision 9. **Files created:** `apps/damage-worker/src/render/claim-form.ts` — exports `renderClaimForm`, `renderThanksPage`, `renderClaimNotFound`, and `htmlResponse`. Single-page port of the legacy two-section form (legacy multi-step JS, animated bubbles, drag-drop file zones, custom toggle switch, JS modals, and staff-password gate dropped per brief's "keep simple but correct" guidance). Splash-blue gradient header preserved, sourcing the logo via `ASSETS.logoWhite`. Inline `<script>` mirrors the equipment-malfunction checkbox into a hidden `equipmentMalfunction` input so the worker continues to read the legacy `"true"/"false"` string verbatim. EQUIPMENT_CHOICES + DETERMINATION_CHOICES inlined from legacy. **Files modified:** `apps/damage-worker/src/index.ts` — route-table comment updated to list the two new GETs and the dual-mode POST; two new public-dispatch branches inserted between `/claims-api/photo/*` and `/manage/api/*`; `handleRenderClaimForm` + `handleRenderThanks` added after `dispatchManageApi`. Both validate the slug via `getActiveLocationByCode` and 404 with friendly HTML on miss. `handleClaimSubmission` made dual-mode: top-of-handler `browserMode = request.headers.get("Accept")?.includes("text/html")` flag drives `Response.redirect(..., 303)` on success (target `/claims/{slug}/thanks?id=<claimId>`) and on the catch branch (target `/claims/{slug}?error=<encoded>`). JSON callers (no `text/html` in Accept) continue to receive the existing JSON shape unchanged. Error-path slug recovery uses Referer header regex `/^\/claims\/([^/]+)/` because `request.formData()` is single-shot and may have already failed. **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (1) **Field names match what `handleClaimSubmission` actually reads, not what the brief enumerated.** Brief listed `customerMailingAddress`, `damageDescription`, `preexistingDamage`, `staffNotes`, `submittedBy`; worker reads `mailingAddress`, `issueDescription`, `preExistingDamage`, `employeeName` (no `staffNotes` — built worker-side from `customerTold` + `customerDemeanor`). Brief explicitly told me the worker is source of truth ("Field names MUST match what handleClaimSubmission reads"); the brief's enumerated list was a recall miss. Form posts the legacy field names verbatim and hits the worker's existing parse pipeline cleanly. All 24 fields audit-matched against the worker's reads — zero drift. (2) **JSON ↔ 303 dual-mode keyed off `Accept` header**, not a `redirect=html` form field — Accept is what every browser sends automatically and JSON callers send `Accept: application/json`. Edge case: a programmatic caller with browser-shaped Accept gets redirected — flagged latent (no such caller in monorepo today). (3) **303 (See Other) over 302** for the POST→GET-on-success idiom — forces GET on the redirect target, dodges duplicate-submit-on-Back-button re-POST footgun. (4) **Thanks page is a separate `GET /claims/{slug}/thanks` route**, not inline POST response — bookmarkable URL with `?id=<claim-id>`, survives reload, dodges the back-button re-POST. (5) **Invalid slug renders 404 with friendly HTML** rather than 200 — differentiates "I made a typo" from "I'm logged in" for any external healthcheck the operator might wire up. HTML body is identical regardless. (6) **Required attrs match brief spec exactly:** customerName / customerPhone / vehicleMake / vehicleModel / issueDescription / determination / employeeName + the four photo inputs (legacy parity). Optional: vehicleColor / licensePlate / mailingAddress / customerTold / customerDemeanor / customerEmail / vehicleYear / membershipNumber / preExistingDamage. equipmentInvolved kept required for legacy parity (brief didn't enumerate it). (7) **`equipmentMalfunction` is a UI checkbox + hidden input** mirrored by a tiny inline `<script>`; worker reads the hidden `"true"/"false"` value. Only JS in the page. (8) **Visual port is simplified.** Splash-blue gradient header + white card + light-blue gradient page kept; bubbles / multi-step / drag-drop zones / JS modals dropped. Net code drop: ~700 lines of JS eliminated; ~250 lines HTML/CSS retained. (9) **Error-bounce slug recovery via Referer header** rather than `request.clone().formData()` — clone after consume throws, and the catch branch is most often hit precisely because `formData()` itself failed. Fallback slug `"unknown"` renders the not-found card; rare. (10) **URL-bounce caps:** `?error=` 240 chars, `?id=` 64 chars. Defends against malicious or malformed bounce content; `escHtml` defends against the rest. **Latent issues / forward flags:** (a) **`isOriginAllowed` not enforced on `POST /claims-api/submit-claim`** — public endpoint, intentional (legacy parity). Customer posts from arbitrary device-bookmarked URLs without referrer policy. CSRF risk negligible since no auth state to forge from cross-origin script. Worth noting for a future CSRF-posture brief. (b) **`getActiveLocationByCode` runs on every form GET, every thanks GET, and every POST submit** for `location_pretty` resolution. Three D1 reads per claim flow. Future optimization: cache active-locations table at module load. (c) **`location_pretty` posted by the form is not authoritative** — the worker re-resolves at POST time. Defense against DevTools edits is automatic. (d) **`equipmentMalfunction` parses `String === "true"`** — anything else is false. New form + legacy form emit lowercase; non-form callers should be aware. (e) **Programmatic caller with `text/html` in Accept would get 303 redirect** instead of JSON. No such caller today. Mitigation if it surfaces: `?json=1` query opt-out. Out of scope. (f) **Browser-mode error path caps message at 240 chars.** Realistic worker errors are short; cap is a safety bound. (g) **No 1MB / 100MB upload-size guard at form level** — legacy didn't have one either; CF Workers caps at 100 MB. Customers exceeding that see a CF error page, not the form's banner. **`handleClaimSubmission` response reconciliation (Report q1):** browser submit → 303 redirect; programmatic JSON caller → existing JSON shape. **Where renderDamageForm landed (Report q2):** `apps/damage-worker/src/render/claim-form.ts` (extracted module — too large to inline cleanly even after simplification). **Field-name audit (Report q3):** all 24 form fields match the worker's reads at index.ts:1117-1146 (full table in the brief Outcome). **Bundle-size delta (Report q4):** post-Brief-23 damage-worker bundle is **1628.16 KiB / 368.53 KiB gzip**. No clean pre-Brief baseline was captured for an exact delta — the brief's 10-30 KiB gzip estimate is consistent with the observed payload. Comfortably under CF's 3 MiB compressed free-tier limit. **Validation:** `pnpm typecheck` 13/13 successful, 2.707s (12 cached + 1 fresh — only `@splash/damage-worker` invalidated). `pnpm --filter @splash/damage-worker exec wrangler deploy --dry-run` succeeded. Bindings inventoried: DB / R2_BUCKET / IMAGES. Staging routes for `staging.splashcarwashes.info/claims/*` were already wired in Brief 16 — no wrangler.toml change. | Claude Code (Brief 23 session) |
| 2026-05-05 | **Brief 22 completed:** recent-notes sub-box added inside `/admin/damage/[id]`'s SummaryCard with a jump-to-add-note anchor button. **Files modified:** `apps/web/app/admin/damage/[id]/page.tsx` — `SummaryCard` props extended with `activity: ClaimActivityRow[]`; new server-rendered `RecentNotesBox` sub-component renders after `ApprovalDetails` using the same `mt-4 rounded-splash-md border border-gray-light bg-sudsy-blue-soft/30 p-4` idiom. Box header carries the "Recent notes" label on the left and a small `<a href="#add-note">Add note</a>` styled as a white-bg + splash-blue-text + splash-blue-bordered button (mirrors the no-access-card "Back to Dashboard" idiom from /admin/sysadmin). Empty state: `<p className="text-sm italic text-splash-navy/60">No notes yet.</p>`. Populated state: up to 3 notes (filtered by `activity_type === "note"`, sorted desc by `created_at` with id-desc tie-break, sliced to 3) rendered as a `<ul>` of `<li>` items — timestamp (mono `YYYY-MM-DD HH:mm` via existing `formatDateTime`), actor name (bold), note text (`whitespace-pre-line`). When notes exist, a "View all activity ↓" link anchors to `#activity` at the bottom-right. `AddNoteCard` wrapper `<div>` gains `id="add-note"`. `ActivityTimelineCard` wrapper `<div>` gains `id="activity"`. Top-of-file section comment updated: section 3 (SummaryCard appends Recent notes), section 7 (timeline carries `id="activity"`), section 8 (add-note carries `id="add-note"`). `apps/web/app/globals.css` — added `html { scroll-behavior: smooth; }` with a Brief 22 comment. Affects every anchor jump site-wide; harmless on routes without anchors. **Files created:** none. RecentNotesBox is implemented inline as a server component within `page.tsx` (single-use, mirroring `ApprovalDetails`/`AuditStamps` siblings). **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (1) **Placement: inside the SummaryCard at the bottom**, after ApprovalDetails — best serves the brief's "alongside the claim summary" goal and reuses the existing sub-box visual idiom rather than introducing a third stacked card above the Move-forward section. (2) **"Add note" button styling: white-bg + splash-blue text + splash-blue border** — secondary affordance treatment that doesn't compete with the primary splash-blue-fill submit buttons in the transitions section or the AddNoteCard's own submit at the bottom. (3) **"View all activity ↓" anchor link added** as the optional brief feature; renders only when notes exist (no point pointing at an empty timeline). (4) **Sort defensively even though the worker SELECTs ORDER BY created_at DESC, id DESC** — `ActivityTimelineCard` itself defensively re-sorts (page.tsx:1162-1166), so RecentNotesBox matches that posture rather than depending on a fetch-order invariant the worker layer could change without the UI layer noticing. (5) **`whitespace-pre-line` on note text** — matches the timeline's own note rendering; preserves multi-line notes typed into the textarea. (6) **`scroll-behavior: smooth` applied globally on `html`** rather than scoped to a class on the anchor target. The CSS property only takes effect on the scrolling element (typically `html` or `body`); putting it on the target itself has no effect. The brief offered the scoped fallback but it wouldn't actually work as written. Global is the simplest correct path; the app uses very few anchor jumps so side effects are minimal. (7) **`id="activity"`** added even though only the optional "View all activity ↓" link consumes it today — tiny markup; future briefs can deep-link into the timeline without re-touching this file. (8) **No client island** — notes list, jump button, and timeline-anchor link are all server-rendered HTML/CSS; browser-native smooth scroll + plain `<a href="#…">` anchors avoid any "use client" need. Bundle delta is zero. **Latent issues / forward flags:** (a) **Empty-state UX placement.** When the claim has zero notes, the box still renders the title + "Add note" button alongside the "No notes yet." line. The brief's empty-state spec was interpreted as "instead of an empty bulleted list", not "instead of the entire box including the jump button" — the jump button is more useful when the claim has zero notes than when it has many. (b) **`View all activity ↓` link in populated state only.** With zero notes the link is hidden — but the operator can still scroll, and the empty-notes case already implies a sparse claim. (c) **Notes list cap is 3, hardcoded.** Future briefs that want "5 most recent" or a per-user pref can lift the constant. (d) **`formatDateTime` slice trick** preserves the worker's DB-side ISO without timezone shift; works for both "YYYY-MM-DD HH:mm:ss" (D1) and "YYYY-MM-DDTHH:mm:ss(.sss)Z" (Postgres) shapes since both have HH:mm at offsets 11-15. (e) **Smooth-scroll global rule** affects every anchor jump site-wide. Only this brief's two anchors (#add-note, #activity) consume it today. If the app ever ships a feature where instant jump is preferable, wrap that surface in a container with `scroll-behavior: auto` to override. (f) **Bundle size unchanged at 3.08 kB / 108 kB First Load JS** for `/admin/damage/[id]` — RecentNotesBox is server-only markup, the anchor-link CSS is plain Tailwind, and the smooth-scroll rule is in globals.css (already part of the layout). ApprovalDetails picks up a Brief 20 comment but the existing populated-fields gate already handles the defensive null check (no behavior change — the worker's clearApprovalDetails fix is the load-bearing piece). The inline `UploadDocumentCard` and `DocumentEditForm` are gone; both replaced by imports of new client islands (see below). `apps/web/app/admin/damage/[id]/actions.ts` — `editDocumentAction` wrapped in try/catch as defensive cover against E394 (uncaught exception in a server action surfaces as "An unexpected response was received from the server"); catch branch returns a serializable `{ ok: false, error }` and console.errors the cause. `apps/web/app/admin/_components/ActionForm.tsx` — adds an optional `onResult: (result) => void` prop. The callback is stored in a ref so its identity doesn't have to be stable; a new useEffect fires the callback on every fresh result. Used by DocumentEditDetails to close its `<details>` on success; reusable elsewhere. `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/actions.ts` — `grantToolAction` and `revokeToolAction` read the worker's new `changed` field via a small `readChanged(body)` helper and surface distinct copy: green "Granted X" vs. green "X was already granted (no change)" (visual treatment stays green per brief — message text carries the nuance; the operator gets a clear "nothing actually changed" signal without an alarming red banner). `PRE_DEPLOY_SYSADMIN.md` — smoke test 4/5 wording updated to reflect `changed: true|false` response shape and the noop audit row. **Files created:** `apps/web/app/admin/damage/_components/UploadDocumentCard.tsx` — client island wrapping the upload form. `useState` for `docType` and `payToType` drives conditional `required` attrs: amount + pay_to_type required when docType="Quote"; vendor + vendor_address required when docType="Quote" AND payToType="vendor". The form's container `<div>` carries `id="upload-document"` (anchor target for the no-quotes hint card). Receipt rows stay loose. `apps/web/app/admin/damage/_components/DocumentEditDetails.tsx` — client island wrapping the per-document Edit affordance. Owns the `<details>` open state via useState (vs. the prior server-rendered `<details>`); on a successful save (via ActionForm's new `onResult` callback) sets open=false so the panel collapses (Bug 8 fix). Quote-row required attrs match the upload card. Cancel button now closes locally instead of navigating. **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (1) **Worker response shape: rename `was_new`/`was_present` → `changed`** rather than retain both. The only consumer is the apps/web action which moves in lockstep, so backwards-compat shims would be dead code. (2) **No-op audit row uses distinct action names** (`grant_tool_noop` / `revoke_tool_noop`) instead of reusing the success action with a `notes` discriminator — clean SQL filtering and unambiguous when scanning the log. (3) **clearApprovalDetails scope: 18 worker transitions flagged**, distinguished from the 5 intra-workflow transitions where the approval is still valid (Submitted-for-Payment → Check-Request-Submitted, Check-Issued → Check-Request-Submitted, In-House-Repaired → In-House-Parts-Ordered, Closed-Approved/No Response → Check-Request-Submitted). The "Pending RM Review → Pending GM Review" rm-role send-back and "No Responsibility → Pending GM Review" rm-role reopen also got the flag defensively (no approval should be present at those stages, but clearing is a no-op when columns are already null). (4) **Activity log note: `[Reset approval details on revert]` suffix** appended to whatever the operator typed (or used as the entire note when they didn't type one) — keeps the timeline self-describing without requiring a new activity_type and the D1 CHECK rebuild that would entail. (5) **Stamps reset is part of clearApprovalDetails** — the brief listed gm/rm/ceo audit stamps as part of the clearing scope; bundled with the approval-detail nulls in a single UPDATE SET clause so it's atomic. Future stamp-only reverts (none today) would need a separate flag. (6) **ActionForm `onResult` callback rather than per-call useActionState replication** — extending the shared wrapper is cheaper than duplicating its state machinery in DocumentEditDetails. The ref-based callback storage means the parent doesn't have to memoize the handler. (7) **Edit form: required attrs enforce Quote shape on save**, not inheritance from the existing row. The form pre-fills with defaultValue so an unmodified save re-submits the existing values; an explicit clear is treated as a misconfiguration and the worker rejects with a useful 400 (surfaced inline). Operators can't accidentally clear amount on a Quote row anymore. (8) **Defensive try/catch on editDocumentAction only** — the brief specifically called out E394 on edit. The other four damage actions follow the same shape and don't need wrapping today, but the pattern is now established if the same symptom appears elsewhere. (9) **The dcRole transition filter on the damage detail page stays dropped** (Brief 18 condition); not restored here. The DcRoleDebugLine also stays. Both belong to a separate cleanup brief, not Brief 20. (10) **Bug 4 hint card replaces the entire form** for that transition row, not just the disabled state — eliminates "why is this greyed out" confusion entirely. The action's label + target status are still rendered so the operator knows what they're being asked to enable. (11) **CLAUDE.md addition is deferred** — Brief 20's no-op-detection pattern is reusable but the only place it currently applies is sysadmin's grant/revoke. Codifying it in CLAUDE.md without a second reference instance feels premature; flagged for future extraction if a similar pattern lands elsewhere. **Latent issues / forward flags:** (a) **Receipt-row Quote-shape validation gap.** The worker now strictly enforces Quote required fields; Receipt rows stay loose (amount/pay_to_type/vendor optional). Per brief, this is intentional — Receipts don't drive the check-request PDF — but a Receipt with a vendor pay-to and no vendor_address is silently allowed. Cleanup brief candidate if Receipts ever start driving downstream flows. (b) **Edit form: `vendor` field on a Quote-customer row is no longer marked required by the UI**, but remains optional on the worker side too. If pay-to is `customer`, vendor is informational-only. Symmetric across upload/edit. (c) **`canMutateDocument` UI gate** in apps/web/app/admin/damage/_lib/permissions.ts is unchanged; DocumentEditDetails only renders when the gate passes. (d) **router.refresh() after edit-save** still fires from ActionForm's existing useEffect — Brief 20's `onResult` is additive, not replacing. The `<details>` close-on-success happens in parallel with the refresh; no race because both target the same React tree update cycle. (e) **`<details>` open state via React** vs. native HTML — switching from server-rendered `<details>` to controlled means open/close transitions go through React state. Some browsers animate `<details>` natively; the controlled version may lose that micro-animation. Acceptable trade-off for the close-on-save fix. (f) **clearApprovalDetails flag on UI side has no rendering** today — the UITransition mirror carries the flag for code-review parity and so a future "This action will reset approval details" hint can be added without another sync round. | 2026-05-04 | **Brief 19 completed:** server-action ActionResult + router.refresh() pattern landed on damage detail and sysadmin. Operator's first end-to-end staging test (post-Brief 18) showed actions completing on the server (DB updates, worker logs OK) but the browser sitting on the pre-action URL until manual reload — the Next 15 server-action `redirect()` response wasn't reliably propagating as a visible client navigation in the OpenNext-on-Cloudflare-Workers runtime. Pricing admin was unaffected because it uses direct client `fetch()` + `setState`. Brief 19 flips damage and sysadmin to the `useActionState` + `router.refresh()` pattern that matches Next 15 + React 19 docs for inline post-action feedback. **Files created:** `apps/web/app/admin/_components/ActionForm.tsx` — shared `"use client"` wrapper exporting `ActionResult` type and `<ActionForm>` component. `useActionState(action, null)` dispatches the action; `useEffect` calls `router.refresh()` on every fresh `ok` result; success/error rendered inline as `<p role="status">` / `<p role="alert">` under the form; `resetOnSuccess` (default true) keys the `<form>` off `ok:<message>` so React remounts the subtree on success and clears uncontrolled inputs; `encType` passthrough so multipart forms work; `data-pending` attribute on the form for downstream styling hooks. **Files modified:** `apps/web/app/admin/damage/[id]/actions.ts` — all five actions (transition, addNote, uploadDocument, editDocument, deleteDocument) signature changed to `(prevState, formData) => Promise<ActionResult>` and bodies return typed results instead of `redirect()`. `errorRedirect` helper + `next/navigation` import gone. `revalidatePath` calls retained on success. Brief 18's `[damage-action]` diagnostic logging retained. Per-action success messages are specific (e.g. `"Status updated to <to_status>"`, `"<doc_type> uploaded"`). `apps/web/app/admin/damage/[id]/page.tsx` — wrapped TransitionForm, AddNoteCard, DocumentEditForm, ConfirmDeleteBanner's delete form, and UploadDocumentCard with `<ActionForm>` (passing `encType="multipart/form-data"` on upload). Dropped `actionError` searchParam reading + page-level `<ActionAlert>` banner + its function definition. `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/actions.ts` — same shape change for create/setRole/grant/revoke/reset; success messages match brief spec (`"User created: <email>"`, `"Role updated"`, `"Granted <tool>"`, etc.). `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/page.tsx` — wrapped all five OperationCard forms; dropped `?action_error`/`?action_success` searchParam handling + page-level banners + `PageProps`/`firstParam` (no longer needed). PASSWORD_MATCH_SCRIPT rewritten to use event delegation on `document` instead of binding to element references at parse time — necessary because `<ActionForm>` remounts the form on success and the old script would have stale references. `CLAUDE.md` — new "Server actions: useActionState + router.refresh() pattern (Brief 19)" subsection under "Working with apps/web", inserted before "Service bindings (Brief 17)". Documents the rationale, four-step pattern (signature/body/wrapper/drop searchParam), and the inline-script gotcha. **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (1) `<ActionForm>` is the only client island; no per-form bespoke wrappers. (2) Specific per-action success messages (parallel structure between damage and sysadmin) over a generic "Saved" — operator gets a distinguishable inline toast for each operation. (3) `resetOnSuccess` left at default true everywhere — the multipart upload, note textarea, transition forms, and document edit form all benefit from clearing on success; the reset password card was the closest call (security smell to keep plaintext passwords in DOM after success), and remounting clears them. (4) PASSWORD_MATCH_SCRIPT changed to event delegation rather than dropping `resetOnSuccess` on the reset password card — event delegation is the more durable fix and survives any future remount. (5) `revalidatePath()` retained on every success — without it, `router.refresh()` could serve stale cached HTML. (6) Brief 18's diagnostic logging stays in the damage actions; not Brief 19's job to remove. (7) The dcRole transition filter on the damage detail page stays dropped (Brief 18 condition); not restored here. (8) Sysadmin page signature simplified to `()` from `({ searchParams })` — dead `PageProps`/`firstParam` removed; damage detail keeps its searchParams for the `confirm_delete_id` feature. **Latent issues / forward flags:** (a) **performance-worker submission form not migrated** — out of scope per brief; uses redirect+searchParam-banner pattern from Brief 6; flag for follow-up if operator hits the same symptom there. (b) **Brief 18 cleanup brief grows to 4 items** — should now also remove `[damage-action]` diagnostic console.log calls. Cleanup recipe: confirm dcRole populates → restore transition filter → remove DcRoleDebugLine → remove logActionEntry/logActionResult. (c) **`router.refresh()` is a soft refresh** — server data re-fetches but client islands keep state across the boundary (PhotoLightbox open state, UserPicker selection survives). Intended behavior; no-op for Brief 19's surfaces but worth knowing for future actions where you want a hard reset. (d) **`data-pending` form attribute is exposed but unused** — future briefs can add `[data-pending="true"]` styling hooks (gray submit button, spinner, etc.); zero cost today. (e) **TS inference on the `ActionResult` union is clean** — `useActionState` resolves the generic params from the action signature, no explicit type annotations needed at call sites. Verified by `pnpm typecheck` 13/13 green. (f) **Login flow's redirect is intentional and unchanged** — that's a navigation by intent (move from /login to /admin/dashboard), not a post-action UX feedback redirect; pattern doesn't apply. **Will the visible-refresh problem actually be fixed?** Yes by data flow: action runs → revalidatePath invalidates Next's route cache → action returns ok result → ActionForm's useEffect fires router.refresh() → Next re-fetches the route's server-component data → server re-renders with the post-mutation state → the new HTML replaces the old in-place. Same end result as pricing admin's client-fetch+setState approach, just routed through Next's standard server-action machinery instead of a worker JSON API. No reliance on redirect-response propagation, which was the OpenNext-on-CF gap. **Validation:** `pnpm typecheck` 13/13 successful, 4.4s (12 cached, 1 cache miss for `@splash/web` after source changes invalidated turbo cache); `pnpm --filter @splash/web build` succeeded — Next 15.5.15 compiled in 4.3s, 12/12 static pages generated. **Bundle deltas:** `/admin/damage/[id]` 965 B → 1.33 kB / 106 kB → 106 kB (+365 B route, 0 First Load); `/admin/sysadmin` 1.93 kB → 2.27 kB / 107 kB → 107 kB (+340 B route, 0 First Load). First Load JS unchanged because `useActionState`/`useRouter` were already in the vendor bundle (used by login form + existing client islands). All other route bundle sizes unchanged from Brief 18 snapshot. | Claude Code (Brief 19 session) |

updated the leading comment block. `apps/web/app/admin/damage/[id]/actions.ts` — added `logActionEntry(action, claimId, formData)` and `logActionResult(action, claimId, result)` diagnostic helpers (sanitized — long string values truncated to 80 chars, file values become `<File name size>` placeholders) and wired them into all five actions (transition, addNote, uploadDocument, editDocument, deleteDocument) so each click writes one entry log + one outcome log to splash-web Worker logs prefixed `[damage-action]`. **Part B (sysadmin UserPicker):** **Files created:** `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/_components/UserPicker.tsx` — client component email typeahead mirroring `apps/web/app/admin/performance/_components/LocationPicker.tsx`'s contract: 250 ms debounced fetch to `/sysadmin/api/users?q=...`, hidden `<input>` carries the selected user_id (form serializes it; empty/whitespace query returns `[]`; PostgREST wildcards/separators (`%`, `_`, `,`, `(`, `)`, `*`) are stripped from the needle before substitution. `apps/sysadmin-worker/src/index.ts` — split `OWNED_API_PATHS` into `OWNED_POST_PATHS` + `OWNED_GET_PATHS`, added GET branch with `isOriginAllowed` carve-out (per Brief 11b convention), new `handleSearchUsers` returns `[]` for empty `q` and the searchUsersByEmail result otherwise, super_admin gate at the top of `fetch()` covers all paths; **closed two latent issues flagged in Brief 7's outcome**: `handleSetRole` now rejects 400 "location_code is required when role is location_admin" instead of silently inserting a misconfigured row, and `handleCreateUser` reads + forwards `body.location_code` to `createUserPermissionsRow` when role is location_admin (was hardcoded null until now, requiring the legacy two-step Create + Set role workflow to attach a location). `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/page.tsx` — replaced the four `<input name="user_id" type="text">` text inputs (Set role / Grant tool / Revoke tool / Reset password) with `<UserPicker name="user_id" inputId="..." required />`; added a `Location code` field to the Create user card (helper: "Required only for location_admin role") to feed the worker's new accept-on-create path; tightened Set role's helper text to mention the new worker-side rejection; removed the now-vestigial `userIdHelper` constant. `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/actions.ts` — `CreateUserBody` gains optional `location_code`; `createUserAction` reads + forwards `location_code` only when role is `location_admin` (mirrors `setRoleAction`'s inclusion logic). **Files NOT modified:** `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/_lib/worker-fetch.ts` — `sysadminGetJson` already exists from Brief 17; UserPicker is a client component using browser `fetch("/sysadmin/api/users?q=...")` (relative URL, same posture as LocationPicker — same-origin in prod, dev relies on the `/sysadmin/api/:path*` rewrite in `next.config.mjs`). `apps/damage-worker/src/index.ts` — Part A diagnosis came back pointing at the apps/web filter, not the worker. **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (1) **Diagnostic logging stays in until follow-up:** the `[damage-action]` console.log calls are intentionally not removed; per the brief's §A.3 they're allowed to stay in prod-staging since the operator owns the test data, and removing them is a follow-up brief once the action chain is verified working end-to-end. The DcRoleDebugLine on the detail page should be removed in the same follow-up alongside the dcRole filter restoration. (2) **dcRole filter dropped (path A.4(i) from the brief):** preserving the filter "for security" is the wrong call — worker re-validates dc_role on every POST already (defense in depth; see `apps/damage-worker/src/index.ts:478` `if (!isOriginAllowed(request)) return jsonError(403, "bad origin");` followed by `loadAndScopeCheck` and the explicit `allowedRoles.includes(session.dcRole)` check in `findTransition`). The UI filter was a UX hint, not an access control. With dcRole-null the hint hides every action; dropping it restores usability while the population issue is investigated. (3) **UserPicker query escaping:** strip `%_,()*` from the needle so a malicious or pasted email with PostgREST wildcards can't widen the ilike pattern; `+` is preserved so Gmail aliases like `josh+test@…` still match. The actual ilike call is parameterized through supabase-js so no SQL injection risk; the strip is purely a "don't accidentally widen" hygiene step. (4) **`isOriginAllowed` carve-out for the new GET endpoint:** GETs are not state-changing; browsers omit Origin on same-origin GETs; matches the Brief 11b convention codified after dashboard-worker `/api/me` had the same anti-pattern. (5) **Inline location_code on Create user (vs. requiring two-step Create-then-Set-role):** the brief explicitly listed it as a small ride-along (`B.5`); honored. The form's helper text indicates it's only honored for location_admin, mirroring the worker's gate. (6) **`searchUsersByEmail` source = `auth_unified` (option A from the brief):** consistent with `getAuthContext`'s call site and includes role + tools without an extra join. The picker dropdown's per-row chip ("super_admin · tools: claims, pricing") makes the choice unambiguous when multiple users share an email prefix. (7) **Server-side `sysadminGetJson` left untouched:** the picker calls the worker from the browser, not the apps/web Worker, so the service-binding helper isn't exercised on this path. Leaving the existing helper in place is correct (it's the right shape for any future SSR caller; e.g., a row-level edit page that needs to pre-resolve a user). **Latent issues / forward flags:** (a) **dcRole-null mystery still unresolved** — Brief 11b deferred to operator action; Brief 18's filter removal + debug line is a workaround so the damage manager is usable while the source-of-null investigation continues. The cleanup brief should: (i) confirm dcRole populates for the test user, (ii) restore the filter in `[id]/page.tsx`, (iii) remove the `DcRoleDebugLine`, (iv) remove the `[damage-action]` console.log calls in `actions.ts`. (b) **handleCreateUser idempotency** unchanged — re-submitting the same email surfaces Supabase's 422 via the action-error banner. Acceptable v1; legacy parity. (c) **searchUsersByEmail worker-side limit is 20**, mirroring performance-worker's `/api/locations`. The picker doesn't expose a "show more" affordance; if the operator needs deeper search they refine the query. (d) **Service binding GET dev-fallback unused on this brief** — `sysadminGetJson` is still in the helper for future SSR callers, but the picker's browser-side `fetch` doesn't go through it. Production hits the same-origin `/sysadmin/api/users` path; dev hits the same path proxied via `next.config.mjs` rewrite to `NEXT_PUBLIC_SYSADMIN_WORKER_URL`. (e) **Diagnostic console.log volume** — every transition click writes two log lines to splash-web's Worker logs. CF Workers logs are bounded; a follow-up brief should remove these once the action chain is verified. (f) **DcRoleDebugLine has no role-gate** — every authed user sees it on the detail page. Acceptable for a diagnostic; remove in the follow-up brief alongside the filter restoration. **Validation:** `pnpm typecheck` 13/13 successful, 7.426s (all packages ran fresh — turbo cache invalidated by source changes); `pnpm --filter @splash/web build` succeeded — Next 15.5.15 compiled in 4.4s, 12/12 static pages generated, `/admin/sysadmin` route is `ƒ` at **1.93 kB / 107 kB First Load JS** (up from 161 B / 105 kB — the UserPicker client island adds ~1.7 kB to the route chunk + ~2 kB to First Load, in line with LocationPicker's budget on `/admin/performance`); `/admin/damage/[id]` route still `ƒ` at **965 B / 106 kB** — no client-side regression from Part A (filter removal + debug line are server-only; diagnostic logging is server-only). All other route bundle sizes unchanged from Brief 17 snapshot. | Claude Code (Brief 18 session) |
| 2026-05-04 | **Brief 17 completed:** service bindings for apps/web → sibling worker subrequests. **Files modified:** `apps/web/wrangler.toml` (added 5 `[[services]]` blocks above `[assets]` — DASHBOARD_WORKER → splash-dashboard, SIGNUP_WORKER → splash-signup-next, PERFORMANCE_WORKER → splash-performance, SYSADMIN_WORKER → splash-sysadmin, DAMAGE_WORKER → splash-damage); `apps/web/app/_lib/me.ts` (`getMe()` tries `env.DASHBOARD_WORKER.fetch(req)` first, falls back to URL-based fetch on `getCloudflareContext()` throw or undefined binding); `apps/web/app/admin/damage/_lib/worker-fetch.ts` (extracted `damageGetResponse` + `damagePost` internal dispatchers — both prefer service binding, fall back to URL fetch; `damageGetJson`/`damageGetJsonOrStatus`/`damagePostForm`/`damagePostMultipart` reshape onto these); `apps/web/app/admin/pricing/_lib/worker-fetch.ts` (`workerGetJson` inline-pattern for SIGNUP_WORKER, fallback in catch and undefined-binding branches); `apps/web/app/admin/performance/_lib/worker-fetch.ts` (`performanceGetJson` + `performancePostJson` for PERFORMANCE_WORKER); `apps/web/app/admin/sysadmin/_lib/worker-fetch.ts` (`sysadminGetJson` + `sysadminPostJson` for SYSADMIN_WORKER). **File created:** `apps/web/cloudflare-env.d.ts` — declaration-merges the 5 worker bindings into the global `CloudflareEnv` interface declared by @opennextjs/cloudflare. Uses `/// <reference types="@cloudflare/workers-types" />` to make `Fetcher` resolvable. ASSETS not redeclared (opennext's own .d.ts already declares it). `CLAUDE.md` gained a "Service bindings (Brief 17)" subsection under "Working with apps/web" — documents the binding-with-URL-fallback pattern + lists the 5 bindings + explains *why* (CF same-zone Worker-to-Worker URL fetches loop and 522). **Decisions made on operator's behalf:** (1) **`https://internal` placeholder host** consistently across all 5 helpers — service bindings ignore the URL host so the literal value doesn't matter, but pinning it prevents debugging confusion if a request leaks. (2) **`damagePhotoUrl` and `damageCheckRequestUrl` stay URL-based** — both return absolute URLs the *browser* fetches (via `<img src>` and `<a href>`), not the apps/web Worker, so the same-zone subrequest concern doesn't apply. Comment block in `damage/_lib/worker-fetch.ts` calls this out so future maintainers don't migrate them by reflex. (3) **Try/catch around `getCloudflareContext({ async: true })` + check for undefined binding** — covers both `next dev` (where `getCloudflareContext` throws because Workers runtime isn't running) and any future runtime where the binding might be unbound. The catch branch + undefined-binding branch both fall through to the same URL-based fetch path so behavior is uniform. (4) **`Origin` header set to `new URL(url).origin`** in POST helpers across both binding and URL paths — under the binding, `url = "https://internal" + path`, so Origin becomes `https://internal`; the worker's `isOriginAllowed` check derives an allow-list from `ALLOWED_ORIGINS` and accepts cross-binding traffic the same way it accepts the URL-based path's Origin (no special-casing needed; the worker's logic is `targetOrigin matches one of allowed_origins`). (5) **Type declaration approach = single `cloudflare-env.d.ts` at apps/web root**, declaration-merging `CloudflareEnv` rather than redeclaring it — opennext's own `cloudflare-context.d.ts` already declares `ASSETS?: Fetcher; IMAGES?: ImagesBinding; ...` in `CloudflareEnv`, so we extend instead of fight upstream. `**/*.ts` in apps/web/tsconfig.json's include picks the new file up automatically. (6) **No changes to `next.config.mjs` rewrites** — they stay as the dev-mode fallback when service bindings aren't available (per brief Out-of-scope). (7) **Service name `splash-signup-next` (not `splash-signup`)** matches `apps/signup-worker/wrangler.toml`'s `name` field. **Forward flag for cutover (item 13):** when signup-worker renames to `splash-signup` at full production cutover, the SIGNUP_WORKER binding's `service = "splash-signup-next"` line must update too — added inline mention in the wrangler.toml comment block. **Latent issues / observations:** (a) `getCloudflareContext({ async: true })` is the right shape for OpenNext 1.19.5 (verified via `apps/web/node_modules/@opennextjs/cloudflare/dist/api/cloudflare-context.d.ts:56-58`); the async overload returns `Promise<CloudflareContext>`, the sync overload throws if not initialized. We use async and rely on the throw → URL-fallback path for dev. (b) **`Fetcher` type resolution.** apps/web's tsconfig (via `@splash/config/tsconfig.next.json`) does NOT include `@cloudflare/workers-types` in `types`, but the helpers don't reference `Fetcher` directly — they just call `.fetch(req)` on `env.DASHBOARD_WORKER` (etc.) which is typed via the merged interface. The merged interface declaration in `cloudflare-env.d.ts` does name `Fetcher`; the `/// <reference types="@cloudflare/workers-types" />` directive at the top pulls workers-types globally for that file (and consequently the project, since it's in the include set). With `skipLibCheck: true`, the heavy workers-types globals don't conflict with DOM lib at lib-check time. Verified clean: `pnpm typecheck` 13/13 green. (c) **POST helpers' Origin under service binding.** I derive Origin from `new URL("https://internal" + path).origin` → `"https://internal"`. The damage-worker / sysadmin-worker / performance-worker `isOriginAllowed` reads `ALLOWED_ORIGINS` from each worker's wrangler.toml (or vars), comparing against the request's Origin/Referer header. None of the worker `ALLOWED_ORIGINS` values include `https://internal`, so under the binding the check would fail. **However:** Cloudflare Workers' service bindings are documented to bypass the edge entirely; `isOriginAllowed`-style checks are a CSRF mitigation against cross-origin browser POSTs and are not technically meaningful for Worker-to-Worker subrequests where there is no malicious script to forge the call. The operator can either (a) leave the helper sending `https://internal` Origin and add it to each worker's allow-list, or (b) drop the Origin header under the binding path and adjust each worker to skip the check when the request comes via service binding (e.g., via a custom header). **Pre-emptive recommendation:** option (a) — explicitly add `https://internal` to each worker's `ALLOWED_ORIGINS` env var at next deploy, since it's small surface and keeps the check on. Flagged for the cutover/staging deploy step; not blocking this brief because the brief explicitly says "Don't deploy from headless mode" and binding-vs-URL behavior is asymmetric enough that the operator should decide. (d) **TS inference with Fetcher type** — clean, no issues. The merged interface declares each binding as `Fetcher` (non-optional); call sites use `if (env.DASHBOARD_WORKER) { ... }` to narrow against undefined-at-runtime (in case the deployment is ever missing the binding), which TS happily allows on a non-optional `Fetcher`. (e) **Dev-mode fallback verification** — the catch branch around `getCloudflareContext` covers `next dev`. Logically clean, but couldn't smoke-test in next dev within this session because the brief is headless and `pnpm dev` would need an interactive terminal. Operator can verify by running `pnpm --filter @splash/web dev` and exercising one of the admin pages — the helper should log nothing unusual and the URL-based fallback should fire (with the existing `NEXT_PUBLIC_*_WORKER_URL` env vars or same-origin proxies, same as before). (f) **No regression on bundle sizes** — every route's First Load JS is identical to the Brief 16 snapshot (e.g., `/admin/damage/[id]` still 965 B / 106 kB). Service-binding code is server-only. (g) **Photo / check-request URLs in damage helper** stay URL-based per brief §scope.4 and explicit comment in code. **Latent issues spotted in the existing helpers (out-of-scope but flagged):** (i) `damageGetResponse` and `damagePost` extracted in this brief duplicate the URL/binding fork; a future cleanup could hoist a generic `workerSubrequest` into `apps/web/app/_lib/worker-subrequest.ts` parameterized on binding name + helper. Five copies of the same try/catch/fall-through pattern is duplication-by-design for now (each helper has its own URL env-var name + Cookie/Origin posture); collapse if the pattern grows further. (ii) When the binding is bound but throws at the worker layer (e.g., 5xx), the helpers will surface it without falling back to URL — correct (we don't want to hide real bugs behind a fallback) but worth knowing for future debugging. **Validation:** `pnpm typecheck` 13/13 successful, 9.055s (all packages re-ran fresh after TOML/.d.ts changes invalidated the turbo cache); `pnpm --filter @splash/web build` succeeded — Next 15.5.15 compiled in 4.2s, 12/12 static pages generated, all route bundle sizes unchanged from Brief 16's snapshot (`/admin/damage/[id]` 965 B / 106 kB; `/admin/performance` 1.85 kB / 107 kB; `/admin/sysadmin` 161 B / 105 kB; etc. — service bindings are server-only so zero client JS delta). | Claude Code (Brief 17 session) |

Two PS 5.1 quirks surfaced and fixed during testing: (1) `Set-Content -Value` with an array containing sub-arrays silently dropped trailing bytes — replaced with chronological append via `[System.IO.File]::AppendAllText` + UTF-8-no-BOM encoding (more predictable than `Add-Content -Encoding UTF8` against heredoc-created files lacking a BOM); (2) the Cowork sandbox's Linux mount caches metadata stale for files modified by Windows processes — `stat`/`ls`/`wc`/`cat` may report old sizes/contents, but the Anthropic `Read` tool and `dd` reading raw bytes bypass the cache and show Windows reality. **Future sessions:** when verifying daemon-written files, use Cowork's Read tool, not bash `cat`. | Cowork (orchestration session) |

Renamed; comment in the upload card explains why. (2) **Confirm-delete = simpler v1** per brief §scope.5 (no anti-replay token). The Delete tile-button is a `<Link>` to `?confirm_delete_id=N#docs`; the page resolves that to a `pendingDelete` photo (filtered by canMutateDocument so stale URLs after a delete or scope change collapse to null) and renders a confirmation banner with `<form action={deleteDocumentAction}>`. Reloading the confirm URL re-renders the banner; only the "Yes, delete" button click submits — same UX safety as a typical confirm-then-action flow without the token machinery. Code comment in `actions.ts` explains the v1 vs strict tradeoff. (3) **Lightbox is one client component per image tile**, not a single context-shared modal. Each `<PhotoLightbox>` instance manages its own `open` state; only the open one registers the Esc/scroll-lock effects. Cleaner than threading shared state through context, and the gallery markup stays a server component — children are passed in as `children` prop and rendered server-side. (4) **Edit affordance = native `<details>` element**, not a client modal. Server-only, no JS, browser-native disclosure widget. Cancel link is `<Link>` to the bare detail URL — closes the `<details>` via navigation. Drawback: opening the form scrolls to whatever was already focused, no fade animation; acceptable for v1. (5) **`isImagePhoto` extension fallback** — when `content_type` is null, falls back to `.{ext}` matching against jpg/jpeg/png/gif/webp/heic/heif rather than 5b's "treat null as image" heuristic. Customer-uploaded photos always have a content_type set by the worker, but Quote/Receipt rows imported from older paths might not. Safer to render the type chip than show a broken `<img>`. (6) **Per-Quote check-request links unconditional on photo_type === "Quote"** (brief §scope.8). The worker requires `quote.amount && quote.pay_to_type` (and `vendor_address` when vendor) before generating the PDF; if those aren't set, clicking returns the worker's 400 JSON in the new tab. Acceptable for v1; an upgrade would gate the link client-side on the same fields the worker checks. (7) **Activity rendering for delete/edit** — no change. The 5b ActivityBody already renders `document_added` rows verbatim from `notes`, and the worker's delete/edit handlers write `Deleted ...` / `Edited ...` prose into the same column. Legacy parity intentional — see ActivityType doc in `@splash/types/claims`. **Latent issues:** (a) the brief's `document_type` field-name spec is incorrect — flagging as a brief-text bug for the planner; the implementation uses `doc_type` to match the worker. (b) The lightbox's body-scroll lock uses `document.body.style.overflow` directly; if a parent layout sets its own overflow style this will stomp it on close. None observed in the current layout, but the pattern is brittle if the design system grows. (c) `damagePhotoUrl` is called once per photo and once per Quote (twice for Quote rows in the gallery render) — fine for typical claim volumes (single-digit photos), but if photos balloon the gallery becomes O(n) sequential awaits. Could be batched, not blocking. (d) The `<details>` edit form for Receipt rows still shows `pay_to_type` / `vendor_address` only when `photo.photo_type === "Quote"` — receipts skip those fields, matching the worker (which only honors them on Quote rows per `apps/damage-worker/src/index.ts:1246`). (e) In dev cross-origin mode, the upload's multipart body and Cookie header round-trip cleanly because the apps/web server-side fetch attaches both — but the `Origin` header is set by the helper to the worker's origin (not apps/web's), which is correct for the worker's `isOriginAllowed` check. Same pattern as `damagePostForm`; no new edge. **Validation:** `pnpm typecheck` 13/13 successful, 3.741s (12 cached + apps/web ran fresh; one TS error during development — `IMAGE_EXTENSIONS.has(m[1])` failed `noUncheckedIndexedAccess` strictness, fixed by extracting `m?.[1]` first); `pnpm --filter @splash/web build` succeeded — Next 15.5.15 compiled in 5.4s, 12/12 static pages generated, `/admin/damage/[id]` route is `ƒ` at **965 B / 106 kB First Load JS** (up from 5c's 171 B / 105 kB — the lightbox client island adds ~800 B to the route chunk and ~1 kB to First Load, well within budget for a useState/useEffect dialog component). | Claude Code (Brief 5d session) |

## Known production state

(As reported by operator. Do not modify without explicit instruction.)

- **`info-signup-worker` (legacy)** — owns customer-facing routes on `splashcarwashes.info`: `/api/*`, `/admin/*`, `/signup/*`, `/q/*`, `/join/*`. This is the only worker carrying real customer traffic today.
- **`splash-dashboard` (new)** — bound to `splashcarwashes.info/`, `/login`, `/logout` in Cloudflare. Returns 404s today because the new dashboard-worker code only handles `/api/login`, `/api/logout`, `/api/forced-reset` — apps/web is the intended owner of `/` and `/login`. Note: this binding is in Cloudflare's production state but is **not** reflected in the repo's [apps/dashboard-worker/wrangler.toml](apps/dashboard-worker/wrangler.toml), where `routes` is still commented.
- **`splash-signup-next`, `splash-performance`, `splash-sysadmin`, `splash-damage`** — workers.dev only, no production routes bound.
- **`splash-web`** — workers.dev only, no production routes bound.

## Staging

`staging.splashcarwashes.info` is a same-origin rehearsal environment
for end-to-end testing of the full apps/web + 5-worker stack. Brief 16
landed the wrangler.toml route bindings for staging across all 6
deployable artifacts (5 workers + apps/web); deploys + DNS are
operator-side action items per `STAGING_DEPLOY.md` at repo root.

- **Same-origin posture:** all 6 components share
  `staging.splashcarwashes.info`, so `sb-access-token` cookies set by
  dashboard-worker reach apps/web natively under SameSite=Lax. CSRF
  gates pass without carve-outs. Mirrors production behavior exactly.
- **Same-data posture (operator decision):** staging uses the same
  Supabase, R2, and D1 backends as production. No staging-isolation
  infrastructure. Operator uses identifiable test data and cleans up
  at their discretion. No separate staging secrets — every worker
  carries the same secret inventory whether served at workers.dev or
  staging.
- **Production routes untouched.** Staging is purely additive: new
  `routes = [...]` blocks coexist with the still-commented production
  blocks in every wrangler.toml. The legacy `info-signup-worker`
  continues to own production traffic on `splashcarwashes.info`
  regardless of staging state.
- **Rollback path:** comment out the staging `routes = [...]` blocks
  and re-deploy. CF removes the route bindings on the next deploy that
  omits them. Production state is unaffected.
- **Per-worker route patterns** are mirrored in the Deployed
  components table above. apps/web carries a catch-all
  (`staging.splashcarwashes.info/*`) and CF's most-specific-match-wins
  routing automatically routes per-worker patterns ahead of it.

## How this project is run

The work is split between two Claude surfaces:

- **Cowork** (planner) drafts briefs into `BRIEFS/`, maintains
  `BUILD_STATE.md`, and reviews outcomes after Claude Code executes.
- **Claude Code** (doer, runs in VS Code) reads `CLAUDE.md` +
  `BUILD_STATE.md` + a brief, makes the file edits, runs typecheck +
  build, fills in the brief's Outcome section, updates
  `BUILD_STATE.md`. Operates in headless mode via `claude --print`.

**Bridge:** `scripts/orchestrator.ps1` — a polling daemon the operator
launches once per work session. It watches `BRIEFS/QUEUE.md` and invokes
Claude Code headlessly when a brief is queued. On success the entry
moves to `BRIEFS/DONE.md`; on failure it moves to `BRIEFS/FAILED.md` and
the daemon halts. See `BRIEFS/README.md` for the full workflow + file
conventions, and `CLAUDE.md` at the repo root for the project rules
Claude Code auto-loads.

To start a work session:

```powershell
powershell -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File scripts/orchestrator.ps1
```

Stop with Ctrl+C. The daemon writes to `BRIEFS/orchestrator.log`.

(Use `pwsh` instead of `powershell` if you have PowerShell 7+ installed.
The operator's machine has only Windows PowerShell 5.1 by default, hence
`powershell` in the documented invocation.)

For one-off / manual brief execution without the daemon, open Claude
Code in the repo and paste:

```
Read CLAUDE.md, BUILD_STATE.md, and BRIEFS/<brief-filename>. Execute
the brief end-to-end. Fill in the Outcome section and update BUILD_STATE.md.
```

## Load-bearing constraints

These constraints are project-level. Treat as immutable; any brief that proposes touching them must be rejected without explicit operator override.

- **`/signup/{location}`, `/q/{location}`, `/join/{location}` URLs MUST NEVER change.** They are saved on hundreds of customer/admin device home screens as per-location bookmarks. Renaming them silently breaks the bookmark UX and is irreversible until devices are re-bookmarked manually. The signup-worker's `wrangler.toml` carries a warning comment immediately above the (commented) production-routes block; preserve it through any wrangler edits.
- **`pkg$` column name** in `pricing_simple`. See "Findings & decisions log" — kept as a quoted identifier; bracket-notation accesses (`row["pkg$"]`) are not to be normalized.
- **`SUPABASE_SERVICE_KEY` env-var name.** Legacy used `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY`; the monorepo standardized on `SUPABASE_SERVICE_KEY`. Code reads the new name across all five workers. A binding set under the legacy name in Cloudflare results in a 401 on every authenticated read.
- **`manually_flagged` rows in `suspicious_phones`** are immutable from worker code. Auto-detection skips writes when the row has `manually_flagged = true`. Admin-curated deny entries (`'0000000000'`, etc.) stay untouched.
- **CF same-zone Worker-to-Worker subrequests must use service bindings, not URL fetches.** When apps/web (running on splash-web at staging/prod) does a URL-based `fetch()` to a sibling worker on the same CF zone, the request loops through the edge inefficiently and 522s after ~19s. apps/web SSR helpers (under `apps/web/app/**/_lib/worker-fetch.ts` and `_lib/me.ts`) call sibling workers via `env.<BINDING>.fetch(req)` instead, with a URL-based fallback for dev. The 5 service bindings are declared in `apps/web/wrangler.toml` (DASHBOARD_WORKER, SIGNUP_WORKER, PERFORMANCE_WORKER, SYSADMIN_WORKER, DAMAGE_WORKER) and merged into the global `CloudflareEnv` interface in `apps/web/cloudflare-env.d.ts`. Adding new server-side worker calls without the binding pattern reintroduces the 522. This is a CF runtime limitation; not negotiable.

## Conventions

For Claude Code sessions:

- **Read this file first** before starting any task on this repo.
- **Update "Last updated"** + add an entry to "Findings & decisions log" at task completion. Use absolute dates (YYYY-MM-DD).
- **Don't modify "Known production state"** without explicit instruction from the operator.
- **If a brief's work invalidates this file's content** (e.g., a deploy happens, a route gets bound, an item changes status), flag it explicitly in your task report so this file gets updated alongside the change.
- **Treat the prioritized work list as authoritative** — derived from the audit + the work the operator has called out. Don't reorder or add briefs without surfacing it.
